# EX10 audit

## Status

The 2026-09-12 revalidation verifies **74/74 cards at 10/10 (740/740)** on
branch `ex10-full-reaudit`, based on `de4dda717d8c9e0c2420796cb387f68b1379b863`.
Three Luna lanes independently reviewed every card against the current catalog,
local rulings, executable IR, and existing behavioral and stack evidence. The
current per-card sections and closing gates supersede the historical reports.

The audit closes the Q5177/Q5179 batch DigiXros gap used by EX10-064 and adds
public cross-event once-per-turn proof for EX10-058. No catalog or production
card-module changes were needed. Structural inspection confirms 74 direct
modules, 74 focused suites, exactly one `registerIrCard` call per module, no
`registerCard` calls, and no `@ts-nocheck` anywhere in EX10. There was therefore
no suppression to remove. No retained, skipped, or todo EX10 tests remain.

### Historical status (2026-09-08)

All 74 EX10 cards were reported at 10/10 by the 2026-09-08 independent re-audit
(`docs/audits/EX10-REAUDIT-LEDGER.md`, commit `956a47288`), run from base
`46810fe1e271215d173854b82513f4ce30508b01` on branch `audit-ex10-reaudit`, which was merged
into main. Every row was re-scored from zero: catalog and rules, IR trace, behavioral proof
through public intents, peer or stack proof, and delivery gates. The re-audit rejected the
earlier 2026-09-05 revalidation, which had also claimed 74/74 at 10/10
(`docs/audits/EX10-AUDIT.md`): most of its suites proved clauses only through injected
timing, and eleven modules carried defects. That pass was the newest complete
per-card ledger at the time. It also fixed engine seams, corrected catalog text on six cards,
and left six retained reds marked `it.fails`, all listed under Open items.

### EX10-001 — Flickmon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-001.test.ts`: Inherited link-trash scope, controller turn, optional-rule replacement distinction, once-per-turn reset, hatch/evolution carrier.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Base: 46810fe1e271215d173854b82513f4ce30508b01. Worktree: audit-ex10-reaudit.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Digi-Egg, Green, level 2,
playCost -1, dp 0, forms [Appmon], attributes [System], types [Flick], rarity C,
maxCountInDeck 4. No main effect, no security effect, no evolution requirements.

Printed clauses — one, the inherited effect:

1. `[Your Turn]` — the payout only happens on the controller's own turn.
2. `[Once Per Turn]` — one payout per turn.
3. `When effects trash any of this Digimon's link cards` — an *effect* trash (not a
   rule trim), scoped to the carrier this egg sits under.
4. `gain 1 memory` — exactly +1 for the egg's owner.

No catalog discrepancy found: every field matches the official listing and the
printed text is reproduced verbatim in the module's `raw`.

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Question | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5006 | Does the inherited effect trigger when a card gets linked by an effect to an already linked Digimon whose link card is trashed and replaced? A: No. | `Q5006: gains no memory when a link replacement trashes the old link card` |

Q5006 resolves through the engine, not through card IR: linking past the link limit
lands both cards and the §17-1-3-2-5 rule-check sweep
(`GameEngine.trashExcessLinkCards`) trashes the excess with `byRule: true`, and the
trash seam suppresses `whenLinkTrashed` for a rule trim
(`apps/api/src/engine/linkState.test.ts`, "suppresses whenLinkTrashed for rule trim
but fires it for an effect trash"). The new EX10-001 test proves the suppression at
the card level rather than only at the engine level, and then proves the same host
still pays out for a genuine effect trash, so the assertion cannot pass on a dead
watcher.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| whole printed contract | `compiled` (exact `toEqual`) | `matches the catalog and carries the printed inherited contract` |
| effects trash a link card -> gain 1 memory | `SubTrigger` event `whenLinkTrashed` -> `GainMemory { amount: 1 }` | `gains 1 memory when an effect trashes this Digimon's link card, once per turn` |
| "any of THIS Digimon's link cards" | `sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true }` | `does not gain memory when another Digimon's link card is trashed` (own neighbour and opponent host both silent, own link still pays) |
| link card, not any card | `event: "whenLinkTrashed"` | `does not gain memory when a non-link card of the same carrier is trashed` |
| "effects trash" (not a rule trim) | engine trash-seam provenance | `Q5006: ...` |
| `[Your Turn]` | `trigger: "YourTurn"` -> `withSubTriggerTurnScope` stamps `turnScope: "yourTurn"` on the watcher (`interpreter/effect.ts`) | `pays only on your turn and resets on your next turn` (opponent-turn leg) |
| `[Once Per Turn]` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` -> `withSubTriggerFrequency` stamps `oncePerTurnKey` on the watcher | same test (same-turn refusal + next-own-turn reset) and the second trash in the first behavioural test |
| inherited only | `isInherited: true` | fixtures place EX10-001 in the carrier's `under` stack; a loose copy installs no watcher (`isHandTrashWatcherHost` returns false for inherited effects) |
| the egg reaches play by the legal Digi-Egg route | the watcher is installed on the carrying permanent when the stack enters the battle area | `hatches, digivolves in breeding, moves to the battle area, and pays out on that carrier` (`hatchEgg` -> `digivolve` -> `moveFromBreeding` -> `linkCard` -> `verb.trash`, all public) |
| the binding follows the permanent, not the card that was on top | `sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true }` resolved against the carrying permanent | `keeps the isSelfRef binding on the carrier permanent when the carrier digivolves again` |

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file scored the clause list only partially:

1. **No `[Your Turn]` proof.** Nothing exercised the opponent's turn, so a watcher
   that fired on both turns would have passed. Confirmed by mutation: changing the
   module's `trigger` from `YourTurn` to `AllTurns` now turns the turn-loop test red
   (it did not fail any committed test).
2. **No once-per-turn reset.** Same-turn refusal was asserted; the next-own-turn
   reset through the real turn loop was not.
3. **Q5006 was not covered at all.** The only listed ruling for this card had no
   test and no documented reason.
4. **A vacuous negative.** `does not gain memory when a non-link card is trashed`
   built a host with **no EX10-001 under it**, so the fixture had no inherited effect
   installed and the assertion was true by construction. It now carries the egg and a
   live link card, so it is a real negative.
5. **Non-public proof seam.** Every trash went through a private
   `(engine as unknown as { primitives }).primitives.trash(...)` reach-in. All trashes
   now go through the production verb `advance(s.engine).verb.trash(ids, byEffectSeat)`
   — the same seam production effects use — with the acting seat named, and the link
   replacement goes through the public `linkCard` intent.
6. **No catalog assertion.** Nothing pinned the printed fields or the exact
   inherited text; a catalog drift would have gone unnoticed.

#### Defects fixed locally

All in `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-001.test.ts` (test-only; the module IR was
already correct and is unchanged). Nine tests, up from four committed.

No retained red. No `it.fails` / `it.skip`.

#### Round 2: the Digi-Egg route and carrier movement

Both round-1 gaps are now closed by two new behavioural tests (nine tests in total, up
from seven). Nothing was removed.

**1. The real Digi-Egg route, through public intents and the production turn loop.**
`hatches, digivolves in breeding, moves to the battle area, and pays out on that carrier`
drives the whole route:

| Step | Intent | Asserted endpoint |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hatch | `hatchEgg` (turn 1, Breeding phase) | egg deck empties; `breeding.topCard.instanceId` is the seeded egg instance |
| digivolve | `digivolve` onto the breeding permanent (turn 1, Main phase) | breeding top is BT21-047, `breeding.stack` is exactly the egg instance, same `permanentId`, battle area still empty |
| move | `moveFromBreeding` (turn 3, Breeding phase) | `breeding` is `undefined`; the battle-area permanent carries the same `permanentId`, top BT21-047, stack exactly the egg instance |
| link | `linkCard` (turn 3, Main phase) | one linked card, memory down by exactly `linkCostOf(BT21-047, 0)` |
| payout | `advance(...).verb.trash([link], 0)` | memory +1, `linked` empty, the link card in trash, no pending decision |

Two of the controller's own turns are required, not one: §6-4-1 allows exactly one
breeding action per turn and both `hatchEgg` and `moveFromBreeding` are gated on the
Breeding phase (`validateHatchEgg` / `validateMoveFromBreeding` in
`apps/api/src/engine/actions/breeding.ts`). The turn loop parks in the Breeding phase
whenever a breeding action is legal, so the test settles on `Phase.Breeding` with
`settleAcrossTimers` and sends the breeding intent there, then `waitForMainPhase` for
the Main-phase work — no `s.state.phase` assignment anywhere.

Fixture choice: BT21-047 Navimon is Green Lv.3 with the [Appmon] trait, digivolves from a
Green Lv.2 for 0, prints only an `[On Play]` (which a digivolve never fires), and its own
`[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 1` header makes a second copy a legal link card for that
carrier — so the whole route needs no synthetic grant.

**2. Carrier movement.** `keeps the isSelfRef binding on the carrier permanent when the
carrier digivolves again` digivolves the carrier BT21-047 -> BT22-050 Roamon (Green Lv.4,
still [Appmon], so the §17-1-3-2-6 link-legality sweep leaves the link card alone) and
then asserts, on the SAME permanent id: the stack is `["EX10-001", "BT21-047"]`, the link
card is untouched, a neighbouring permanent's link trash pays nothing, and the carrier's
own link trash still pays +1. Mutation-confirmed: dropping `sourceFilter` from the module
turns this test red.

#### Remaining gaps

- **De-digivolve / bounce of the carrier is still untested.** Carrier movement is proved
  upward (digivolve) and across zones (breeding -> battle area). A carrier demoted by
  ＜De-Digivolve＞ or returned to hand is not exercised; the module has no clause that
  depends on it, so this is coverage breadth, not a suspected defect.
- **`[Once Per Turn]` on the `whenMovedFromBreeding` path.** Not applicable to this card
  (its watcher is `whenLinkTrashed`), noted only because the brief calls the general gate
  out.

3 tests including the behavioural turn-loop test.

### EX10-002 — Koromon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-002.test.ts`: Attack-target-switch draw, controller scope, unblocked negative, once-per-turn reset, egg-to-battle stack.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

#### Card summary

Catalog record (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`):

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Name | Koromon |
| Kind / level / form | Digi-Egg, Lv.2, In-Training |
| Color | Black |
| Type | [Lesser] |
| DP / play cost | 0 / -1 (Digi-Egg) |
| Rarity / max in deck | C / 4 |
| Main text | none |
| Security text | none |
| Inherited text | `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When attack targets change, ＜Draw 1＞` |

Printed clauses to prove:

1. The effect is inherited (it acts only from under a Digimon).
2. It triggers when an attack target changes.
3. It draws exactly 1 card for the controller of the Digimon carrying Koromon.
4. `[All Turns]` — it fires on either player's turn.
5. `[Once Per Turn]` — a second change in the same turn does nothing; the use resets on the
   next turn.
6. The clause is unscoped: the text says "attack targets change", not "this Digimon's attack
   target", so any target change arms it.

#### Rules and Q&A

- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-002` → "(no knowledge-base entries)". This matches
  `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/KB-INDEX.md`, which lists 0 ids for this card. Nothing to cover.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs rules "attack target"` → comprehensive §11-2-7 "Attack Targets":
  "After the attack declaration is made and an attack target is chosen, the attack target may
  switch". The production switch paths are ＜Blocker＞ and ＜Raid＞, plus effect redirects.
- Scope evidence from peer text rather than a ruling:
  - BT11-008 / BT11-010 print "When **this Digimon's** attack target is switched" and their
    modules carry `condition: { kind: "triggerAttackerIsSelf" }`.
  - BT17-056 prints "When **an** attack target is switched" and carries no such condition.
  EX10-002's wording is the unscoped form, so the absence of a self condition in its IR is the
  convention-consistent reading, not an omission.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-002.ts` (unchanged by this audit):

- one effect, `trigger: "AllTurns"`, `isInherited: true`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`;
- action `SubTrigger { event: "whenAttackTargetSwitched" }` → `Draw { controller: "mine", amount: 1 }`;
- `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []`.

Natural emitters of `whenAttackTargetSwitched` in production:

| Seam | Location |
| --- | --- |
| ＜Blocker＞ declared (`switchDefenderToBlocker`) | `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts:1200` |
| ＜Raid＞ redirect at declaration | `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts:722` |
| effect-driven redirect primitive | `apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:5731` |

The audit drives the ＜Blocker＞ seam through the public `attack` / `declareBlock` intents, so
no clause below rests on injected timing.

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | Test in `EX10-002.test.ts` | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, inherited-only text | "matches the catalog: a Black Lv.2 [Lesser] Digi-Egg…" | — |
| Trigger + draw 1 (positive) | "draws 1 when the opponent blocks this Digimon's attack" | `SubTrigger` → `Draw 1` |
| No target change → no draw (negative) | "does not draw when the attack resolves unblocked" | `SubTrigger` event gate |
| [Once Per Turn] same-turn refusal, unscoped watcher | "[Once Per Turn]: a second block in the same turn does not draw again" | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| [All Turns] + next-turn reset + controller is the host's | "[All Turns]: fires on the opponent's turn too, and the once-per-turn use resets" | `trigger: "AllTurns"`, `controller: "mine"` |
| Inherited from a real stack (breeding → Lv.3 → battle area) | "carries its inherited draw through a real breeding stack" | `isInherited: true` |
| IR shape | "records the inherited [All Turns] once-per-turn contract" | whole effect |

Evidence detail:

- The positive test asserts the drawn card's identity (`BT1-013`), the remaining deck order,
  that security was untouched (the attack was redirected onto the blocker), that the blocker
  was deleted, that `EX10-002` is still the host's only digivolution card, that no decision is
  pending and that combat has ended.
- The [All Turns] test runs the production turn loop (`startTurnLoop`,
  `waitForMainPhase(0)` → `endMainPhaseIfOpen(0)` → `waitForMainPhase(1)`). On the opponent's
  turn the opponent attacks and seat 0 blocks: the switch belongs to the opponent's attacker,
  the draw still goes to seat 0 and the opponent's hand is unchanged. That second draw is also
  the once-per-turn reset, since the first use was spent on the previous turn.
- The breeding test digivolves the egg to BT2-052 inside breeding (0 memory, the usual
  digivolve draw asserted separately), moves the permanent to the battle area, and only then
  measures the inherited draw. `Permanent.stack` is asserted to hold exactly `["EX10-002"]`.

  matches `effects.json`, so no sync is needed for this card.

Printed clauses:

2. `[Once Per Turn]` gate.

4. Processing condition ("by"): trash 3 **cards** with the `[Mineral]` or `[Rock]` trait from

Catalog agrees with the official text (single `inheritedEffectText`, no main or Security text,

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Question | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5007 | Can I trash just 2 digivolution cards for the condition? Answer: no; a "by" condition is unmet unless every required card is trashed. | Behavioural — "cannot pay with 2 matching sources and does not partially trash the stack": the stack keeps all 3 cards, trash holds only the checked security card, and the security check resolves. |
| Q5008 | What does "end the attack" mean? The timing transitions to end of attack; no counter or block timing occurs and the attack does not succeed. | Partly behavioural — "Q5008: ends the attack at declaration, so no block timing occurs" proves the block half (an eligible ＜Blocker＞ is on the board and no `blockWindowOpened` is emitted) and the "does not succeed" half (no `securityChecked`, security intact). The counter half is **not** proved; see Remaining gaps. |
| Q5009 | Can "end the attack" end an attack by a Digimon that isn't affected by effects? Answer: yes; it changes the timing, it does not affect the attacker. | Behavioural — "Q5009: ends the attack of an attacker unaffected by the opponent's Digimon effects". EX8-029 on the attacking seat with 3 memory grants itself the `beAffected` immunity; `observe(engine).hasRestriction(attacker, "beAffected", "Digimon")` is `true`, and the inherited effect still ends the attack (attacker suspended, security intact, no `securityChecked`, 3 cards trashed). |

| `[Opponent's Turn]` gate | every behavioural test runs with `turnSeat = 1` and the attack coming from seat 1 | `trigger: "OpponentsTurn"` |

| `[Mineral]` or `[Rock]` **trait**, exact | same test — BT1-010 (Reptile) is skipped, EX10-025 (Mineral), BT13-061 (Rock) and EX10-003 (Rock) are taken | `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Mineral", "Rock"], match: "trait" }]` |

#### Round 2 additions

1. **The real Digi-Egg route is now proved end to end.** New test: "hatches from the egg deck,
   grows in breeding, moves to the battle area and ends an attack there". It runs on the real
   turn loop and uses only public intents:
   - Turn 1 (seat 0): settle on `Phase.Breeding`, `hatchEgg` — the egg deck goes 1 -> 0, a
     `hatched` event is emitted, breeding holds EX10-003 with an empty stack. A
     `moveFromBreeding` in the same turn is then refused with `wrong-phase` (the single
     breeding action of the turn is spent, Comprehensive Rules 6-4-1).
   - Turns 1, 3 and 5: `digivolve` in the breeding area builds the stack
     EX10-003 [Rock] -> BT4-065 Gotsumon Lv.3 [Rock] -> BT10-062 Golemon Lv.4 [Mineral] ->
     BT10-064 Gogmamon Lv.5 [Rock] top. All four are fully inert (no effect and no inherited
     text), so no fixture effect can move the endpoints. The breeding window on turns 3 and 5
     is skipped with `endPhase`.
   - Turn 7: `moveFromBreeding` carries the stack to the battle area; the permanent keeps its
     `permanentId`, the top card is BT10-064 and `stack` is exactly
     `["EX10-003", "BT4-065", "BT10-062"]`.
   - Turn 8: the opponent attacks the player. The inherited clause fires on that carrier,
     trashes exactly those three cards (asserted by instance-id difference against the trash
     before the attack), leaves the top card in play, keeps security at its previous length,
     emits no `securityChecked`, and leaves no pending decision.

   Three matching cards under the top is the minimum the printed cost can be paid with, which
   is why the chain runs to Lv.5; each digivolve is a separate turn so the memory cost of one
   never ends the turn early.
2. **The last internal cast is gone.** Three tests read
   `(s.engine as unknown as { combat: { isAttacking: boolean } }).combat` to settle on the end
   of the attack. They now use the seam's `observe(s.engine).isAttacking()`. No test in this
   file reaches past `apps/api/src/engine/testkit/` any more.

#### Changes made

Only `EX10-003.test.ts`. The module `EX10-003.ts` is byte-identical to `HEAD`
(`git diff --stat HEAD` lists the test file only) and is correct as written.

Gaps found in the inherited suite and closed:

1. **No once-per-turn proof at all.** The suite had no same-turn refusal and no reset. Added
   "fires once per opponent's turn and resets on the next opponent's turn", driven through
   `startTurnLoop()` and `advance(...).waitForMainPhase` across three turns: pay on the first
   attack (stack 7 → 4), refuse on a second attack in the same turn (security 2 → 1, stack
   unchanged), pay again on the next opponent turn (stack 4 → 1).
2. **Q5008 was not covered.** Added the block-timing test with an eligible ＜Blocker＞
   (ST18-07) on the defending board; no `blockWindowOpened`, no `securityChecked`.
3. **Q5009 was not covered.** Added the scenario; it now passes behaviourally (see Retained red).

Temporarily deleting `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` from the module fails 2 of 8 tests (the IR
contract test and the new once-per-turn test), so the new test is sensitive to the gate rather

#### Retained red

None. The previously retained `it.fails` for Q5009 is now a passing `it`.

Root cause of the old red: the test file never imported the card index
(`import "../index.js";`), so the immunity-granting fixture card EX8-029 was unregistered and
its `[All Turns]` grant never installed. The engine seam named in the earlier report
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/grantStatic.ts`,
`immuneToOpponentDigimonEffects`) is correct as written: with the index imported,
`hasRestriction(attacker, "beAffected", "Digimon")` reads `true` after
`recomputeContinuousEffects()` and the whole scenario resolves as the ruling says.

Follow-on fixture change forced by the same import: with the index registered, EX10-025's
inherited "when effects trash this card from a [Mineral] or [Rock] trait Digimon's digivolution
cards, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with a play cost of 4 or less" now actually fires and
deleted the second attacker in the once-per-turn test. That test's cost pool was swapped to the
inert [Mineral]/[Rock] fixtures the brief names (BT10-062, BT10-064). The other tests keep
EX10-025 on purpose: they assert the trashed card ids, and the side deletion does not touch
their endpoints.

#### Remaining gaps

- **Q5008 counter-timing half is unproved, and the engine looks wrong here.** In
  `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts` (`CombatController.resolveAttack`),
  `runCounterWindow` is awaited *before* the `if (this.endRequested)` check, so an attack ended
  during the When Attacking timings — which is where `whenOpponentAttacks` fires — still
  transitions to counter timing. Q5008 says it must not. I could not turn this into a red test:
  no `counterWindowOpened` event is emitted in any fixture I built, because none of the
  battle-area `[Counter]` cards I placed (BT26-055, BT26-103, EX12-017, EX12-052, EX12-057,
  BT15-076, LM-001) is reported by `GameEngine.counterEligibleSources`, even on an attack that
  runs to a full security check. That is a second, independent seam and it is what blocks the
  proof; a false-green `it.fails` would have been worse than reporting it. Coordinator: the
  ordering fix and the counter-eligibility gap both need an engine lane.
- **The `[Opponent's Turn]` gate is not isolated from the trigger.** "One of your opponent's
  Digimon attacks" can only happen on the opponent's turn, so no fixture separates the two.
- **The counter half of Q5008 is still the only unproved printed consequence** (see above).
- **Peer coverage is narrow by nature.** The card has no name gate, no evolution route of its
  own and no Security text, so the peer axis is the trait pool and the host stack.

#### Changes made

Only `EX10-004.test.ts`. The module (`EX10-004.ts`) was already correct and is unchanged.

Defects found and fixed in the test:

1. **The once-per-turn assertion was vacuous.** The fixture had a single hand card, trashed by

   unrelated battle-area Digimon while a different Lucemon moves out of breeding.
4. **No opponent-side negative.** Added the opponent's own breeding move on the opponent's
   turn; nothing is granted.
5. **No Digi-Egg move negative.** Added the lone-Cupimon case, rejected `not-movable`.
6. **Q5010 was implied but not named.** The digivolution-card placement is now labelled with

(the IR contract test and the Lucemon: Chaos Mode behavioural test). The module was restored

#### Round 3: catalog search and the natural-origin once-per-turn proof

##### Catalog search for a second breeding move in one turn

Searched all 4388 catalog entries for printed text mentioning the breeding area together with
move / return / place (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`, main, inherited and
security text). 45 cards matched; the ones that move a Digimon out of breeding by EFFECT
rather than by the breeding-phase action are:

| Card | Text | Implemented |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EX10-013 Lucemon | `[Breeding] [When Digivolving] This Digimon may move.` | yes — `MovePermanent` `direction: "toBattle"` |
| P-130 Lui Ohwada | `[On Play] You may move 1 of your level 3 or higher Digimon from the breeding area to the battle area.` | yes |
| BT1-089 Mimi Tachikawa | `[Main]` suspend to move a level 3+ Digimon from breeding | module present |
| BT14-088 Gennai | `[Opponent's Turn]` … move 1 of your Digimon from breeding | module present |
| EX9-057 Abbadomon Core | `[Breeding] [Opponent's Turn]` … this Digimon moves to the battle area | module present |
| BT20-095 Fellowship of Hope's Keepers | ＜Delay＞ move a [Chronicle] Digimon from breeding | module present |

`primitives.ts movePermanentZone` fires `whenMovedFromBreeding` (and
`whenOpponentMovedFromBreeding`) for effect moves exactly as
`GameEngine.handleMoveFromBreeding` does for the player verb, and it is explicitly NOT gated on
`Phase.Breeding` or the one-action-per-turn limit. So a second move in the same turn IS publicly
reachable; the rubric-ceiling policy is not needed.

Refilling the emptied breeding area mid-turn also has printed support: P-123 Ukkomon and
BT16-082 Ukkomon both hatch on `whenMovedFromBreeding` (an effect hatch, no phase gate).

##### The proof

`refuses the second grant in the same turn: two Lucemon leave breeding through public intents`
drives only public intents:

1. `moveFromBreeding` (the breeding-phase action) moves EX10-013 Lucemon out. EX10-004, sitting
   under a battle-area host, pays the hand trash, draws and gains memory. P-123 Ukkomon's own
   watcher hatches BT1-006 Cupimon into the freed breeding slot. BT1-006 prints only a
   `[When Attacking]` inherited clause, so the new stack adds no competing EX10-004 source.
2. `digivolve` in the breeding area turns that Cupimon into a second EX10-013, whose
   `[Breeding] [When Digivolving]` move carries it to the battle area — the second
   `whenMovedFromBreeding` of the turn, from the same single EX10-004 source.
3. Endpoints: the second Lucemon is in the battle area and breeding is empty (the move really
   happened), the trash still holds exactly the one card paid the first time, the deck lost
   exactly one card (the digivolution draw, §6-2-2) and memory moved by exactly the
   digivolution cost of 5. No second trash, draw or memory.

`resets on the next own turn: a move on turn 1 and a move on turn 3 both grant` runs the real
turn loop: turn 1 uses the breeding-phase move verb, turn 2 is the opponent's, turn 3 hatches
(that turn's breeding action) and moves the fresh Lucemon by EX10-013's effect. Each firing
trashes exactly one hand card, so the trash count (1 after turn 1, still 1 after turn 2, 2 after
turn 3) is the reset signal that survives the loop's own draws.

##### Mutation sensitivity

Changing `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` to `"Unlimited"` in the module fails 3 of 13 tests,
including the new same-turn refusal. The module was restored from a scratchpad copy and is
byte-identical to `HEAD` (`git diff --stat apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-004.ts` is empty).

#### Remaining gaps

- **The `[Your Turn]` gate cannot be isolated.** Your own Digimon can only move from breeding
  on your own turn, so no fixture separates the turn gate from the `controller: "mine"` subject
  filter. The opponent-move test exercises both together.
- Observation for the coordinator, not a defect in this card: digivolving in the breeding area
  charges the full digivolution cost from memory (the same-turn test goes 8 -> 3 for EX10-013's
  cost of 5). That matches the printed cost; it is recorded only because the reset test can
  drive memory negative inside the turn loop.
- No engine seam is red. No `it.fails` / `it.skip` retained.

the modules it imported directly: every fixture card's effects were silently inert and the
behavioural proofs were weaker than they read. Added `import "../index.js";` next to the
module import. No fixture needed swapping — all assertions hold with the real registry
loaded, which makes the existing behavioural and peer/stack proofs genuine rather than
vacuous. Scores are unchanged (the proofs are the same proofs, now actually exercised).

### EX10-005 — Pagumon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-005.test.ts`: Opponent direct deck-trash trigger, own-turn gate, reveal negative, inherited lifecycle and reset.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`):

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Name | Pagumon |
| Kind / level | Digi-Egg, Lv.2, In-Training |
| Colors | Purple |
| Types | Lesser |
| Main / Security text | none |
| Inherited text | `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When your opponent's deck is trashed from, ＜Draw 1＞` |

No catalog discrepancy found. The card has exactly one printed clause.

Printed clause, split into the parts a test must pin:

1. Window: `[Your Turn]` only.
2. Frequency: `[Once Per Turn]`, resetting on your next own turn.
3. Trigger: the **opponent's** deck is trashed from (not your own).
4. Effect: draw 1 card.
5. It is an inherited effect, so it only works from under a Digimon.

#### Rules and Q&A

- `Q5011` (2025-09-05): "Does this card's inherited effect trigger if a card is revealed
  from the deck and trashed?" — **No.**
- Backing rule: comprehensive §15-15-3 "Effects That Reveal Cards": *even if cards revealed
  from a deck are trashed, they aren't treated as being trashed from the deck.*

#### Clause → test → IR

Module: `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-005.ts` (unchanged this audit). One inherited
`YourTurn` / `OncePerTurn` effect carrying a `SubTrigger` on `onDiscardLibrary` with
`sourceFilter: { controller: "opponent" }` and a `Draw 1` for `mine`.

| Clause | Test in `EX10-005.test.ts` | IR element |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog + IR shape | "matches the catalog and carries the printed inherited contract" | whole `compiled` |
| Draw 1 on an opponent deck trash | "draws 1 when a played effect trashes the opponent's deck on your turn" (BT14-077 `[On Play]`, public `playCard` intent) | `Draw`, `sourceFilter.controller` |
| Second real deck-trash route | "draws when a real deck-trash effect mills the opponent's deck" (EX10-009 `[On Deletion]` trashes their top 5) | `SubTrigger` consumer |
| `[Once Per Turn]` + reset | "draws only once a turn and resets on your next turn" (two BT14-077 plays in one turn, then the real turn loop to your next turn and a third play) | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Own deck excluded | "ignores a mill of your own deck" (BT2-068 `[On Deletion]` mills only your top 3) | `sourceFilter: { controller: "opponent" }` |
| `[Your Turn]` window | "does not draw on the opponent's turn" (opponent plays BT14-077 on their own turn, milling both decks) | `trigger: "YourTurn"` |
| Q5011 / §15-15-3 | "Q5011: does not draw when opponent deck cards are revealed and then trashed" (opponent's BT12-071 AncientWisemon reveals their top 3 on your turn and trashes the rest) | engine: `RevealAdd` never calls `fireOnDiscardLibrary` |
| Digi-Egg route: `isInherited` from a real hatched stack | "carries its inherited draw through the real Digi-Egg route: hatch -> digivolve -> battle area" (`hatchEgg` from the egg deck in the production Breeding window, `digivolve` BT2-067 onto the egg in breeding, `moveFromBreeding` on the next own turn, then BT14-077 mills the opponent's deck on that carrier) | `isInherited: true` |

Engine seams read to confirm the semantics:

- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:2947` `fireOnDiscardLibrary(deckSeat, ids)` —
  the payload's `addedToHand.byEffect.ownerSeat` carries the **milled deck's** seat, not the
  effect owner's seat.
- `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts:247` `discardLibraryGate` —
  compares that seat against the watcher's controller direction.
- Fire sites: `interpreter/actions/resources.ts:126` (`TrashTopDeck`),
  `interpreter/actions/removal.ts:581` (trash target with `zone: "deck"`),
  `interpreter/costs.ts:1109` (deck-trash cost).
- `interpreter/actions/reveal.ts:755` (`returnRevealed === "trash"`) does **not** fire it —
  this is what makes Q5011 correct in the engine today.

#### What changed

Only `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-005.test.ts`. The module needed no change.

The previous test proved three of its four cases with injected timing
(`advance(...).fireSubTrigger("onDiscardLibrary", …)` and hand-built payloads). Under the
re-audit rubric those earn no behavioural credit, and one of them was actively misleading:
the payload named `byEffect.ownerSeat` as if it were the effect owner, when the production
seam puts the milled deck's seat there. All injected cases were replaced with
natural-origin ones driven by public intents (`playCard`, `attack`), the production turn
loop, and the production deletion verb. `EX10-005.test.ts` no longer calls
`fireSubTrigger`/`fireTiming` at all.

Fixture correction after coordinator review: the EX10-009 case still carried the previous
test's seat 1 deck of `BT1-001..BT1-006`, which are Digi-Eggs and must never sit in a deck
fixture. It now uses inert main-deck Digimon (`BT1-009..BT1-014`). No assertion changed —
the case still asserts 5 cards trashed, 1 card left in the deck, and the exact drawn
instance in hand.

Added coverage that did not exist before: the catalog/IR contract case, the once-per-turn
**reset** across a real turn boundary, the own-deck negative, the `[Your Turn]` negative
proved by an opponent's own play on their turn, and Q5011.

#### Digi-Egg route (round 2)

Round 1 proved the clause only from pre-seeded `under:` stacks, so the Peer/stack column
scored 1. Round 2 adds the real Digi-Egg lifecycle, driven end to end by public intents on
the production turn loop:

1. `startTurnLoop()`; the loop parks in `Phase.Breeding` because seat 0's egg deck holds
   EX10-005 (asserted: breeding empty, egg deck length 1).
2. `applyIntent(0, { type: "hatchEgg" })` — breeding top card becomes EX10-005, egg deck
   empties. The egg's instance id is captured here and asserted by identity from then on.
3. `applyIntent(0, { type: "digivolve", permanentId: <breeding>, instanceId: BT2-067 })` —
   a Purple Lv.3 DemiDevimon (no printed text) digivolves onto the egg in the breeding area
   for 0 memory and draws 1. Asserted: breeding stack is exactly the egg instance, hand is
   exactly `[skullsatamon, evoDraw]`, battle area still empty.
4. `endPhase` for both seats through `advance().endMainPhaseIfOpen`, one full opponent turn,
   then settle on `Phase.Breeding` for seat 0 again.
5. `applyIntent(0, { type: "moveFromBreeding", permanentId: <breeding> })` — breeding is now
   undefined, battle area holds one permanent whose top card is BT2-067 and whose
   `stack` is exactly the egg instance.
6. `applyIntent(0, { type: "playCard", instanceId: BT14-077 })` in that turn's Main phase —
   the `[On Play]` mills the top 2 of both decks and the inherited clause fires **on the
   hatched carrier**.

Endpoints asserted after settling: seat 0's hand is exactly
`[evoDraw, turnDraw, drawn]` by instance id (the third is the Pagumon draw), seat 0's trash
is exactly `[milled1, milled2]` by instance id, seat 0's deck is `["BT1-014"]`, seat 1's
deck is down to 2, the carrier's stack is still the egg instance, and `pendingDecision` is
undefined. The turn loop is closed with `surrender` and awaited.

No `advance().fire*` call appears anywhere in this case, and no `s.state.phase` is written
by hand — the Breeding windows are the production ones.

#### Defects found

None in the module. The implementation matches the printed text and Q5011.

The defect was in the evidence: the previous test's synthetic `onDiscardLibrary` payloads
described a seat the engine does not put there, so the passing "only reacts to the opponent

### EX10-006 — Agumon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-006.test.ts`: Virus plus Greymon-text conjunction, optional trash return, decline/no-target, ordinary evolution, inherited DP and expiry.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Worker lane, session 1 (2026-09-08). Base 46810fe1e. Files touched:
`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-006.ts`, `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-006.test.ts`,
this report and `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-006-*.log`.

#### Card summary

| Field | Catalog value |
| --- | --- |
| Name | Agumon |
| Colors | Red, Black |
| Kind / Level / Form | Digimon / 3 / Rookie |
| Play cost / DP | 3 / 1000 |
| Digivolution costs | Red Lv.2 for 1, Black Lv.2 for 1 |
| Attribute / Type | Virus / Reptile |
| Rarity / Max in deck | U / 4 |

Printed clauses:

- **C1** `[Digivolve] [Koromon]: Cost 0`
- **C2** `[Start of Your Main Phase] You may return 1 [Virus] trait Digimon card with
  [Greymon] in its name from your trash to the hand.`
- **C3** (inherited) `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP.`

No Security effect, no ACE, no DigiXros, no once-per-turn limit.

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-006` returns **no knowledge-base entries** — no Q&A,
no errata, no restriction. This matches KB-INDEX.md (EX10-006: 0 Q&A ids). Nothing to
cover, nothing skipped.

Two comprehensive/manual passages govern the wording and are cited in the tests:

- `data/kb/rules/manual.md` §1 "Specifying Names" uses **this card's own clause** as its
  worked example: *"Digivolve [Koromon]: Cost 0 — When a name is specified only using
  brackets, it means that only cards with matching names are specified."*
- The same section: *"When an effect specifies 'with [XX] in its name,' cards whose names
  include the text in brackets can be referenced."* — so C2's `[Greymon]` half is a
  substring match while C1's `[Koromon]` is exact.

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | IR | Behavioural test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 exact-name gate | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ namesExact: ["Koromon"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` | "digivolves in breeding from the printed black Koromon / an off-colour green Koromon for 0 memory" (two cases), "refuses the 0-cost route from an on-colour egg that is not Koromon / an off-colour egg that is not Koromon" (two cases) |
| C1 does not replace the printed cost | same | "still pays the ordinary level 2 cost of 1 when no alternate route is claimed" |
| C2 candidate filter | `Return` from `zone: "trash"`, `controller: "mine"`, `kind: ["Digimon"]`, `traits: ["Virus"]`, `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Greymon"], match: "name" }]`, `count: 1` | "returns the one [Virus] [Greymon] card at the natural start of my main phase" (three same-trash decoys plus the opponent's trash), "accepts MetalGreymon because [Greymon] in its name is a substring match", "opens no decision when the trash holds no legal candidate" |
| C2 optionality | `optional: true` | "leaves the matching card in the trash when the 'may' is declined" |
| C2 timing and turn ownership | `trigger: "StartOfYourMainPhase"` | "stays silent at the start of the opponent's main phase" |
| C3 inherited DP | `ModifyDP` +1000, `duration: "permanent"`, `isSelfRef`/`isSelf` | "grants +1000 DP the moment it becomes a digivolution card, and none to itself on top", "does not raise a vanilla host that carries no EX10-006 under it", "keeps the +1000 DP across a turn boundary" |
| Catalog fidelity | — | "matches the catalog and carries both printed clauses as IR" |

All C1/C2/C3 proof runs through public intents (`digivolve`) or the real turn loop
(`startTurnLoop()` + `advance().waitForMainPhase(seat)`). **No injected timing remains** —
the previous suite proved C2 only through `advance.fire(EffectTiming.OnStartMainPhase)`,
which earns no behavioural credit.

#### Defects found and fixed

1. **C1 used a substring name gate.** `digivolutionRequirement` carried
   `names: ["Koromon"]`. `DigivolutionRequirement.names` is documented as a SUBSTRING
   match (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/requirements/digivolve.ts:14`); the printed
   `[Koromon]` is a bracketed exact name, and the rule manual quotes this very clause as
   its example of exact matching. Changed to `namesExact: ["Koromon"]`.
   No released card has "Koromon" as a proper substring of its name (checked against the
   whole catalog: 16 cards contain the token, all named exactly "Koromon"), so the fix is
   fidelity, not a behaviour change today; a future "…Koromon" print would have been
   wrongly accepted.

2. **Illegal fixture in the previous suite.** The old C1 test placed EX10-002 (a Lv.2
   Digi-Egg) in `battleArea`. Digi-Eggs only ever sit in the breeding area. Every C1 case
   is now a breeding-area digivolution with the source-card identity, the stack contents,
   the memory paid and the bonus draw asserted.

3. **No negative source, no ordinary-route case.** The previous suite asserted
   `{ok: true}` on one route and nothing else. Added an on-colour non-Koromon egg
   (BT13-005 Dorimon) and an off-colour non-Koromon egg (BT1-005 Kyaromon) as refusals,
   plus the ordinary Lv.2 route at cost 1 to show the alternate is a real reduction. The
   green Koromon (BT7-004) case proves the alternate is name-driven, not colour-driven —
   no ordinary route reaches a green source.

4. **No turn-boundary proof for C3.** Added a case that carries the +1000 DP across the
   end of my own turn into the opponent's main phase, which is what separates `permanent`
   from `forTheTurn` in the printed `[All Turns]` text.

#### Mutation evidence (fails when reverted)

Each mutation was applied to `EX10-006.ts`, the suite run, and the file restored.

| Mutation | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Fold `traits: ["Virus"]` back into a second `nameOrTrait` entry (a UNION, the pre-existing bug the committed comment describes) | 3 failures: the IR test plus **two behavioural tests** — the decoy test and the no-candidate test |
| `duration: "permanent"` → `"forTheTurn"` | 1 failure, the IR test only — see gap G1 |

#### Remaining gaps

- **G1 — `duration` is inert on a continuous inherited `ModifyDP`.** Setting the C3
  duration to `forTheTurn` does not change any observable DP, even across a turn end;
  only the IR assertion catches it. The continuous-effect recompute
  (`GameEngine.recomputeContinuousEffects`) re-derives inherited `AllTurns` modifiers each
  pass, so their `duration` never gates anything. Not a defect of this card, but it means
  no card of this shape can earn behavioural credit for its duration field. Coordinator:
  worth one engine-seam note — either honour the field or reject a non-`permanent`
  duration on a continuous inherited modifier at compile time.
- **G2 — `kind: ["Digimon"]` in C2 is not behaviourally provable.** The clause says
  "Digimon card"; the catalog contains no Tamer or Option card with "Greymon" in its name,
  so no fixture can distinguish the kind gate from the name gate. Asserted structurally
  only.
- **G3 — no [Virus] Greymon-named card is free of printed text.** Every candidate used
  (BT10-019, BT1-114) has effects, but all of them are trash-inert, so the fixtures cannot
  open a decision or alter the result.

#### Catalog discrepancy (reported, not edited)

`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` stores EX10-006's `effectText` with a
duplicated phrase:

```
...1 [Virus] trait Digimon card with [Greymon] in its name in its name from your trash...
```

The official text has "in its name" once. The IR is unaffected (the compiled filter is
correct); the stored string is wrong. The catalog assertion in the test pins the current
string so the drift is visible when it is corrected upstream. Coordinator: this belongs in
REVIEW-NOTES.md "Catalog corrections".

#### Commands and results

```bash
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-006.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000   # 1 file, 14 tests, all passed
pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck          # no EX10-006 diagnostic
pnpm exec oxlint  apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-006.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-006.test.ts   # clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>    # correct format
git diff --check                            # clean
```

Typecheck fix (coordinator follow-up): `s.give` takes the `Zone` enum, not a string
literal. `s.give(0, "trash", …)` raised TS2345 against `CardZone`
(`apps/api/src/engine/state/access.ts:52`, a union of `Zone` members). Changed to
`s.give(0, Zone.Trash, …)` with `Zone` imported from `@aegis/shared`. The assertion in
"stays silent at the start of the opponent's main phase" is unchanged.

Logs: `logs/EX10-006-baseline.log` (the committed suite: 5 tests passing before any
change), `logs/EX10-006-vitest.log`, `logs/EX10-006-typecheck.log`,
`logs/EX10-006-checks.log`.

| Q5013 | Buffing the opponent's EX10-010 [BlackWarGreymon ACE] to 15000 arms both ACEs' [All Turns] clauses; both settle at 15000 and stay there after this card's buff expires. | `Q5013: settles both BlackWarGreymon ACEs at 15000 DP and keeps them there after expiry` |

Both listed ids are now covered by behavioural tests. The previous test file covered neither
id behaviourally: Q5012 was proved only through injected timing, and Q5013 was absent.

Negative: `refuses the Agumon alternate route from a level 3 source without [Agumon] in its name` (BT1-009 Monodramon) at 5 memory, so the refusal is the name gate rather than cost. |

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file scored no behavioural credit for the card's main clause:

1. **[On Play] was proved only by injected timing.** It called
   `advance(s.engine).fire(EffectTiming.OnPlay, s.perm("greymon"))` on a pre-seeded board
   permanent. Per the evidence standard that earns 0 in the Behavior column. Replaced with a
   real `playCard` intent from hand, with memory, hand size and battle-area membership asserted.
2. **The duration was proved by hand-driven ledger sweeps.** It set `s.state.turnSeat = 1` and
   called `advance(s.engine).ledgers.continuous.sweep(...)` / `.modifiers.sweep(...)` directly.
   That asserts the ledger API, not that the real turn loop expires the modifier. Replaced with
   a full turn loop, including the intermediate assertion that the buff survives the opponent's
   Main phase.
3. **Q5013 was not covered at all**, despite being listed in KB-INDEX.md.
4. **The substring name gate was untested.** The only digivolve case used EX10-006, whose name
   is exactly "Agumon", so swapping `names` for `namesExact` would not have failed anything.
   Added a ToyAgumon (BT7-007) case that only passes under a substring match.
5. **No illegal-source negative** for the alternate route. Added.
6. **The alternate route was never selected explicitly.** The old case omitted
   `useAlternateCost`. Added, alongside the memory-2 assertion.
7. **`toMatchObject` masked an added controller gate.** The shape assertion accepted extra keys
   on the target filter, so a regression re-adding `controller` (contradicting Q5012) would have
   passed. Tightened to `toEqual` on the actions array.
8. **No catalog field assertion.** Added `getCardDefinition("EX10-007")` field match.

#### Defects found in the module

None. The compiled IR of `EX10-007.ts` is correct for every printed clause and both rulings,
and the file is unmodified by this lane. Mutation checks confirm the new tests bite:

| Mutation to `EX10-007.ts` | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `amount: 3000` -> `5000` | 6 of 9 tests fail |
| filter gains `controller: "opponent"` | 4 of 9 fail (the own-Digimon, duration and both digivolve cases) |
| filter gains `controller: "self"` | shape test fails; **no behavioural test fails** — see the engine note below |

#### Engine seam for the coordinator

`controller: "self"` on a `ModifyDP` target filter is silently a no-op. Adding it to this card's
`OnPlay`/`WhenDigivolving` target changed no behaviour: the opponent's Digimon remained a legal
target and the Q5012 case still passed. `controller: "opponent"` on the same filter *does*
restrict correctly. So the two spellings of the field are asymmetric, and a card that means "one
of your Digimon" and writes `controller: "self"` gets no gate at all.

- Seam: the `ModifyDP` target resolution path under `apps/api/src/engine/effects/` (filter
  evaluation for `CardPredicate.controller`, `packages/shared/src/effects/ir/filters/cardPredicates.ts`).
- Expected: `controller: "self"` restricts candidates to the controller's own battle area.
- Actual: the field is ignored; candidates include both players' Digimon.
- Not a defect in EX10-007 (this card must have no gate), but it means any peer card relying on
  `controller: "self"` for a DP buff is unproven. Worth a sweep across the set.

No test is retained red for this card.

- Peer `EX10-010` is used as a fixture for Q5013 and behaves per the ruling. Its `AllTurns`
  `opponentHas` condition filter omits `zone: "battleArea"`; per the worker brief that filter
  also counts breeding. Not my file — flagging for the EX10-010 lane.
- Stack proof: EX10-007 was exercised both as the evolving top card (two alternate-route
  digivolutions with source-stack identity asserted) and as a digivolution card under EX10-008,
  where only its inherited clause applies and ＜Raid＞ does not leak upward.

### EX10-008 — MetalGreymon

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated

across the collection. The older report below is historical.

Lane: card-only worker. Base commit 46810fe1e271215d173854b82513f4ce30508b01.

#### Card summary

Catalog record (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Lv.5 Red/Black Digimon,
play cost 7, DP 7000, Ultimate, Virus, Cyborg, R, max 4. Two ordinary evolution routes
(Red Lv.4 cost 4, Black Lv.4 cost 4).

Printed clauses:

#### Catalog discrepancy

The catalog `inheritedEffectText` reads "... if this Digimon has [Greymon] in its name in
its name, ...". The phrase "in its name" is duplicated. This is a catalog text defect, not
a rules difference; the module already encodes the intended single name check as a
structured `selfHasNameContaining` condition rather than parsing the malformed string. Not
edited (catalog is out of lane scope).

#### What this re-audit changed

The module `EX10-008.ts` was **not** changed. The previous audit's IR is correct as far as
this lane could test it.

The test file was rewritten. The previous version proved C2 and C3 only through injected
timing (`advance.fire(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)` and
`advance.fireSubTrigger("startOfYourMainPhase" / "whenAttackTargetSwitched")`), which is
structural evidence only. Every clause now has a natural origin:

- C2 On Play now originates from a `playCard` intent (cost 7 paid, hand and battle area
  asserted), not a fired timing window.
- The gained `[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks.` now fires from the real
  turn loop (`engine.runOneTurn()` for seat 1). No intent declares that attack; the test
  waits for the ＜Collision＞-forced block window to open, blocks, and asserts the
  attacker suspended, `hasAttackedThisTurn`, and seat 0's security untouched because the
  attack was blocked.
- The ＜Collision＞ half is now proved by its rules effect (§16-30): a plain Digimon with
  no ＜Blocker＞ becomes an eligible blocker, and `declineBlock` is refused.
- C3 now originates from a real ＜Blocker＞ declaration
  (`combat/controller.ts switchDefenderToBlocker`, the production publisher of
  `whenAttackTargetSwitched`), for the positive case, the once-per-turn case (two real
  attacks and two real blocks in one turn), the name gate and the turn window.
- A catalog conformance test was added.

Also fixed in the test: `expect(perm.stack).toEqual([])` compared a colyseus `ArraySchema`
against a plain array; it now compares `stack.map(card => card.cardId)`.

#### Round 3 additions (peer / stack 2/2)

Three tests were added; nothing was removed and every previous assertion still stands.

##### (a) Comparative peer case for the target-switch convention

Round 3 settled seam 5: a generic "When attack targets change" watcher has no attacker-is-self
gate; only cards printing "this Digimon's attack target" carry one. The peer chosen is
**BT11-008 Bearmon** — implemented, quiet (its own file passes), and its IR carries
`condition: { kind: "triggerAttackerIsSelf" }`, the gate EX10-008 deliberately lacks. EX9-054 was
rejected as the peer: its printed text is also the unscoped "When attack targets change", so it
is on EX10-008's side of the convention, not the opposite side.

The contrast runs on one board, in one turn, on one switch, with both windows open at once.
EX10-008's window is `[Opponent's Turn]`, so its host sits on the defending seat; BT11-008's is
`[Your Turn]`, so its host sits on the attacking seat. Seat 1's turn, a third Digimon attacks and
a fourth blocks:

- EX10-008's unscoped watcher fires although its host neither attacked nor blocked — seat 1's
  top security card is trashed (2 -> 1).
- BT11-008's self-scoped watcher sees the identical switch with its `[Your Turn]` window open and
  stays silent — its host holds at 5000 DP.

The control test proves that watcher is alive on the same board shape: when the BT11-008 host is
itself the attacker and is blocked, it pays out (5000 -> 8000 DP) while the unscoped EX10-008
watcher fires for that same switch too.

##### (b) Q5014 proved end to end through public play

The pre-existing Q5014 test reaches immunity through `advance.verb.restrict`. Two new tests reach
it through cards only, using **EX10-010 BlackWarGreymon** as the grantee: its `[All Turns]` clause
grants "your opponent's Digimon's effects don't affect this Digimon" while its opponent controls a
Digimon with 13000 DP or more.

- Negative (gate opened publicly): seat 0 plays EX10-008 for 7 and grants ＜Collision＞ plus the
  forced attack to the opposing EX10-010 (both halves asserted live). Seat 0 then plays
  **BT8-030** (printed 13000 DP) from hand — the only board change. EX10-010's gate arms, which
  the board shows independently as 12000 -> 15000 DP. ＜Collision＞ goes inactive at once, and
  running seat 1's real turn produces no attack: the grantee is unsuspended, has not attacked, no
  block window opened, and seat 0's security is untouched.
- Mirror positive (gate shut): the same fixture without BT8-030. EX10-010 stays at 12000, the
  granted `[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks.` fires from the real turn loop, the
  ＜Collision＞ half forces seat 0 into the block window, and seat 0 blocks.

Q5014 no longer rests on a test-only restriction primitive: the immunity now arrives from a
printed card clause armed by a public `playCard`.

##### Engine seam observed on the peer (BT11-008, not this card)

While building the comparison, a two-switch variant showed BT11-008's `[Once Per Turn]` budget
being consumed by a switch its `triggerAttackerIsSelf` condition then rejected: after a
non-qualifying switch earlier in the same turn, a later qualifying switch paid nothing, while the
same qualifying switch alone pays +3000. That points at the frequency gate being decremented
before the action-level condition is evaluated
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts`, the `frequency` bookkeeping
around the installed watcher versus the per-action `condition` check in `runAction`). It does not
affect EX10-008, which has no such condition, so no red test is retained here; reported for the
coordinator because it would misprice BT11-008/BT11-010/BT11-014. The two shipped peer tests use
one switch each, so they do not depend on the ordering either way.

#### Q5014 — what is actually proven, and what is not

The test plays MetalGreymon, gives the grant, then applies a blanket `beAffected`
restriction to the grantee through the production restriction primitive and runs seat 1's
turn. The observable result matches the ruling: the grantee does not attack, no block
window opens, and seat 0's security is untouched.

The ＜Collision＞ half is genuinely gated: `ContinuousEffects.keywordGrantIsActive`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/continuous.ts:1164`) re-checks the recipient's `beAffected`
restriction on every read, so the grant deactivates the moment immunity applies. That is
the mechanism Q5015 needs, and Q5015 is proved end to end.

The gained-trigger half is **not** demonstrably gated the same way. Grep found no
`beAffected` check in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/subtriggers.ts` or in
`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts`. The installed
`startOfYourMainPhase` watcher is a discrete install, not a continuously re-derived grant,
so nothing re-evaluates immunity at the trigger timing. In the fixture used, the absence of
the attack is consistent with the ruling but may be produced downstream (the `Attack`
action failing for an unaffectable subject) rather than by a trigger-timing immunity gate.
This is recorded as a gap rather than claimed as proof — see below.

#### Remaining gaps

1. **Q5014 gated-watcher seam (open question, not a red test).** Round 3 note: the ruling's
   endpoint is now proved through a fully public route (EX10-010's printed immunity armed by
   playing a 13000 DP Digimon), so the observable behaviour is card-driven rather than
   primitive-driven. The internal question below is unchanged — the correct outcome is reached,
   but this lane still cannot show which mechanism produces it. Seam:
   `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts`, the gate list built in
   `installGrantedTriggeredEffect` (the `ownerMainPhaseGate` / `grantedPermanentAttackGate`
   family, around line 1254). Expected: a granted triggered effect does not run when the
   grantee has an active `beAffected` restriction against the granting seat at the trigger
   timing. Actual: no such gate exists; the outcome under test happens to match. A coordinator
   with engine scope should confirm whether the correct behaviour is a gate here or a
   teardown of the watcher when immunity is granted. No test is left red because the
   observable endpoint currently agrees with the ruling.
2. **No inherited-effect proof through a real evolution stack.** C3 is proved with a
   board-laid host carrying EX10-008 as a digivolution card. Building that stack by playing
   and digivolving through Lv.3 → Lv.4 → EX10-008 → Lv.6 was out of budget for this lane.
3. ~~**`whenAttackTargetSwitched` has no source filter in the IR.**~~ **Settled in round 3** and
   now proved by the comparative peer case above: the unscoped IR is correct for this printed
   text, and BT11-008 is the gated counterpart. Original note kept for the record: The printed text is
   generic ("when attack targets change"), and the once-per-turn test relies on this: the
   second switch is caused by a different blocker and is still seen by the host's watcher.
   If the intended reading is "when THIS Digimon's attack target changes", the IR and the
   once-per-turn test would both need changing. Peer cards differ here — BT11-008 and
   EX9-054 gate on the host being the attacker, EX12-019 and ST15-01 do not — so the
   coordinator should settle the convention. Flagged, not changed.
4. ＜Reboot＞ is asserted only as an active keyword, not as unsuspend-in-the-opponent's-
   unsuspend-phase behaviour.

| --- | --- |
| C1 | `[When Digivolving] [On Deletion]` Delete all of your opponent's lowest DP Digimon. If this effect didn't delete, trash their deck's top 5 cards. |
| C2 | `[When Attacking]` If your opponent has 10 or more cards in their trash, you may play 1 red or purple 5000 DP or lower Digimon card from your trash to your empty breeding area without paying the cost. |
| C3 | `[End of Your Turn]` This Digimon may attack. |

The catalog text matches the official English list. No discrepancy found.

#### Q&A coverage

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-009` returns five rulings. All five are covered.

| Q&A | Subject | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5016 | No opposing Digimon still meets "didn't delete" | `Q5016 mills exactly 5 when the opponent has no Digimon to delete` |
| Q5017 | A single undeleteable Digimon still meets "didn't delete" | `Q5017 mills 5 when the only opposing Digimon cannot be deleted by opponent effects` |
| Q5018 | One of two tied Digimon deleted → condition NOT met | `Q5018 does not mill when at least one of the tied lowest DP Digimon is deleted` |
| Q5019 | Breeding play does not fire `[On Play]` | `Q5019 does not fire the played Digimon's On Play effect` |
| Q5656 | Ordering against BT24-078's `{Trash} [Your Turn]` effect | Retained red `it.fails` — see "Retained red" below |

Q5017 and Q5018 use BT14-062 Datamon (`[All Turns] This Digimon can't be deleted by your
opponent's effects.`) as the prevention source, so the ruling is proved against a real
printed static rather than an armed ledger entry.

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | IR (`EX10-009.ts`) | Behavioural test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 delete, `[When Digivolving]` | `trigger: "WhenDigivolving"` → `Delete` with `superlative: "lowestDP"`, `count: "all"`, `controller: "opponent"` | `publicly digivolves from a red / purple level 5 source ...` (public `digivolve` intent, both printed colours), `deletes every tied lowest DP Digimon at once and does not mill`, `ignores the opponent's breeding-area Digimon when picking the lowest DP` |
| C1 delete, `[On Deletion]` | `trigger: "OnDeletion"`, same action list | `fires the same clause on deletion after losing a battle it declared` (real `attack` intent, Creepymon 12000 loses to a 15000 DP defender, its `[On Deletion]` then deletes that defender) |
| C1 fallback mill | `TrashTopDeck` with `condition.kind: "ifThisEffectDidNotDelete"`, `amount: 5`, `controller: "opponent"` | Q5016, Q5017 (fires); Q5018 and the two tie tests (does not fire) |
| C1 evolution routes | catalog `evoCosts` | both colour routes assert stack identity, memory 4 → 0 and the exact bonus-draw instance; `refuses a level 4 source ...` is the illegal-source negative |
| C2 gate | `condition: { kind: "zoneCount", seat: "opponent", zone: "trash", op: "gte", value: 10 }` | `plays a red / purple 5000 DP Digimon ...` (at 10) and `offers no breeding play at 9 opposing trash cards ...` (at 9) |
| C2 candidate filter | `colors: ["Red","Purple"]` (OR, `interpreter/matching/definition.ts:111-113`), `dp: { op: "lte", value: 5000 }` against printed DP (`definition.ts:206-208`), `kind: ["Digimon"]` | `plays nothing when the trash holds only off-colour or over-DP Digimon` (blue 3000 and red 6000 both refused), plus the mixed pool in the positive cases |
| C2 destination | `breeding: true`, `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: false` | positive cases assert the card is in breeding and no cost was paid; `offers no breeding play while the breeding area is occupied` covers the empty-slot gate (`interpreter/actions/play.ts:221-224`) |
| C2 optionality | `optional: true` | the "may" prompt is answered by `autoAcceptOptional` in the positive cases |
| C2 no `[On Play]` | engine-side: `primitives.ts:1147` skips the played-trigger block when `opts.breeding` | Q5019 asserts the +3000 DP from EX10-007's `[On Play]` lands nowhere |
| C3 | `trigger: "EndOfYourTurn"` → `Attack` with `target.filter.isSelfRef`, `withoutSuspending: false`, `optional: true` | `attacks at the end of its controller's real turn and suspends doing so`, `declines the optional end-of-turn attack ...`, `cannot attack again at end of turn once its main-phase attack suspended it` |

`Attack` with a self-referencing `target` and no `attacker` resolves to the source
permanent and leaves the attack target open to the controller
(`interpreter/actions/combat.ts:17,37-41,49-53`), which matches "This Digimon may attack"
(player or suspended Digimon). `withoutSuspending: false` is correct: the printed text has
no "without suspending" wording, unlike BT21-072.

All C1–C3 proof now runs through public intents (`digivolve`, `attack`) and
`advance.runTurn` / `runOneTurn` + `waitForMainPhase`. No injected timing
(`advance.fire`, `fireTiming`, `fireSubTrigger`) remains in the suite.

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed suite (base `46810fe1e`) proved every clause with `advance(...).fire(...)`
and `advance(...).verb.deletePermanent(...)`. Under this run's rules that is structural
only, so the prior 10/10 claim had zero behavioural credit. It also had:

- no digivolution at all (no route, no cost, no bonus draw, no illegal-source negative);
- no real attack declaration and no real turn loop;
- no Q5017 and no Q5018 case (deletion prevention was untested in either direction);
- no Q5656 case;
- a colour/DP filter "proved" only by a card that matched it (EX10-007 is Red), with no
  candidate the filter had to reject;
- a suspended-Creepymon fixture (`suspended: true`) for the end-of-turn case that cannot
  prove anything through the real loop, because the turn's own Unsuspend step clears it.

No defect was found in `EX10-009.ts`. The module is unchanged from `HEAD`.

#### Retained red

`it.fails("Q5656 loses its When Attacking window when the trash digivolve is ordered first")`.

Q5656 says the two `[When Attacking]` reactions (EX10-009's own clause and BT24-078
Creepymon (X Antibody)'s `{Trash} [Your Turn]` digivolve) trigger simultaneously so the
player chooses the order, and that digivolving first costs you EX10-009's `[When
Attacking]`.

- Seam: the sub-trigger fire path in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/subtriggers.ts` and the
  timing-window queue in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/EffectContext.ts` (`fireTiming` /
  `fireSubTriggers`).
- Expected: an `orderTriggers` decision is raised; with the BT24-078 branch first, breeding
  stays empty and BT1-013 stays in trash.
- Actual: decisions are `["optional","optional","optional"]` — no `orderTriggers` is ever
  offered — and after BT24-078 digivolves over EX10-009 the already-queued `[When
  Attacking]` entry still resolves, putting BT1-013 into breeding. The queue does not
  re-check that the effect's source is still the permanent's top card.
- Not fixable in this lane: both defects live in the engine, and BT24-078 is another card's
  file (it also still carries `// @ts-nocheck`).

| `breeding: true` → `false` | 5 failed / 14 passed — log `logs/EX10-009-mutation-breeding-false.log` |

Both mutations were reverted; `git diff` on the module is empty.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q5656 is unproved (retained red above); it needs an engine fix, not a card fix.
- The `[On Deletion]` timing is proved through a battle loss only. Deletion by an opponent's
  effect or by a rule check is not separately covered; both reach the same `OnDeletion`
  fire site, so the extra cases would add coverage, not new semantics.

  can also attack on the opponent's turn only through C3, which is on your own turn, so no
  opponent-turn case exists to write.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR in `EX10-010.ts` | Behavioural test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ＜Blast Digivolve＞ from hand | `trigger: "Counter"`, `isFromHand: true`, keyword `BlastDigivolve` | "[Hand] [Counter] ＜Blast Digivolve＞ enters play in the opponent's real counter window" |
| ＜Raid＞ | `Static` + keyword `Raid` | "＜Raid＞ redirects a public player-directed attack onto the highest-DP unsuspended Digimon" |
| ＜Reboot＞ | `Static` + keyword `Reboot` | "＜Reboot＞ unsuspends it during the opponent's real unsuspend phase" |
| ＜Blocker＞ | `Static` + keyword `Blocker` | "＜Blocker＞ blocks a public attack, and the battle removing the 13000 DP Digimon drops the gate" |
| [On Play] deletion, cost 7 boundary | `OnPlay` -> `Delete` with `playCostLte: 7`, `kind: ["Digimon","Tamer"]`, `controller: "opponent"` | "[On Play] through a public playCard deletes the cost-7 Digimon and leaves the cost-8 one"; "[On Play] finds no target when every opposing Digimon costs more than 7" |
| [When Digivolving] deletion | `WhenDigivolving` -> same `Delete` | "public digivolution over an inert Lv.5 keeps the source under the top card and draws the bonus"; "uses the normal cost-4 evolution route and can delete an opposing Tamer" (Tamer half of the target kind); Blast Digivolve test (same clause in a counter window) |
| Evolution requirement Lv.5 cost 4 | catalog `evoCosts` | public digivolve test (memory 4 -> 0) plus "refuses an illegal Lv.4 source for the Lv.5 digivolution requirement" |
| ＜Overflow＞ 4 (ACE) | catalog `overflowMemory` | "＜Overflow＞ 4 charges its own controller when it is deleted in a public battle"; "＜Overflow＞ is not charged when the ACE ENTERS the battle area" |
| [All Turns] +3000 DP, gated | `AllTurns` -> `ModifyDP` amount 3000, `condition: opponentHas ... dp gte 13000`, self target | Blocker test (public play arms and a public battle disarms the gate); threshold tests at 13000/12999; opponent-only test |
| [All Turns] "opponent's Digimon's effects don't affect this Digimon" | `GrantImmunity` `immuneFrom: "opponentDigimonEffects"` with the same `condition` | "Q5020/Q5021 an opposing Digimon's -3000 DP effect still CHOOSES it but cannot affect it" (gate open / gate shut); "Q5023 an already-applied opposing -3000 DP stops applying the moment the gate opens" |

The module's `condition` field is the typed spelling the shared per-action gate reads
(`action.condition ?? action.while`), so both actions are re-derived on every continuous pass.
That is what makes the gate lapse the moment the qualifying Digimon leaves.

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed suite proved the card mostly through injected timing and direct state mutation:

  card. The cost-7 boundary was proved against a cost-12 Digimon, so cost 8 — the first value
  outside the boundary — was never tested.
- ＜Raid＞, ＜Reboot＞ and ＜Blocker＞ were proved only as `observe(...).hasKeyword(...)` reads.
  No attack, no block, no turn loop.
- ＜Blast Digivolve＞ had no behavioural test at all. Only the compiled record's
  `isFromHand: true` was asserted.
- ＜Overflow＞ 4 had no test at all. Only the catalog value was asserted.
- The 13000 DP gate was moved by writing `perm.baseDP` / `perm.currentDP` directly and by
  `advance.verb.deletePermanent`, never by a card entering or leaving through public play.
- No digivolution negative: an illegal source was never refused.
- "Effects don't affect" was proved only through the `isRestrictedByEffect` observer flag.
  Nothing showed an opposing effect actually failing to change the board.

#### Changes made in this lane

Test file only, all additions plus one replacement:

- Replaced the injected `[On Play]` test with a public `playCard` proof (memory 7 -> 0, the
  cost-7 Digimon in the trash, the cost-8 one untouched), and added the no-candidate case.
- Added a public digivolution proof asserting the source card sits in `Permanent.stack`, the
  evolution bonus draw, the cost-4 route, and the deletion in the same window.
- Added an illegal Lv.4 source negative.
- Added public ＜Raid＞ (redirect onto the 9000 DP Digimon, no security check), ＜Blocker＞
  (block declared through `declareBlock`, security untouched, attacker deleted) and ＜Reboot＞
  (both seat-0 Digimon suspended by real attacks during seat 0's turn; after the production
  turn loop reaches seat 1's Main, only the ＜Reboot＞ Digimon is unsuspended — the inert
  BT1-009 beside it stays suspended as a negative control).
- Added the ＜Blast Digivolve＞ counter-window test: on the opponent's turn, an attack opens the
  counter window, seat 0 answers with `respondCounter` / `blast-digivolve:<permanentId>`, the ACE
  lands on the Lv.5 base with the base card underneath, memory is unchanged, the evolution draw
  happens and `[When Digivolving]` deletes the opponent's cost-7 Digimon in that window.
- Added ＜Overflow＞ 4: charged to the ACE's own controller when it loses a public battle on the
  opponent's turn (`MemoryGauge.memoryFor(1)` drops by exactly 4), and not charged on entry.
- Added the Q5020/Q5021 and Q5023 immunity tests described above.

Fixtures use inert cards only: BT1-024 (Lv.5 Red base), BT10-065 (play cost 7), BT12-069
(play cost 8), BT8-030 (printed 13000 DP), BT1-009..BT1-014 filler, plus BT1-055 Angemon whose
single `[On Play]` -3000 DP clause is the opposing Digimon effect under test.

1. Q5022 and Q5025 are untested. Both need a card that confers an effect onto an opposing
   Digimon; that behaviour belongs to the conferral ledger, not to this card's IR. No engine

2. The two pre-existing Q5013 / Q5024 tests still use `advance.fire` and `advance.verb.*` for

3. The Q5023 test resets `state.memory` between the two public plays so the second play is

4. No retained red tests. No `it.fails` or `it.skip` in this file.

| Printed colour routes | `evoCosts: [{ Red, 5, 6 }, { Purple, 5, 6 }, { Blue, 5, 6 }]` | `digivolves by the printed Red Lv.5 route for memory 6…` — BT1-020 Groundramon (Red Lv.5) through the public `digivolve` intent with no `alternateRequirementIndex`: memory 6 → 0 (the printed cost, not the alternate route's 5), `stack === ["BT1-020"]`, and the named top-of-deck card ends in hand as the evolution bonus draw. |
| 2 Trash Main | `trigger: "Main"`, `isFromTrash: true`, `PlayWithoutCost` with `payCost: true`, `reduceCostBy: 11`, cost `deleteOwn` count 2, filter `levelComparison gte 5` + `nameOrTrait [{ tokens: ["Myotismon"], match: "text" }]` | `deletes exactly 2 qualifying Digimon…` — `memoryChanged 3 → 0` (14 − 11), both cost Digimon in the trash, MaloMyotismon on the board. Negatives in the two Q5028 tests. |
| 3 Shared two-delete | three effects (`OnPlay`, `WhenDigivolving`, `WhenAttacking`), each `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `sharedUseKey: "ir-shared-0"`, `Delete` count 2, filter `controllerDefault: "any"`, `excludeSelf`, `unsuspended` | `Q5029 deletes 2 other unsuspended…` (controller and mandatoriness), `shares one two-delete use across When Digivolving and a real attack…` (public `digivolve`, then a real `attack` intent the same turn deletes nothing, then the same attack on the next own turn through `startTurnLoop` deletes the survivor), `deletes exactly 2 qualifying Digimon…` (On Play path). |
| 4 All Turns payoff | `trigger: "AllTurns"`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `SubTrigger onDeletionOf` with `sourceFilter { controller: "mine", excludeSelf: true, kind: ["Digimon","Tamer"] }`, then `trashSecurityTop` (opponent, 1) and `Return` `superlative: "lowestDP"` to `deckBottom` | `trashes security and bottom-decks exactly 1 lowest-DP Digimon once per turn` (pre-existing) and `Q5029 deletes 2 other unsuspended…`, where deleting my own Digimon costs the opponent one security card and bottom-decks their surviving Digimon. |

#### Commands and results

Run from `/Users/viniciusluiz/orca/workspaces/aegis-digimon-tcg/audit-ex10-reaudit`.

# 0 errors overall                               -> logs/EX10-011-typecheck.log
