#### What the previous lane left, and what changed

`EX10-032.ts` is unchanged from HEAD; only the test was edited. The committed version is in
`logs/EX10-032-head.test.ts.log` (238 lines) for comparison. The cancelled lane's partial file
was kept where it was correct and repaired where it was not:

- Kept: the catalog assertion, the IR map, Q5091, Q5092, all three trigger tests, the
  "such Digimon" trait reading, the duration test and the decline test. Re-run and verified.
- Fixed: the `never places under the OPPONENT's Sunarizamon` test guarded its activation
  behind `if (entries.length > 0)`, which made the whole branch vacuous if the effect were
  never offered. It IS offered (the activatable list only screens the `[Close]` condition,
  not host availability), so the branch is now an unconditional
  `expect(entries).toHaveLength(1)` followed by the activation and endpoint assertions on
  memory, hand, trash and the opponent's untouched stack.
- Added: `never spends the OPPONENT's digivolution cards and never buffs their [Mineral]
  Digimon` — a mirror board where the opponent's [Mineral] top card and its [Mineral] stack
  card are listed FIRST in `preferInstanceIds`. Fixture note: the first attempt used EX10-025
  Sunarizamon as the cost card and the opponent's Digimon vanished — EX10-025's inherited
  clause deletes an opponent Digimon with play cost 4 or less, and EX10-028 costs 4. The
  fixture now uses BT10-062 Golemon, a [Mineral] Digimon with no main and no inherited text,
  so the cost trash cannot move anything by itself.

Round 2 added four tests, no assertion removed or weakened:

- `Q5092 P-107's ＜Delay＞, played and activated publicly ...` — P-107 is played from hand
  (`playCard`), a full turn passes through `startTurnLoop`, then its ＜Delay＞ is activated
  through `activateEffect` and digivolves a legal Lv.4 Black source (BT3-067 Tankmon, no
  [Mineral]/[Rock] trait, so clause 2 finds no cost card and cannot disturb the endpoints).
  Red on the P-107 cost defect below; every other endpoint asserted and green.
- `Q5092 negative: the activated ＜Delay＞ cannot put this card onto a Lv.3 [Sunarizamon] ...` —
  the same public route with only a Lv.3 host: nothing moves, this card stays in hand, the
  Landramon stays in the trash, memory is untouched, and clause 1 then still resolves on its own.
- `Q5092 leak: P-107's spent -2 must not reduce this card's [Hand] [Main] cost` — red, the
  Q5092 breach itself.
- `peer: EX10-028 Landramon spends the same [Mineral] cost pool but grants ＜Reboot＞/＜Blocker＞` —
  the comparative [Mineral]-buff peer case.

#### Defects and seams

No defect in EX10-032 itself was found that a test could pin. Exercising the ＜Delay＞ path
publicly did surface a defect in the PEER card, which this lane may not edit:

- **P-107 Defense Training's −2 registers too late and then leaks.** `apps/api/src/cards/P/P-107.ts`,
  `compiled.effects[1].actions`: the `Replacement { event: "wouldDigivolve", mode: "reduceCost",
  amount: 2 }` is listed AFTER the `Digivolve` action in the same array, so it registers only once
  the digivolution it is meant to modify has already been paid for. Two consequences, both red
  here and both kept exact:
  - `Q5092 P-107's ＜Delay＞, played and activated publicly ...` (`it.fails`): the ＜Delay＞
    digivolve into this card pays the FULL printed 3 — memory 5 → 2, expected 5 → 4.
  - `Q5092 leak: P-107's spent -2 must not reduce this card's [Hand] [Main] cost` (`it.fails`):
    the armed replacement survives the Option leaving the battle area and reduces the NEXT
    digivolution — this card's own clause 1 pays 1 instead of 2 (memory 3 → 2, expected 3 → 1).
    That is the Q5092 breach itself: the ruling says the two effects cannot be used together.
  Fix belongs in P-107.ts (register the replacement before the digivolve, and scope it to that
  one digivolution), outside this lane's allowed files.

Two further observations for the coordinator:

- Seam, not a card bug: `activatableEffectsJson` offers the `[Hand] [Main]` clause when the
  `youHave` condition holds even though no legal placement host exists. Activating it is a
  clean no-op (nothing moves, no memory spent, no pending decision), so it is a UI-affordance
  wart rather than a rules break. Named in the test comment.
- The buff target's and the trash cost's `controller: "mine"` are inert: removing them does
  not change behaviour, so the engine already scopes both to the controller. The fields are
  correct and harmless, but the previous audit's comment implies they are load-bearing for
  those two actions. Only `placeCost.hostFilter.controller` is.
- `condition.filter.kind: ["Digimon", "Tamer"]` on the `[Close]` gate is unfalsifiable: every
  card named "Close" in the catalog (EX10-063, EX11-065, EX8-067, P-169) is a Tamer, so no
  fixture can distinguish it from an unkinded filter.

No test is `it.fails` or `it.skip`. No assertion was weakened.

#### Remaining gaps

- P-107's ＜Delay＞ clause carries no `notEnteredThisTurn` condition in its IR, so the engine
  would allow it on the turn the Option entered play. The tests here still respect the rule
  (play on one turn, activate after a full turn loop), so the gap is reported rather than
  relied on. Seam: `apps/api/src/cards/P/P-107.ts` (the condition kind exists —
  `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/conditions.ts:747`).
- The inherited clause's carrier is a seeded stack (`under:`), not a stack assembled by
  public intents. Landramon is a Lv.4 main-deck card, so no Digi-Egg route is required, but
  the carrier was not built through breeding.
- The inherited watcher is proved only for a trash caused by this card's own cost. A trash
  caused by an opponent effect is not covered; the printed text names no controller and the
  IR `sourceFilter` does not restrict it either, so this is untested rather than wrong.
- Clause 2 is compiled as three identical effect entries (`OnPlay`, `WhenDigivolving`,
  `WhenAttacking`). Faithful to the print, but triplicated; the same note applies to EX10-028.
- EX10-063 Close's own `[All Turns]` memory gain ("when effects trash any of your [Mineral]
  or [Rock] trait Digimon's digivolution cards, by suspending this Tamer, gain 1 memory") is
  never exercised alongside clause 2. That peer interaction belongs to a Close-side test.

### EX10-033 — Pyramidimon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-033.test.ts`: Fragment, simultaneous ordering, up-to cost sizes/cross-stack sources, dynamic cost reduction/floor and quota reset.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Black Lv.6 Mega, Virus, [Mineral] [LIBERATOR]. Play cost 11, DP 11000.
Digivolve: Black Lv.5 for 3 memory. No inherited text, no security text.

#### Printed clauses

1. `＜Fragment (3)＞` — keyword only.
2. `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn]` You may place up to 3 [Mineral] or
   [Rock] trait cards from your trash as this Digimon's **bottom** digivolution cards.
3. `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking]` By trashing up to 3 [Mineral] or [Rock] trait cards
   from **any** of your Digimon's digivolution cards, to 1 of your opponent's Digimon, reduce
   the play cost by 2 until their turn ends **for each card trashed**.

Clause 3 carries no `[Once Per Turn]`, so it may fire on the digivolve and again on the attack.

#### Catalog discrepancy

`cards.json` stores `[Rock] trait` in both clauses: the space between `[Rock]` and
`trait` is U+00A0 (non-breaking), not U+0020. Two occurrences, offsets 109 and 258 of
`effectText`. Nothing else in the record deviates from the official list. Not edited — the
catalog assertion now spells the escape so the stored bytes are visible instead of hidden.
Worth a coordinator sweep: any other card whose text was scraped the same way will fail an
exact-text assertion for the same invisible reason.

#### Q&A coverage (KB, 7 ids)

| id | ruling | proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5094 | simultaneous triggers, player chooses activation order | `Q5094: the order choice is offered…` — asserts the pending `orderTriggers` decision offers both effect keys (`EX10-033/ir-shared-0`, `EX10-033/ir-7-1`) and drives placement-first through `preferTriggerKeys`; `Q5094/Q5099: reduction first…` drives the reverse order and reads the smaller reduction |
| Q5095 | [Mineral]/[Rock] **Digi-Egg** cards from trash are legal placement material | `Q5095/Q5096…` places EX10-003 Tumblemon (Black Lv.2 [Rock] Digi-Egg) out of the trash |
| Q5096 | once activated, at least 1 card must be placed; declining activation places none | same test asserts the placement prompt is `min: 1, max: 3`; `Q5096/Q5099: declining the optional placement…` runs the digivolve under `autoDeclineOptional` and asserts no card left the trash |
| Q5097 | the reduced play cost floors at 0, never negative | `Q5097/Q5098/Q5100…` trashes 3 cards against a printed-cost-6 Digimon and reads `modifiers.playCostFor(..., 5) === 0` from the testkit ledger view |
| Q5098 | the 3 trashed cards may come from several different Digimon | same test trashes 1 card off the evolved Pyramidimon's stack and 2 off a second Digimon's stack |
| Q5099 | once activated the cost must trash at least 1 card, and "up to 3" pays with fewer when fewer exist | `Q5094/Q5099: reduction first…` pays with the single eligible card for -2; `Q5096/Q5099: declining the optional placement…` pays with the only 2 eligible cards for -4 |
| Q5100 | a Landramon trashed by this cost still deletes the now-cheaper Digimon | `Q5097/Q5098/Q5100…` seats EX10-028 as a digivolution card, trashes it as cost material, and asserts the chosen opponent Digimon left the battle area while its untouched twin stayed |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| clause | IR | test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ＜Fragment (3)＞ | `effects[0]` Static, `keywords:[{Fragment,3}]` | catalog/IR tests plus `observe(engine).hasKeyword(evolved,"Fragment")` on the live permanent |
| clause 2 (digivolve face) | `effects[1]` `WhenDigivolving` `PlaceUnder position:"bottom"`, `frequency:"OncePerTurn"`, `sharedUseKey:"ir-shared-0"`, `optional:true`, target `zone:"trash"`, `count:3 upTo minimum:1` | `Q5095/Q5096…` asserts the 3 placed cards sit **below** the digivolution source instance |
| clause 2 (attack face) | `effects[2]`, same shape, same `sharedUseKey` | `[Once Per Turn] is shared by both triggers…` — real `attack` intent through `startTurnLoop`, same-turn refusal, next-own-turn reset |
| clause 3 (both faces) | `effects[3]`/`effects[4]` `CostModifier reduce play 2`, `existingPermanent`, `duration:"untilOpponentTurnEnd"`, cost `trash` `from:["digivolutionCards"]` `count:3 upTo minimum:1`, `scaling:{per:1,usePaidCount:true}`, `abortOnDecline` | `Q5097/Q5098/Q5100…` (3 cards trashed -> -6), `Q5096/Q5099…` (2 eligible -> -4), `Q5094/Q5099…` (1 eligible -> -2) — the full `upTo` range of the mandatory cost |

No `advance.fire*` / `fireSubTrigger` / `fireTiming` anywhere in the file: both windows
(`[When Digivolving]`, `[When Attacking]`) open from public intents. `duration:
"untilOpponentTurnEnd"` is a real `EffectDurationRef`.

#### Defects found and fixed in this lane

The previous (cancelled) lane left three broken assertions in the test file; all three were
wrong, not the card:

1. **Catalog text assertion** compared against plain spaces and failed on the two U+00A0
   characters. Fixed with explicit ` ` escapes plus a comment.
2. **Once-per-turn test** called `declineBlock` after both attacks. The opponent controls no
   Digimon in that fixture, so no block window stays open and the intent returned
   `{ok:false, reason:"wrong-phase"}`. Removed; the attacks now settle straight on the
   security count.
3. **Q5094 was retained red claiming the engine raises no `orderTriggers` decision.** That is
   false — the engine does raise it, and the `it.fails` failed because it passed. Replaced
   with a real behavioural ordering proof: because clause 2 feeds clause 3 (cards it places
   become legal trash material), the chosen order changes the outcome. Placement first gives
   clause 3 four eligible cards and a -6 reduction on a cost-6 Digimon (down to 0).

#### Retained reds

None. The previously retained red
(`any-digimon-digivolution-trash-cost-ignores-upTo` in
`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/costs.ts`, `payCost`) was fixed by engine lane 1
(seam 19): `cost.target.upTo` and `cost.target.minimum` are now honoured for the "any of
your Digimon's digivolution cards" trash cost, so the clause pays with 1, 2 or 3 cards and
scales the reduction by the paid count. All three payment sizes are now asserted
behaviourally. `EX10-033.ts` was not modified in either lane.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q5097's "minimum value is 0" is read off `advance(engine).ledgers.modifiers.playCostFor`,
  because no card effect can distinguish a stored `-1` from `0`. The floor itself is covered
  by `engine/effects/modifiers.test.ts`.
- The `[Once Per Turn]` gate is proved through the real turn loop (digivolve spends it, the
  same turn's attack cannot place, the next own turn's attack places again), so no structural
  exception is claimed here.
- Which 3 of 4 eligible cards clause 3 trashes is steered by `preferInstanceIds`; the engine
  offers no ordering guarantee and none is asserted.
- Clause 3 is mandatory, so no "decline the reduction" case exists to test; the only
  optional-decline case is clause 2, and it is covered.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Lv.6 Black/Purple Digimon, play cost 13,
DP 13000, form Mega, attribute Vaccine, types `[Mineral]` `[Bagra Army]`, rarity R,
`maxCountInDeck` 4. EvoCosts: Lv.5 Black cost 5, Lv.5 Purple cost 5. No inherited text, no
security text. Catalog and official text agree; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

1. `＜Collision＞`
2. `＜Fragment (3)＞`
3. `＜Blocker＞`
4. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` Until your opponent's turn ends, give 1 of their Digimon
   `"[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks."`
5. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` When Digimon attack, by trashing any 2 of this Digimon's

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5101 | The gained "[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks" may be given to a Digimon unaffected by effects, but it will not trigger if that Digimon is unaffected at the trigger timing. | Full. Positive half: the grant, the subscription, the granted Digimon really declaring the attack on its own Main Phase, and the grant expiring. Negative half (round 3): an opposing EX10-010 BlackWarGreymon whose "unaffected by your Digimon's effects" gate is open still RECEIVES the grant (`preserveUnaffectableSelection`, Q6740) and still never attacks, while a second, ordinary opposing Digimon on the same board does attack. The peer pair on EX10-008 proves the same gate open and shut. |
| Q5102 | You cannot pay by trashing only 1 digivolution card; a "by" cost is all-or-nothing. | Proved twice: with 5 cards under, exactly 2 are trashed and 3 remain; with only 1 card under, nothing is trashed and no part of the effect happens. |
| Q5103 | The `[All Turns]` effect triggers when either player's Digimon attacks. | Proved on both sides: the opponent's attack arms it (buff taken on the opponent's turn) and Blastmon's own attack arms it (decline path). |

Natural origin: no `advance.fire*`, `fireSubTrigger` or `fireTiming` call appears in the file.
Every clause is driven by `playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `declareBlock`, `declineBlock` and
the production turn loop (`startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase` / `endMainPhaseIfOpen`). Nothing
reaches `engine.continuous`, `engine.ledger` or `engine.primitives`; the duration is proved by
walking the turn loop past two turn ends.

Round-3 fixtures add BT8-030 Surfimon (printed 13000 DP, no text — the gate opener), EX10-010
BlackWarGreymon (the immunity) and EX10-008 MetalGreymon (the peer granter). None of those three
files was edited.

Fixtures use only cards with no effect text at all: BT1-009, BT1-013, BT1-014, BT1-019
(DarkTyrannomon, Lv.4 6000), BT10-064 (Gogmamon, Black Lv.5), BT10-079 (Sandiramon, Purple Lv.5).
EX10-026 / EX10-027 / EX10-039 appear only as DigiXros material candidates, where they are placed
as digivolution cards and their `[On Play]` text never resolves. No Digi-Egg in deck or security,
no numeric `security: <n>` form.

#### Defects found and fixed in this lane

The file on disk carried a cancelled lane's partial edits. Kept what was correct; fixed three
things, all in the test:

1. Typecheck error at the `＜Fragment (3)＞` attack: `target: { kind: "digimon", … }` is not an
   `AttackTarget`; the union is `"player" | "permanent"`. Corrected to `kind: "permanent"`.
2. Second attack of the same turn: the test declined a block that could not be offered. Blastmon
   suspended itself blocking the first attack, so the second block window opens with no eligible
   blocker and closes on its own. The `declineBlock` intent returned `{ ok: false, reason:
   "wrong-phase" }`. Replaced with a settle on the security drop plus `pendingDecision`
   undefined.
3. Wrong `＜Collision＞` assertion: the test expected the defender to publish a `Blocker` keyword.
   `＜Collision＞` is read at block legality (`apps/api/src/engine/combat/legality.ts`,
   `hasCollision`, CR 16-30), not granted as a keyword on the defender. The assertion now records
   `hasKeyword(chump, "Blocker") === false` and proves the rule behaviourally: `declineBlock` is
   refused and the non-Blocker's `declareBlock` is accepted.
4. The turn loop stalled on seat 1's Main phase; added the missing `endMainPhaseIfOpen(1)` before
   waiting for seat 0's Main phase.

Added: the Purple Lv.5 digivolve route, which the previous file never exercised (only the Black
row was proved).

No module change was needed. The module already matches the printed text and the three rulings.

#### Mutation check

Changed the `[All Turns]` `GainKeyword` duration from `"untilYourTurnEnd"` to
`"untilOpponentTurnEnd"` in `EX10-034.ts` and re-ran the focused file: the duration test failed
(`expected +0 to be 1` on the `＜Security A. +1＞` amount after the opponent's turn ended). Module
restored; `git diff` on `EX10-034.ts` is empty. Log:
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-034-mutation-duration.log`.

#### Round 3: the Q5101 negative

Three tests were added, all driven from public intents and the production turn loop.

1. **Blastmon against an unaffected recipient.** Seat 0 holds BT8-030 (printed 13000 DP), so
   EX10-010's `[All Turns]` gate — "while your opponent has a Digimon with 13000 DP or more,
   your opponent's Digimon's effects don't affect this Digimon" — is already open (BlackWarGreymon
   reads 15000 DP). Seat 0 then plays two Blastmon copies through `playCard`, aiming the first
   grant at BlackWarGreymon and the second at an ordinary BT1-019 on the same board
   (`preferInstanceIds`). Both grants are installed — the engine deliberately preserves an
   unaffectable selection (Q6740) — but at seat 1's Main Phase only BT1-019 attacks. Endpoints:
   exactly one `blockWindowOpened`, one security card checked, BlackWarGreymon unsuspended, no
   pending decision.

2. **Peer EX10-008, gate shut.** MetalGreymon is 7000 DP, so with it as seat 0's only Digimon the
   gate is shut (BlackWarGreymon reads 12000). Its identical grant lands: BlackWarGreymon gains
   ＜Collision＞ and the attack trigger, then really attacks on seat 1's Main Phase. ＜Collision＞
   is proved at block legality, not as a defender keyword — `declineBlock` is refused with
   `wrong-phase` and the non-＜Blocker＞ MetalGreymon's `declareBlock` is accepted, losing 7000
   to 12000 with security untouched.

3. **Peer EX10-008, gate open.** The same board plus BT8-030. ＜Collision＞ is never conferred
   (`GainKeyword` has no unaffectable-selection exemption and BlackWarGreymon is the only
   opposing Digimon, so there is no legal target), the trigger grant is installed and suppressed,
   and seat 1's whole Main Phase passes with no attack: security 2, no `blockWindowOpened`,
   BlackWarGreymon unsuspended.

Finding worth recording: Blastmon can never grant *into* a shut gate against EX10-010, because
Blastmon is itself a printed 13000 DP Digimon — the moment it enters, its own controller satisfies
"a Digimon with 13000 DP or more" and BlackWarGreymon is immune to it. The gate-shut mirror
therefore has to be staged with a lower-DP granter, which is what the EX10-008 pair does.

### EX10-035 — Machinedramon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-035.test.ts`: Hand/Main reduction/delayed deletion, De-Digivolve, Apocalymon-only evolution, face-up security placement and Security play.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`.
Files touched: `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-035.ts`, `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-035.test.ts`,
this report, and `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-035-*.log`.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Black, Digimon, Lv.6 Mega, Virus,
`[Machine]`/`[Dark Masters]`, play cost 11, 11000 DP, `evoCosts: []` (no digivolution
requirement — it can only be played), `maxCountInDeck: 4`, no inherited text.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Hand] [Main]` If you don't have any Digimon other than Digimon with `[Dark Masters]` in
   their texts, you may play this card with the play cost reduced by 5. At turn end, delete
   the Digimon this effect played.
2. `[On Play]` `[When Attacking]` ＜De-Digivolve 2＞ 2 of your opponent's Digimon.
3. `[All Turns]` This Digimon can only digivolve into `[Apocalymon]`.
4. `[On Deletion]` If you have no black face-up security cards, place this Digimon face up as
   the bottom security card.
5. `[Security]` If this card was face-up, you may play 1 level 5 or lower card with
   `[Dark Masters]` in its text from your hand or trash without paying the cost.

No catalog discrepancy found: the catalog text matches the official print, and the module
mirrors it clause for clause.

#### What the previous state missed

The committed test passed (11 tests) but proved four clauses through injected timing or
private engine state, which earns no behavioural credit under this run's rules:

- `[On Play]` De-Digivolve: `advance(s.engine).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)`.
- `[When Attacking]` De-Digivolve: **not tested at all**.
- Turn-end self-delete: a private `fireTiming(EffectTiming.OnEndTurn)` cast plus a hand-set
  `state.turnSeat`, not the turn loop.
- `[All Turns]` digivolve restriction: a hand-built `ContinuousEffectLedger` and a direct
  `validateDigivolve` call with a hand-written `digivolveIntoAllowed` dep — the card wiring
  was never exercised.
- `[On Deletion]`: fired through `advance.verb.deletePermanent`, and it asserted only
  `faceUp`, never the **bottom** position and never the negative branch (a black face-up
  security card already present).
- Only 3 of the 11 listed Q&A ids were named.

The rewritten suite drives every clause from a public intent (`playCard`, `attack`,
`digivolve`, `activateEffect`) or the real turn loop (`startTurnLoop` +
`advance.waitForMainPhase`). No `advance.fire*` / `fireTiming` remains in the file.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Hand] [Main]` gate + cost −5 | "plays this card from hand for 11 − 5 …", "does not play … while a non-[Dark Masters] Digimon is out" | `effects[0]` `trigger: "Main"`, `isFromHand: true`, `condition.youHaveNone` with `excludeNameOrTrait [{tokens:["Dark Masters"], match:"any"}]`, `PlayWithoutCost { from:["hand"], payCost:true, reduceCostBy:5, isSelfRef }` |
| turn-end delete of the played Digimon | "deletes the Digimon this effect played at its own turn end through the real turn loop", negative: "leaves a normally played copy alive through its own turn end" | `effects[0].actions[1]` `DelayedDeletePlayed` (inside the activated effect, **not** under `OnPlay`) |
| `[On Play]` ＜De-Digivolve 2＞ ×2 | "De-Digivolves exactly 2 of the opponent's Digimon by 2 when played from hand" | `effects[1]` `OnPlay` -> `DeDigivolve { target.count: 2, amount: 2, controller:"opponent", kind:["Digimon"] }` |
| `[When Attacking]` ＜De-Digivolve 2＞ ×2 | "De-Digivolves again on a real attack declaration", "stops at level 3 …" | `effects[2]` `WhenAttacking` -> same `DeDigivolve` |
| `[All Turns]` only into `[Apocalymon]` | "allows [Apocalymon] and refuses another otherwise legal level 7 …" | `effects[3]` `AllTurns` -> `RestrictDigivolveInto { into.nameOrTrait [{tokens:["Apocalymon"], match:"name"}], duration:"permanent" }` |
| `[On Deletion]` bottom face-up security, gated | "places itself face up as the BOTTOM security card after losing a real battle", "goes to the trash instead when a black face-up security card already exists" | `effects[4]` `OnDeletion` -> `SecurityManipulation { op:"placeAsSecurity", controller:"mine", toTop:false, faceUp:true, condition.youHaveNone{zone:"security", faceUp:true, colors:["Black"]} }` |
| `[Security]` free play, level ≤5, `[Dark Masters]` in text | "plays a level 5 or lower [Dark Masters]-text card from hand and then battles", "plays the same card from the trash", "does nothing when the card was checked face down", "refuses a level 6 [Dark Masters] card and a low-level card with no [Dark Masters] text" | `effects[5]` `Security`, `isSecurity: true` -> `PlayWithoutCost { from:["hand","trash"], payCost:false, levelComparison lte 5, nameOrTrait match:"text", condition.sourceWasFaceUpSecurity, optional:true }` |

IR verification notes (read against the engine, not assumed):

- `duration: "permanent"` is a real `EffectDurationRef`
  (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/durations.ts`) mapping to `EffectDuration.Permanent`.
- `match: "any"` and `match: "text"` are the same full union (name ∪ traits ∪ every text
  field) in `matchNameOrTrait`
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:296`). That is exactly the
  Q5104 semantic, so both the exclude gate and the `[Security]` filter are faithful.
- `RestrictDigivolveInto` is consulted by the production digivolve intent:
  `apps/api/src/engine/actions/digivolve.ts:540` -> `continuous.digivolveIntoAllowed`. The
  test therefore proves the card wiring, not a hand-installed ledger entry.
- `DeDigivolve` is dispatched with `byEffectSeat: ctx.source.ownerSeat`
  (`interpreter/actions/digivolution.ts:48`), so the EX11-070 stacked-trash-lock and the
  EX10-029 `cantBeDeDigivolved` grant apply correctly. No defect here.
- The level floor is the engine default `stopAtLevel ?? 3` in `peelStackTops`
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:1868`); the test asserts it on a Lv.4 top.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Where | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5104 | "plays this card from hand for 11 − 5 …" and "plays a level 5 or lower [Dark Masters]-text card …" | BT15-072 Vilemon carries `[Dark Masters]` only in its EFFECT TEXT (name Vilemon, trait `[Evil]`). It satisfies the exclude gate and is a legal `[Security]` target. |
| Q5105 | "places itself face up as the BOTTOM security card …" | The placed card is `faceUp: true` and the two pre-existing security cards stay face down. |
| Q5106 | "plays a level 5 or lower [Dark Masters]-text card … and then battles" | The face-up card is checked normally: the check runs, the Security Digimon battles, and it ends in the trash. |
| Q5107 | same test | The `[Security]` effect fires on the check of a face-up card. |
| Q5108 | **not tested — documented** | No public intent shuffles the security stack, and the testkit exposes no shuffle affordance, so "face-ups are turned face down, then shuffled" has no reachable trigger from a card lane. Seam gap for the coordinator: an `advance`-level or intent-level security-shuffle affordance would be needed. |
| Q5109 | "activates with no Digimon at all" | Empty battle area; the `youHaveNone` condition holds. |
| Q5110 | "still deletes the played host at turn end after it digivolved …" | The reduced-cost copy digivolves into BT15-102 Apocalymon; at turn end the delayed delete and Apocalymon's `[End of Your Turn]` are offered to seat 0 as an `orderTriggers` decision with more than one key. |
| Q5111 | "does nothing when the card was checked face down" | "if this card was face-up" is met only when the card is face-up **as it is checked**. |
| Q5737 | "deletes the Digimon this effect played at its own turn end through the real turn loop" | Real turn loop, no injected `OnEndTurn`. |
| Q5738 | same test as Q5110 | Simultaneous turn-end processing -> the turn player orders it. |
| Q6512 | "plays a level 5 or lower [Dark Masters]-text card from hand and then battles" | The `[Security]` effect resolves, then the 11000 DP Security Digimon battles and deletes the 3000 DP attacker. |

#### Module change

One line: `const compiled` -> `export const compiled`, so the test can assert the registered
IR against the authored object the way the accepted EX10-012 lane does. The IR content is
unchanged, so `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` does not drift from this lane.

#### Defects found and fixed

No behavioural defect in the module. Every clause already resolved correctly once driven from
a public intent; the gap was in the evidence, not the implementation. The audit added:

- the missing `[When Attacking]` proof,
- natural-origin replacements for four injected-timing proofs,
- the bottom-of-security position assertion and the black-face-up-security negative,
- the level-3 floor boundary for ＜De-Digivolve＞,
- the "exactly 2" boundary (a third opposing Digimon left untouched, exact trash contents),
- eight previously unnamed Q&A ids.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q5108 (shuffling a security stack containing face-up cards) has no reachable public trigger.
  Named above as a testkit seam for the coordinator; nothing card-side can prove it.
- The `[Security]` target filter carries no `kind`, matching the printed "1 … card". An
  Option card has no level, so `levelComparison lte 5` already excludes it; that is correct
  but rests on the absence of a level rather than on an explicit rule, so it is worth a
  cross-card check if the level comparison ever changes.

### EX10-036 — Magneticdramon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-036.test.ts`: Simultaneous effects, exact three-card costs, delete/security trash, placement/unsuspend and Fragment; buried-main probe supplemental.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Black Lv.7 Mega, Virus, DP 14000, play cost 14, digivolve Black Lv.6 for 4.
Traits [Rock Dragon] / [LIBERATOR] / [Mineral]. SR, max 4.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|--------|
| C1 | `[Digivolve] While you have [Close], [Proganomon]: Cost 6` |
| C2 | `＜Fragment (3)＞` |
| C3 | `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] By trashing 3 [Mineral] or [Rock] trait cards from any of your Digimon's digivolution cards, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon and trash their top security card.` |
| C4 | `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] By placing 3 [Mineral] or [Rock] trait cards from your trash as this Digimon's bottom digivolution cards, it unsuspends.` |

The catalog record matches the official text. No discrepancy found.

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Content | Proof |
|-----|---------|-------|
| Q5112 | The simultaneous triggers can be activated in the player's chosen order | `Q5112 offers both simultaneous [When Digivolving] effects as one orderTriggers choice` |
| Q5113 | C3's "by" condition cannot be met by trashing fewer than 3 | `Q5113 refuses a partial payment` |
| Q5114 | C3's 3 cards may come from several different Digimon | `Q5114 [When Digivolving] trashes 3 sources spread across two Digimon` |
| Q5115 | C4's "by" condition cannot be met by placing fewer than 3 | `Q5115 refuses a partial placement` |

Rules used beyond the card Q&A: comprehensive §16-37 (＜Fragment＞) and §1-3-2
(an impossible part of an effect is skipped, the rest is still performed).

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Behavioural test | Route |
|--------|----|------------------|-------|
| C1 | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` (`namesExact: ["Proganomon"]`, `controllerControls` [Close] Tamer, cost 6, alternate) | `uses the Close-gated Proganomon evolution for 6 and rejects it without Close` | `digivolve` intent with `alternateRequirementIndex: 0`; source stack identity and the digivolution draw asserted; two negatives (no [Close]; printed Lv.6 requirement against a Lv.5 source) |
| C2 | `Static` effect, `keywords: [{ keyword: "Fragment", amount: 3 }]` | `＜Fragment (3)＞ saves it from battle deletion` | real `attack` against a suspended 20000 DP Digimon; 3 own digivolution cards trashed, permanent survives |
| C3 | `WhenDigivolving` / `WhenAttacking` effect 0: `Delete` (cost `trash`, count 3, `from: ["digivolutionCards"]`, trait-exact) then `trashSecurityTop` | `Q5114 ...`, `Q5113 ...`, `trashes the top security card even when the opponent controls no Digimon` | real `digivolve` (Lv.6 BT2-064 -> EX10-036 for 4) |
| C4 | `WhenDigivolving` / `WhenAttacking` effect 1: `Unsuspend` (cost `place`, count 3, `from: ["trash"]`, `destination: "digivolutionStack"`, `position: "bottom"`, `host: "self"`), `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `sharedUseKey: "ir-shared-0"` | `Q5115 [When Attacking] places exactly 3 ... at the BOTTOM`, `Q5115 refuses a partial placement`, `[Once Per Turn] is shared by both timings and resets on the next own turn` | real `attack`, and a full turn loop for the once-per-turn reset |

Extra coverage, none of it injected timing:

- Exact trait matching. BT2-011 Vorvomon's only trait is `[Rock Dragon]`. It sits in the
  cost pool of four tests and is never eligible, so `match: "trait"` is proved to be exact
  and not a substring.
- Sources spread across two different Digimon (Q5114) with the exact three chosen instances
  asserted in the trash and the untouched near-miss asserted still in its stack.
- The [When Digivolving] window is proved not to fire for a copy that merely sits in a
  stack: the clauses are main-body text, not inherited text.
- Once-per-turn sharing is proved ACROSS the two timings in one turn — a real attack
  suspends the source, `digivolve` fires C4 through [When Digivolving], and the following
  attack in the same turn cannot use it again.

##### Fixtures

The previous test file used EX10-025, EX10-028 and EX10-032 as the cost pool. All three
print `When effects trash this card from a [Mineral] or [Rock] trait Digimon's digivolution
cards, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon ...` (EX10-032 prints ＜De-Digivolve 1＞). Trashing
three of them fires three inherited deletions, so the old assertion "the opponent's battle
area is empty" could not tell Magneticdramon's own deletion from the fixtures'. Every fixture
is now a card with no printed and no inherited text at all: BT10-062 ([Mineral]), BT10-064
and BT4-065 ([Rock]), BT2-011 ([Rock Dragon], near-miss), BT1-009/BT1-013/BT1-014/BT1-019,
and BT2-064 (the inert Black Lv.6 digivolution source).

#### Defect fixed locally

`allowCostWithoutTarget: true` added to both `Delete` actions in `EX10-036.ts`.

- Expected: with no opponent Digimon on the board, C3 is still usable — the deletion is the
  impossible part, and comprehensive rules §1-3-2 still performs the rest, so the cost is
  paid and the opponent's top security card is trashed.
- Actual before the fix: nothing happened. Nothing was trashed, the security stack was
  untouched. `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts` preflights every
  costed `Delete` (`action.kind === "Delete" && action.cost !== undefined &&
  action.allowCostWithoutTarget !== true && candidatePermanents(...).length === 0`) and, with
  `abortOnDecline: true`, aborts the whole action list — including the sibling
  `trashSecurityTop`. The preflight is right for a single-result clause; this clause has two
  results, so it opts out.
- Covered by `trashes the top security card even when the opponent controls no Digimon to delete`.

This changes the compiled IR, so `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` now drifts for
EX10-036. The coordinator runs the sync.

#### Open question, not a retained red

The `Unsuspend` preflight in the same file aborts a costed `Unsuspend` whose target is not
suspended. C4 is therefore never offered when Magneticdramon is already unsuspended — for
example on a plain [When Digivolving] window during your own turn after the unsuspend phase.
A player might reasonably want to pay the cost anyway, because placing 3 cards under it feeds
＜Fragment (3)＞ and C3's own cost. No ruling in the KB settles whether an effect whose only
result is a no-op may still be activated, so the behaviour is documented rather than changed
or asserted red. Every test here works with the engine's current reading and says so.

Also observed, not asserted as an order: when three cards are placed at the bottom in one
step the engine lays them in reverse selection order. Placement order among simultaneously
placed cards is the placing player's choice, so the test asserts membership of the bottom
three and the exact positions of the cards that were already in the stack.

```
     docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-036-vitest.log

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
  -> 0 errors in EX10-036.ts / EX10-036.test.ts.
     Pre-existing errors in another lane's file: EX10-039.test.ts (3x TS2322,
     `target: { kind: "digimon" }` — the intent type only accepts "player" | "permanent").
     docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-036-typecheck.log

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-036.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-036.test.ts
  -> no findings
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>   -> All matched files use the correct format.
git diff --check                            -> clean
```

#### Remaining gaps

- No comparative test against a peer that prints the same `[Mineral] or [Rock]` vocabulary
  (EX10-025, EX10-028, EX10-032). Their own effects make them unusable as inert fixtures, so
  a peer comparison needs its own scenario.
- C4 is never exercised from a Digi-Egg / breeding route; Magneticdramon is Lv.7, so the
  shortest legal breeding line is long and was not built.
- The interaction between C3's cost and its own ＜Fragment (3)＞ cost competing for the same
  digivolution cards in one window is untested.

### EX10-037 — Impmon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-037.test.ts`: Direct deck-trash watcher, level-four deletion, Start Main mill and inherited trash-count DP on a real stack.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`, base `46810fe1e`.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Purple, Digimon, Lv.3 Rookie, Virus,
[Evil], play cost 4, DP 2000, evolution cost Purple Lv.2 for 0, rarity U, max 4 per deck.

Printed clauses:

1. "When this card is trashed from the top of the deck, you may delete 1 of your opponent's
   level 4 or lower Digimon."
2. "[Start of Your Main Phase] Trash the top 2 cards of your deck."
3. Inherited: "[Your Turn] For every 10 cards in your trash, this Digimon gets +1000 DP."

No security text.

#### Q&A covered

| Q&A | Content | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5116 | The deck-trash effect fires only on a direct trash from the deck; revealing or searching the card in the deck does not fire it. | Test "Q5116: revealing this card from the deck is not a deck trash, so nothing is deleted" — BT11-046's [On Play] reveals the top 4 (Impmon among them) and bottoms them; the opponent's Lv.4 survives, the trash stays empty and Impmon stays in the deck. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR (`EX10-037.ts`) | Behavioural test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 (deck trash -> may delete Lv.4 or lower) | `AllTurns` effect holding `SubTrigger { event: "whenTrashedFromDeck", sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true } }` -> `Delete { optional: true, target: { filter: { controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon"], levelComparison: { op: "lte", value: 4 } }, count: 1 } }` | Positive: "[Start of Your Main Phase] mills 2 and the milled Impmon deletes an opponent Lv.4". Ceiling: the same board's Lv.5 (BT1-020) is never a candidate and survives; a second test with only a Lv.5 in play deletes nothing. Optional refusal: "declining the optional delete leaves the opponent's Lv.4 alive, mill still happens". Opponent's effect: "an opponent's effect that mills my deck also fires the deck-trash clause" — the opponent plays BT14-077 SkullSatamon ([On Play] trash the top 2 of BOTH decks); my Impmon is milled by THEIR effect and still deletes their Lv.4. |
| 2 ([Start of Your Main Phase] trash top 2) | `StartOfYourMainPhase` -> `TrashTopDeck { controller: "mine", amount: 2 }` | Fired by the real turn loop (`startTurnLoop` + `advance().waitForMainPhase(0)`), never by injected timing. Exact endpoints asserted: the two named deck instances are in the trash in order, the deck keeps exactly the remaining two cards, the hand is untouched (the opening turn has no draw phase), security is unchanged, no pending decision. |
| 3 (inherited [Your Turn] +1000 per 10 trash) | `YourTurn`, `isInherited: true` -> `ModifyDP { amount: 1000, duration: "permanent", target: { filter: { isSelfRef: true }, isSelf: true }, scaling: { per: 10, unit: "trash", filter: { zone: "trash", controller: "mine" } } }` | "inherited [Your Turn]: +1000 per complete group of 10 trash cards, gone on the opponent's turn": 19 trash cards give +1000 (not +1900), a 20th gives +2000, and the buff is back to base DP once the opponent's Main phase is authoritatively open through the real turn loop. Carrier built by a public route in "public digivolution route: a purple Lv.4 digivolves onto Impmon, paying 1 and drawing 1": the `digivolve` intent puts BT3-083 Meramon (purple Lv.4, no effect text, no inherited text) on top of the resident Impmon; the stack holds the exact Impmon instance, memory goes 3 -> 2 (printed cost 1) and the named top deck card reaches hand as the bonus draw. Illegal-source negative in the same test: the same Meramon onto a RED Lv.3 (BT1-009) is rejected with the source untouched, memory unchanged and the card still in hand. |

IR checks against the engine source:

- `scaling.unit: "trash"` is handled by `scaleFactor` in
  `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/scaling.ts` (case `"trash"`), which counts the
  controller's trash and floors by `per`. Matches "for every 10".
- `duration: "permanent"` is a real `EffectDurationRef`
  (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/durations.ts`). The clause is still turn-scoped because
  `turnOwnerGuard` gates `YourTurn` on `state.turnSeat === source.ownerSeat`
  (`interpreter/effect.ts:486`) and the continuous ledger is re-derived every recompute —
  proved behaviourally by the DP dropping back to base on the opponent's turn.
- `StartOfYourMainPhase` maps to `EffectTiming.OnStartMainPhase`, fired by
  `TurnStateMachine.ts:264`, so the turn loop is the production route for clause 2.

#### Defects found and fixed locally

The committed state had **no module defect**; `EX10-037.ts` is unchanged. The defects were in
the test file, which the previous audit scored 10/10:

1. **Clause 2 was proved only by injected timing.** The old test called
   `advance(s.engine).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.StartOfYourMainPhase, ...)`, which the
   brief scores 0 for behaviour. Replaced with the real turn loop.
2. **Q5116 was claimed but not tested.** The old test named Q5116 in its title while only
   exercising a normal deck trash. There was no reveal/search case at all — the exact thing
   the ruling denies. Added the BT11-046 reveal case.
3. **The inherited clause reached outside the test seam.** The old test mutated
   `s.state.turnSeat` by hand and called `s.engine.recomputeContinuousEffects()` to fake the
   turn change. Replaced with `startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase(0)` -> `endMainPhaseIfOpen(0)`
   -> `waitForMainPhase(1)`.
4. **Non-inert fixtures.** The old test used EX10-028 and EX10-030 (both effect-carrying EX10
   cards owned by other lanes) as the opponent board and as the inherited-clause host. Replaced
   with inert BT1-014 (Lv.4), BT1-020 (Lv.5) and BT1-009 / BT1-013.
5. **No scaling boundary.** The old test only checked 20 trash cards -> +2000. Added the 19 ->
   +1000 boundary, which is what distinguishes "for every 10" from "per card".
6. **No optional-refusal path and no empty-candidate path.** Both added.

#### Round-2 additions (Peer/stack 1 -> 2)

1. **Public digivolution route into the carrier.** BT3-083 Meramon (purple Lv.4, no effect text,
   no inherited text) digivolves onto the resident Impmon through the `digivolve` intent. Asserted:
   the permanent keeps its id, `stack` holds the exact Impmon instance, memory drops by the printed
   1, the named top deck card is drawn, and the inherited +2000 (20 trash) is then read off that
   carrier's `currentDP`.
2. **Illegal-source negative.** The same Meramon aimed at BT1-009 (a RED Lv.3) is rejected;
   the source keeps its top card, its stack stays empty, memory is unchanged and Meramon stays
   in hand. Meramon's only evolution cost is purple Lv.3, so the colour is what fails.
3. **Opponent-mill peer case.** BT14-077 SkullSatamon ([On Play] "Trash the top 2 cards of both
   players' decks") is played from the opponent's hand on their turn. My Impmon is milled by their
   effect and still deletes their Lv.4 — confirming the watcher carries no attribution gate, which
   matches the printed text.

#### Retained red / engine seams

None. No `it.fails` or `it.skip` in this lane; every clause passes through a public route.

#### Catalog note (reported, not edited)

The catalog effect text reads "When this card is trashed from **the top of** the deck", while
Q5116 phrases the same clause as "trashed from the deck". The engine fires
`whenTrashedFromDeck` from the `TrashTopDeck` seam only (`primitives.ts:2956`), so both
readings behave identically for every currently implemented mill. Flagging the wording for the
coordinator; no catalog edit made.

#### Remaining gaps

- Impmon's own evolution route (purple Lv.2 for 0 from a Digi-Egg, or a normal play for 4) is
  still not exercised. The carrier is now built by the route that matters for the inherited
  clause — a legal purple Lv.4 digivolving ONTO Impmon — which is the route that puts Impmon in
  a digivolution stack at all.
- Interaction with a simultaneous second milled Impmon (two watchers from one `TrashTopDeck`)
  is not covered.

#### Commands and results

```bash

  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
# Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 9 passed (9)  -> logs/EX10-037-vitest.log

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        # exit 0 -> logs/EX10-037-typecheck.log
pnpm exec oxlint  apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-037.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-037.test.ts   # clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-037.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-037.test.ts  # all files correctly formatted
git diff --check                          # clean
```

Round 1 ran vitest three times on this file only. Round 2 added the two tests above and ran the
same focused file twice (first run: 1 failure — the deleted BT1-014 also lands in the opponent's
trash, an assertion omission, not a behaviour defect; second run: 9 passed) and typecheck once.
No peer file was run.

The deck-trash clause is proved against a real peer card the opponent plays (BT14-077 SkullSatamon mills both decks), and the level filter against a mixed Lv.4/Lv.5 board. |

### EX10-038 — Copipemon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-038.test.ts`: Alternate evolution, Appmon/Leviathan reveal choice, bottom return and inherited link effect.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple Lv.3 Digimon, play cost 3, DP 1000. Forms `Stnd.`/`Appmon`, attribute `System`,
types `Copy & Paste`/`Leviathan`. Link DP 2000.

#### Printed clauses

| Id | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| C1 | `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[Appmon] trait: Cost 0` (alternate route; the printed evoCost is Purple Lv.2 cost 0) |
| C2 | `[On Play] Reveal the top 3 cards of your deck. Add 1 card with the [Appmon] trait and 1 card with the [Leviathan] trait among them to the hand. Return the rest to the bottom of the deck.` |
| C3 | `[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 1` (+2000 link DP) |
| C4 | link effect: `[When Attacking] By trashing 1 of this Digimon's link cards, you may return 1 [Appmon] trait Digimon card from your trash to the hand.` |

No inherited text and no security text.

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Question | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5117 | May the cost trash this card itself while it is a link card? | `Q5117/Q5118 returns an Appmon by trashing itself or another link card of the same host` (loop case `copipemon`), plus `returns the cost card itself when the trash holds no other [Appmon] Digimon` |
| Q5118 | With ＜Link +X＞ and several link cards, may the cost trash one of that Digimon's other link cards? | Same test, loop case `sameHost`; the neighbouring Digimon's link card is asserted untouched |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | `digivolves in breeding from an off-colour Lv.2 [Appmon] egg for 0 memory` (BT21-005 Swipemon, Green) and `refuses the 0-cost route from a Lv.2 egg without the [Appmon] trait` (BT1-007 Tanemon, Green) | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 2, traits: ["Appmon"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` |
| C2 | `On Play from hand adds one Appmon and one Leviathan and bottoms the rest` (public `playCard`, memory 3 -> 0), `adds only the Appmon card when the reveal holds no Leviathan card`, `a single dual-trait card fills one slot only` | `RevealAdd` revealCount 3, two `to: "hand"` add slots, `rest: "deckBottom"` |
| C3 | `links only to an [Appmon] Digimon for 1 memory and contributes +2000 DP` (non-Appmon host refused, memory 1 -> 0, host DP +2000) | `linkRequirement: [{ traits: ["Appmon"], cost: 1 }]` |
| C4 | `Q5117/Q5118 ...`, `declining the "you may" leaves the link cards and the trash untouched`, `returns the cost card itself ...` | `WhenAttacking` + `isLinked`, `Return` to hand from `zone: "trash"` with `kind: ["Digimon"]` and the `[Appmon]` trait filter, cost `trash` with `isSelfRef: true`, `optional`, `abortOnDecline` |

Every behavioural test drives public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `linkCard`, `attack`)
and settles before asserting. No `advance.fire*` injection is used anywhere in the file, so
every clause carries behavioural credit.

#### Catalog fact that shapes C2

Every card in the catalog carrying the `[Leviathan]` trait also carries `[Appmon]`
(checked over the whole of `cards.json`: 0 Leviathan-only cards, 90 Appmon-only cards). The
two add slots therefore always compete for the same revealed cards. The interpreter
resolves the `[Appmon]` slot first and marks the picked card as taken
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/reveal.ts`, the `taken` set), so a
controller must spend a non-Leviathan Appmon on the first slot to fill both. That is the
correct rules outcome — the slots are two separate cards — and the test steers the two
prompts with `preferInstanceIds` to prove it, while a separate test proves a lone
dual-trait card fills exactly one slot.

#### Defects found in the previous audit's test

The committed test claimed 10/10 but proved less than it appeared to:

1. C2 was proved only by `advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)`, which
   is injected timing and earns no behavioural credit. Replaced by a real `playCard` from
   hand with the memory payment and the resulting permanent asserted.
2. C1 was asserted only against the compiled IR, never through the `digivolve` intent. Now
   proved with an off-colour Lv.2 `[Appmon]` egg (the alternate route is the only legal
   route there) plus a non-Appmon negative.
3. The Q5117/Q5118 board seeded two link cards on a host with the default link maximum of
   1. The rule sweep trashes the over-cap link at the attack, so the assertion "the cost
   card is in the trash" passed even without the effect running. Rebuilt on BT26-086
   Dantemon (＜Link +6＞) so two links are legal, which turned the case into real proof.
4. No optional-refusal case and no "cost card is itself the only target" case existed.

No change was needed in `EX10-038.ts`; the compiled IR matches the printed text. Only the
test file changed.

#### Catalog discrepancy

`cards.json` `EX10-038.linkRequirement` is `"[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 1"` — a
non-breaking space (U+00A0) sits between `[Appmon]` and `trait` where every other field
uses a normal space. Cosmetic only (the compiled requirement is correct), reported and not
edited. The catalog test normalizes whitespace and states why.

#### Rules question for the coordinator

C4's cost is paid before the return resolves, so the trashed link card is itself in the
trash when the target is chosen. With no other `[Appmon]` Digimon in the trash, the engine
returns the just-paid Copipemon to the hand — the net effect is "pay nothing, return
nothing but this card". Q5117 says the card may pay for itself, so this looks right, but no
ruling in the KB states whether the return may pick the card that just paid. Recorded as
passing behaviour with the reasoning in the test comment.

Related engine note (no test retained red): `runAction.ts` preflights an empty target pool
only for a battle-area `Return` — the `returnsLooseCard` branch deliberately skips the
preflight when the filter names another zone. For this card the skip is harmless because
the cost always supplies a legal target, but a trash-sourcing return whose cost does not
feed the trash can still consume its cost for nothing.

```bash
# EX10-038.ts or EX10-038.test.ts.                    logs/EX10-038-typecheck.log
```

`logs/EX10-038-run1.log` … `run4.log` (the failures that exposed the link-sweep and
catalog issues).

#### Remaining gaps

- The `[Appmon]` add slot resolving before the `[Leviathan]` slot is proved with steered
  prompts. A controller who answers the first slot with the dual-trait card fills only one
  slot; the engine offers no guidance. This is a UI concern, not an engine defect.
- No test covers a deck of fewer than 3 cards at `[On Play]` (`fx.reveal` returns what it
  has); the reveal count boundary was judged out of scope for this card.
- Link-effect proof uses a seeded link board. The `linkCard` intent is exercised separately
  in the C3 test, but not chained into the same scenario as C4.

### EX10-039 — ChuuChuumon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-039.test.ts`: Start Main Bagra Army placement under Digimon/Tamer, Save, inherited Draw through public source trash and host negative.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple Lv.3 Rookie, Virus, [Beast]/[Bagra Army]. Play cost 4, DP 1000,
digivolve from Purple Lv.2 for 0. Rarity C, max 4 in deck. No security effect.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|---|
| C1 | `[Start of Your Main Phase]` You may place 1 Digimon card with the [Bagra Army] trait from your hand or trash as any of your [Bagra Army] trait Digimon's bottom digivolution cards or under any of your [Bagra Army] trait Tamers. |
| C2 | `[On Deletion]` ＜Save＞ |
| C3 | Inherited: When effects trash this card from a [Bagra Army] trait Digimon's digivolution cards, ＜Draw 1＞ |

C1 is optional ("You may"), has two source zones (hand, trash), one card
selection filter ([Bagra Army] **Digimon card**) and a two-branch destination
filter (own [Bagra Army] Digimon **or** own [Bagra Army] Tamer). Both branches
place at the **bottom**: the Digimon branch says so in print, the Tamer branch by
Q5119.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Question | Where proved |
|---|---|---|
| Q5119 | Placing under a Tamer that already has cards under it — what order? Answer: the new card goes to the **bottom**. | `Q5119: the start-of-main placement lands at a [Bagra Army] Tamer's true bottom` — a Tamer seeded with two cards under it, exact resulting order asserted. |

Q5119 also settles the ＜Save＞ ordering question, so the same rule is asserted
again for C2 with two cards already under the Tamer.

#### Defect found and fixed

The committed module compiled C2 (＜Save＞) **without** `position: "bottom"`.
`runPlaceUnder` reads `belowTop: action.position !== "bottom"`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/placeUnder.ts:340`), so a
positionless ＜Save＞ tucks the saved card directly beneath the Tamer instead of at
the bottom of its stack. That contradicts Q5119 and CR 4-3, and it disagreed with
the accepted shape in the same set (`EX10-026.ts`, `EX10-027.ts`).

The previous test could not see the defect: it saved onto a Tamer with an **empty**
stack, where both positions produce the same result.

Fix: `position: "bottom"` on the `OnDeletion` `PlaceUnder`, with the rules
citation in a comment, matching EX10-026 / EX10-027.

Mutation check (`logs/EX10-039-mutation.log`): removing that one key again makes
the behavioural ＜Save＞ test fail with
`['u1','u2','chuu']` instead of `['chuu','u1','u2']` — the saved card lands
closest to the Tamer, not at the bottom. The IR-shape test fails too.

C1 already carried `position: "bottom"` and was correct.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
|---|---|---|
| C1 destination = [Bagra Army] Tamer, bottom | `Q5119: ... true bottom` (real turn loop) | `StartOfYourMainPhase` -> `PlaceUnder`, `underFilter.or[1]` = Tamer + `trait` |
| C1 destination = [Bagra Army] Digimon, bottom digivolution card, source = hand | `places under a [Bagra Army] Digimon as its bottom digivolution card, from hand` | `underFilter.or[0]` = Digimon + `trait`, `target.from = ["hand","trash"]` |
| C1 card filter is [Bagra Army] **Digimon card** | same two tests: a non-[Bagra Army] trash card stays in the trash and no card selection is even raised | `target.filter.kind = ["Digimon"]`, `nameOrTrait match:"trait"` |
| C1 destination filter negatives | both tests: a non-[Bagra Army] Tamer and a non-[Bagra Army] Digimon are on the board with empty stacks; the recorded `chooseTargets` candidate list is exactly the two [Bagra Army] permanents | `underFilter.controller = "mine"` + `or` |
| C1 optionality | `declines the optional placement and leaves every zone untouched` (`autoDeclineOptional`) | `optional: true` |
| C2 ＜Save＞, bottom of an occupied Tamer stack | `＜Save＞ puts the battle-deleted ChuuChuumon at the true bottom of the Tamer's stack` — real battle deletion (1000 DP attacking into 20000 DP), Tamer seeded with two cards | `OnDeletion` -> `PlaceUnder` `isSelf` + `position: "bottom"` |
| C2 optionality and no-Tamer fallback | `＜Save＞ declined, and with no Tamer the deleted card goes to the trash` | `optional: true`, `underFilter kind:["Tamer"]` |
| C3 inherited draw, public route | `public route: the opponent's BT15-019 trashes ChuuChuumon from my [Bagra Army] host and I draw 1` — seat 1 plays BT15-019 Crabmon through the `playCard` intent on its own turn under the real turn loop | `Static` `isInherited` -> `SubTrigger onDigivolutionCardsDiscardedBatch` |
| C3 host-trait gate, negative | `public route negative: the same BT15-019 trash off a non-[Bagra Army] host draws nothing` — identical Crabmon play, BT1-009 [Mini Dragon] host: the card moves, no draw | `hostFilter` `nameOrTrait match:"trait"` |
| C3 host gate, structural companion | `structural: the inherited watcher draws only when effects trash it from a [Bagra Army] host` (Advance Surface verb, positive on EX10-026, negative on BT1-009) — kept, earns no behavioural credit | same |
| Catalog and compiled shape | `records the exact catalog and printed text`, `compiles both placements to bottom, ...` | whole `CompiledCard` |

Peer/stack evidence: `peer interaction: BT10-093 sees the purple card placed under
it and draws 1`. BT10-093 Yuu Amano prints "[All Turns][Once Per Turn] When a purple
card is placed under this Tamer, ＜Draw 1＞ and gain 1 memory". Every [Bagra Army]
Digimon card in the catalog is purple or black, so C1's placement always arms that
peer trigger; the test asserts the peer's draw actually lands.

#### Fixtures

Real routes only, no injected timing:

- C1 fires through `s.engine.startTurnLoop()` + `advance().waitForMainPhase(0)`, i.e.
  the production `OnStartMainPhase` window, not `advance().fireForPermanent`.
  The committed test used `fireForPermanent` and earned no behavioural credit.
- C2 fires through a real `attack` intent into a 20000 DP suspended wall.
- C3 fires through seat 1's public `playCard` of BT15-019 Crabmon on seat 1's own turn,
  reached with `startTurnLoop()` + `waitForMainPhase(0)` -> `endMainPhaseIfOpen(0)` ->
  `waitForMainPhase(1)`. Crabmon compiles to `TrashDigivolution` with `fromTop: false`,
  so it trashes the BOTTOM digivolution card — this ChuuChuumon — of seat 0's
  [Bagra Army] host, which is exactly the event `trashDigivolutionCards` emits.
  Host: BT14-057 ChuuChuumon (Black/Purple Lv.3, [Bagra Army]); its only main text is
  `[On Deletion] ＜Save＞`, so it is inert while it sits on top of the stack. A second,
  inert digivolution card (BT1-009) sits above the source, so the host keeps a
  digivolution card after the trash and Crabmon's own conditional "then" draw stays off.
- Inert filler: BT1-009 / BT1-013 / BT1-014 as stack and deck cards.
  BT7-086 Tommy Himi is the non-[Bagra Army] Tamer negative — chosen because its
  only text is `[On Play]`, so it stays inert on the board.

Fixture trap worth recording for other lanes: BT12-094 Yuu Amano was the first
choice for the "plain Tamer" negative, but it prints
`[Start of Your Main Phase] By placing 1 Digimon card with ＜Save＞ in its text from
your hand under this Tamer, gain 1 memory`. Under a real turn loop it steals any
＜Save＞ card the turn draw puts in hand and quietly fills its own stack. A Tamer used
as an inert negative must have no start-of-turn or start-of-main text.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-039.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Tests 11 passed (11)                     logs/EX10-039-vitest.log

(mutation: position:"bottom" removed from the OnDeletion PlaceUnder)
  -> Tests 2 failed | 7 passed (9)            logs/EX10-039-mutation.log

(mutation: hostFilter trait token "Bagra Army" -> "Mineral")
  -> Tests 3 failed | 8 passed (11)           logs/EX10-039-mutation-hostfilter.log
     The public-route test fails with it, so the new proof is not vacuous.

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
  -> clean                                    logs/EX10-039-typecheck.log
     (the earlier EX10-043 error from another lane is gone)

pnpm exec oxlint <both files>        -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <both files> -> clean
git diff --check                     -> clean
```

#### Catalog discrepancies

`cards.json` stores a **non-breaking space** (U+00A0) after every `[Bagra Army]`
token in both `effectText` (offsets 77, 135, 211) and `inheritedEffectText`
(offset 48): `"[Bagra Army] trait"`. The official text uses an ordinary
space. It renders identically and does not affect the compiled IR (trait matching
reads `types`, not the text), but any future text-based tooling — a text-to-IR
generator, a `nameOrTrait match:"text"` filter, a diff against the official list —
will mismatch on it. The test asserts the catalog exactly, U+00A0 included, so the
quirk is pinned rather than hidden. Not fixed here: `cards.json` is out of lane.
The quirk is present in `HEAD` (base 46810fe), so it is pre-existing. `cards.json`
is modified in this worktree by another lane, but EX10-039's entry is
byte-identical to `HEAD` — the assertions above are not reading someone else's
uncommitted edit.

#### Remaining gaps

- **C3 now has a public route (round 3).** Earlier rounds could find no public card
  that trashes a chosen digivolution card of one of the controller's **own**
  [Bagra Army] Digimon, and fell back to `advance(engine).verb.trashDigivolutionCards`.
  The route is on the opponent's side: BT15-019 Crabmon,
  `[On Play] Trash the bottom digivolution card of 1 of your opponent's Digimon`,
  compiled as `TrashDigivolution` with `fromTop: false`. Seat 1 plays it with the
  public `playCard` intent; `trashDigivolutionCards`
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:2769, 2883`) emits
  `onDigivolutionCardsDiscardedBatch` and seat 0's inherited clause draws. ＜De-Digivolve＞
  is still not a route: `peelStackTops` trashes the current TOP card and fires
  `whenDigimonTopTrashed`. BT1-099 Hearts Attack is a second public route, not used here.
  The Advance Surface test is kept as a structural companion, clearly labelled.
- `underFilter.or` branches do not carry `excludeToken: true` (the ＜Save＞ branch
  does). No implemented token has the [Bagra Army] trait, so nothing is observably
  wrong today; worth normalising if a [Bagra Army] token ever ships.
- C1's own-self case is proved only incidentally: the recorded candidate list shows
  ChuuChuumon itself is a legal host (it is a [Bagra Army] Digimon), and steering
  had to be added to avoid it. There is no dedicated assertion that a card placed
  under ChuuChuumon itself lands at its bottom.

| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a compiled action; a real IR defect found by following `runPlaceUnder`'s `belowTop` read, fixed, and pinned by a mutation check. |
| Behavior | 2 | All three clauses proved end-to-end through public intents under the real turn loop: C1 through the production start-of-main window, C2 through a real battle deletion, C3 through seat 1's public `playCard` of BT15-019 Crabmon on the opponent's turn, with a non-[Bagra Army] host negative and a host-trait mutation check that kills the test. Optional-refusal and no-Tamer branches covered. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Bottom placement asserted against a pre-seeded two-card stack in three scenarios; peer Tamer BT10-093's own purple-placement trigger asserted; destination candidate set asserted against a mixed board of matching and near-matching Tamers and Digimon; the ＜Save＞ shape aligned with EX10-026 / EX10-027. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

### EX10-040 — DemiDevimon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-040.test.ts`: Conditional mill/memory threshold, inherited once-per-turn dual-deck mill.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Purple Lv.3 Digimon, play cost 3,
DP 1000, digivolve from Purple Lv.2 for 0, form Rookie, attribute Virus, type Evil,
rarity C, max 4. No security text, not an ACE, no overflow.

Printed clauses:

1. Main: `[Start of Your Main Phase] If your opponent has 10 or fewer cards in their
   trash, trash the top 2 cards of both players' decks. Then, if they have 10 or more
   cards in their trash, gain 1 memory.`
   Two independently gated steps. The second gate is read after the first step resolves,
   and it is read against the OPPONENT's trash ("they"), not the controller's.
2. Inherited: `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] Trash the top card of both players' decks.`

No catalog discrepancy found: stored text matches the official wording, and every stored
field matches the printed card. `securityEffectText` is absent, as printed.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5120 | Opponent has 8 in trash: mill runs, and the "then" part is still processed | `Q5120: 8 opposing trash cards …` |
| Q5121 | Opponent has 11 in trash: mill is skipped, but the "then" part is still processed | `Q5121: 11 opposing trash cards …` |

Both rulings turn on the same point: the two steps are separate processing conditions.
Q5120 also fixes that the second gate is evaluated after the mill (8 + 2 = 10 qualifies as
"10 or more"), and Q5121 fixes that a failed first gate does not abort the clause.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog fields and both printed texts | `records the exact catalog and both printed clauses` | catalog only |
| Both gates on SEPARATE actions | `carries both clauses as IR with the two conditions on SEPARATE actions` | `StartOfYourMainPhase` -> `[TrashTopDeck(both,2, cond lte 10), GainMemory(1, cond gte 10)]` |
| Mill 2 from both decks, then gain 1 memory (8 -> 10) | `Q5120 …` | both actions |
| Mill skipped, memory still gained (11) | `Q5121 …` | second action alone |
| "10 or fewer" includes exactly 10 | `boundary: exactly 10 opposing trash cards still mills` | `op: "lte", value: 10` |
| "10 or more" excludes 9 | `boundary: 7 opposing trash cards mills but does NOT reach 10` | `op: "gte", value: 10` |
| `[Start of YOUR Main Phase]` is turn-scoped | `does not fire on the opponent's turn` | trigger `StartOfYourMainPhase` |
| Main text does not apply from under a host | `the main clause is main text only` | `isInherited` absent on the main effect |
| Printed Purple Lv.3 digivolution route, cost and stack identity | `reaches the stack through the real digivolve route …` | catalog `evoCosts` |
| Illegal source refused | `refuses an illegal digivolution source` | catalog `evoCosts` |
| Inherited `[When Attacking]` mills 1 from both decks | `inherited [When Attacking] mills 1 from both decks …` | inherited effect |
| `[Once Per Turn]` resets on the next own turn | same test, second turn through the turn loop | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |

All timing proof runs through public intents and the real turn loop
(`s.engine.startTurnLoop()` plus `advance(...).waitForMainPhase(seat)`, `attack`,
`digivolve`, `endPhase`, `surrender`). No `advance.fire` / `fireSubTrigger` / `fireTiming`
call remains in the file; the previous test drove every clause through
`advance(...).fireForPermanent`, which earns no behavioural credit under this brief.

Fixtures are inert throughout: BT1-009 Monodramon and BT1-013 Muchomon (no text at all)
for decks, trash, security and hand; BT1-014 Kokatorimon (inert Lv.4) as the seeded host;
BT4-082 Dobermon (Purple Lv.4, 7000 DP, no main, inherited or security text) as the real
digivolution target. The previous test used EX10-041 Wizardmon as the host, which prints
`[On Play] [When Digivolving]` and a deck/security trash watcher — a fixture that can
interfere with a mill test.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-040.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
-> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 11 passed (11)
   (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-040-vitest.log)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
-> exits 2, but every error is in another lane's file (EX10-036, EX10-039). No error in
   EX10-040.ts or EX10-040.test.ts.
   (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-040-typecheck-final.log)

pnpm exec oxlint <both files>   -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <both>  -> clean
git diff --check                -> clean
```

Budget note: typecheck ran three times, not the two the brief allows. The third run was
needed because the last two tests were added after the second run; the alternative was
shipping unverified types.

#### Defects and gaps

- No defect in `EX10-040.ts`. The IR already models both gates as separate per-action
  conditions, and the interpreter honours that: `runAction` returns `false` for a failed
  gate (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts`, the "Per-action
  gate" block), and the action loop in
  `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/effect.ts` treats that return as "do not abort",
  so a later action still runs. Q5121 passes for the right reason. The module is unchanged.
- Defect in the previous test, fixed here: every clause was proved with
  `advance(...).fireForPermanent`, an injected timing. Nothing showed the trigger fires
  from the real turn loop, that it is scoped to the controller's own Main phase, that the
  main clause does not apply from under a host, or that the digivolution route and cost are
  real. All are now covered.
- Retained red: none.
- Remaining gap: the `[Once Per Turn]` SAME-TURN refusal on the inherited clause is not
  asserted. A second attack in one turn needs the attacker unsuspended, and no inert
  fixture card can do that; adding an unsuspend card would put another effect in the mill
  path. The gate is proved by its reset through the real turn loop (attack, hand the turn
  over, attack again on the next own turn), and by the IR `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`.
- Not applicable: security effect, ACE / Overflow, DigiXros, Link, ＜Save＞, ＜De-Digivolve＞,
  Digi-Egg route. This card prints none of them.
- Deck-out interaction is untested: nothing was checked for a mill that runs into an empty
  deck. `TrashTopDeck` reveals up to `amount` and trashes what it gets, so the clause
  itself cannot fault, but the loss condition is engine-owned and out of this lane.
