| ------ | ---- | -- |
| 1 (alternate digivolve) | "digivolves for cost 2 off a Lv.3 [Evil] source…" (public `digivolve` with `useAlternateCost: true` from P-017; memory 5 -> 3 proves the reduced route, not `{ok:true}`); "refuses the alternate route from a Lv.3 source without the [Evil] trait" | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 3, traits: ["Evil"], cost: 2, isAlternate: true }]` |
| 2 (deck half, positive) | "Q5122 positive: an effect that mills it straight off the deck…" — public `playCard` of P-017, whose whole text is `[On Play]` trash top 2 | `AllTurns` -> `SubTrigger whenTrashedFromDeck` with `sourceFilter.isSelfRef` -> `GainKeyword SecurityAttack -1` |
| 2 (self-scoping) | "the watcher is self-scoped: milling OTHER cards while it sits in the deck grants nothing" | `sourceFilter.isSelfRef` |
| 2 (deck half, Q5122 negative) | "Q5122 negative: being revealed by a search and then trashed…" — public `playCard` of BT3-051 (reveal 3, add a Lv.5 and a Lv.6, trash the rest); Wizardmon reaches the trash and grants nothing | The `RevealAdd` `rest: "trash"` seam (`interpreter/actions/reveal.ts` `disposeRest`) never calls `fireWhenTrashedFromDeck`, so the ruling holds by construction |
| 2 (security half, positive) | "pays the On Play security cost, mills 2…" — the trashed top security card is a second Wizardmon, so an effect trashes it from the security stack and it grants exactly one opposing Digimon ＜Security A. -1＞ | `OnDiscardSecurity` -> `GainKeyword SecurityAttack -1` |
| 2 (security half, Q5122 negative) | "Q5122 negative: an ordinary security check that trashes it…" — real turn loop, seat 1 attacks the player, the Wizardmon security card is checked and trashed, the attacker keeps its strike count | `EffectTiming.OnDiscardSecurity` is fired only from `GameEngine.fireDiscardedFromSecurity`, reached only from the effect-driven trash seams in `effects/primitives.ts` |
| 2 (duration + consumed endpoint) | "＜Security A. -1＞ lasts \"until their turn ends\"" — grant on seat 0's turn survives seat 0's turn end, is still present in seat 1's Main, a base-1 attacker at -1 checks **no** security, and the grant is gone by seat 0's next Main | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` -> `EffectDuration.UntilOpponentTurnEnd` |
| 3 (cost, mill, DP) | "pays the On Play security cost, mills 2, and gives EVERY opposing Digimon -3000 DP" — public `playCard`, memory 8 -> 3, exact security/deck/trash instance ids, both opposing Digimon debuffed | `OnPlay`/`WhenDigivolving` -> `CostGatedBlock { cost: trash top security, optional, abortOnDecline }` -> `TrashTopDeck 2` + `ModifyDP -3000 count:"all"` |
| 3 (refusal) | "declining the security cost aborts the whole block" — security, deck and trash untouched, DP unchanged | `optional: true, abortOnDecline: true` |
| 3 (When Digivolving) | proved on the public alternate-digivolve route above (security trashed, 2 milled, victim 9000 -> 6000) | `WhenDigivolving` copy of the same block |
| 3 (duration) | "the -3000 DP is \"for the turn\"" — public play, then `advance.runTurn(0)`; the victim is back to 9000 | `duration: "forTheTurn"` -> `UntilEachTurnEnd` |
| 4 (inherited Barrier) | "grants ＜Barrier＞ to a host built by real digivolutions" — P-017 -> Wizardmon (alternate route) -> BT10-079; Barrier absent while Wizardmon is the top card, present once it is a digivolution card, absent on a control Digimon | `Static` effect with `isInherited: true`, `keywords: [Barrier]` |

#### What changed

The previous test file proved `[On Play]`, `[When Digivolving]` and the deck watcher with
injected timing (`advance.fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)`,
`advance.verb.trash(…)`), which the brief scores 0 in the Behavior column, and it proved
clause 1 and clause 4 only as seeded IR / a hand-laid `under:` stack. It also covered
neither half of Q5122's negative.

The rewritten file drives every clause through public intents: `playCard`, `digivolve`
(with `useAlternateCost`), `attack`, and the real `startTurnLoop()` / `runTurn`. No
`advance.fire*` call remains. The only Advance-Surface calls left are the turn drivers
(`waitForMainPhase`, `endMainPhaseIfOpen`, `runTurn`), which the brief treats as the real
turn loop. Nothing reaches `engine.continuous`, `engine.ledger` or `engine.primitives`;
all reads go through `observe()`.

#### Defects found

None in the module. Three things I expected to be wrong and proved were not:

1. **Duration of clause 2.** Reading `ContinuousEffectLedger.sweep` in isolation
   (`keywordGrants` expires via `ownerSeatOfPermanent`, i.e. the RECIPIENT's controller)
   suggested `untilOpponentTurnEnd` on a grant placed on an opponent's Digimon would clear
   at the controller's own turn end, which would make the clause useless. The behavioural
   test through the real turn loop shows the opposite: the grant survives seat 0's turn
   end, is live during seat 1's whole turn, and is gone at seat 0's next Main. The sweep
   seat is the seat whose turn just ended and the ledger's naming is controller-relative
   after all. `untilOpponentTurnEnd` is correct and matches the repo convention
   (BT21-043, BT22-031, BT23-025, BT23-050 all encode "until their turn ends" the same way).
2. **Q5122 by construction.** The module comment claims both clauses are effect-only by
   construction. Verified: `fireWhenTrashedFromDeck` has exactly three call sites
   (`actions/resources.ts` TrashTopDeck, `actions/removal.ts` deck-zone trash, `costs.ts`
   deck cost), all direct-from-deck; `RevealAdd`'s `rest: "trash"` disposal calls none of
   them. `OnDiscardSecurity` is fired per instance from `fireDiscardedFromSecurity`, which
   only the effect-driven security-trash seams call. Both negatives now assert this
   behaviourally rather than trusting the comment.
3. **`OnDiscardSecurity` scoping.** It fires via `fireTimingForInstance`, so it is
   self-scoped; it cannot fire off another player's or another card's security trash.

#### Remaining gaps

- No test for the zero-opponent-Digimon case of clause 2 (a mandatory "give 1" with no
  legal recipient). Low risk: `resolvePermanentTargets` returns an empty set and the
  action is a no-op.
- The Q5122 negative for the *security* half uses an ordinary security check. I found no
  implemented card that reveals cards from a security stack and trashes them, so the
  "revealed from the security stack" wording is covered by the check path plus the
  construction argument above, not by a reveal-from-security effect.

pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same files> -> all matched files use the correct format
git diff --check -> clean

  -> 1 error, and it is NOT in this lane:
     src/cards/EX10/EX10-043.test.ts(118,45): TS2339 Property 'permanentId' does not

### EX10-042 — GulusGammamon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-042.test.ts`: Gammamon evolution, mill/placement, Regulusmon reduction, turn/quota gate and inherited Raid.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Branch `audit-ex10-reaudit`.

#### Card summary

Lv.4 Purple/Red Virus Dragonkin, DP 5000, play cost 5, evoCosts Purple Lv.3 / Red Lv.3 for 3.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] [Gammamon]: Cost 2` — alternate digivolution requirement.
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Trash the top 2 cards of your deck. Then, you may place 1
   Digimon card with [Gammamon] in its name from your trash as this Digimon's bottom
   digivolution card.`
3. `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When effects add to this Digimon's digivolution cards, this
   Digimon may digivolve into [Regulusmon] in the hand or trash with the digivolution cost
   reduced by 1.`
4. Inherited: `＜Raid＞`.

#### Q&A ids covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-042` returns "(no knowledge-base entries)", which matches
KB-INDEX.md (`| EX10-042 | 0 | None |`). No Q&A ids to cover. The rules query used for the
`[Once Per Turn]` question was `node tools/kb/query.mjs rules "Once Per Turn"` (comprehensive
§15-14-1: each activation counts toward one use).

#### Catalog discrepancy (reported, not edited)

`cards.json` `effectText` for EX10-042 reads
"...1 Digimon card with [Gammamon] in its name **in its name** from your trash...". The
phrase is duplicated. Cosmetic only — no compiled behavior reads the string — but the catalog
disagrees with the official English text. The test pins the current string so the fix is
noticed when the catalog is corrected.

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Digivolve] [Gammamon]: Cost 2` | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ names: ["Gammamon"], cost: 2, isAlternate: true }]` | "over [Gammamon] for cost 2" (real `digivolve` intent, `useAlternateCost`, memory 5 → 3, not 5 → 2 for the printed route); "refuses an illegal digivolution source" (green Lv.3 base and the alternate route from a non-Gammamon red Lv.3 base, both `invalid-evolution`) |
| `[On Play]` mill 2 | `OnPlay` → `TrashTopDeck { amount: 2 }` | "[On Play]: mills 2 ..." — played from hand with the public `playCard` intent, exact trash and deck contents asserted |
| `[On Play]` place 1 `[Gammamon]`-named Digimon from trash as the BOTTOM digivolution card | `PlaceUnder { position: "bottom", optional: true, filter: zone trash / kind Digimon / nameOrTrait "Gammamon" match "name" }` | same test (stack is `[LM-016, EX10-042]`, bottom-first) plus "places nothing when the trash holds no [Gammamon]-named Digimon" (no prompt, empty stack, watcher never fires) |
| `[When Digivolving]` same body | `WhenDigivolving` duplicate of the `OnPlay` action list | "over [Gammamon] for cost 2" (stack `[BT8-008, LM-016]` under GulusGammamon) |
| `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When effects add ... may digivolve into [Regulusmon] ... cost reduced by 1` | `YourTurn` + `SubTrigger { event: "onAddDigivolutionCards", sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true } }` → `Digivolve { into nameExact Regulusmon, from ["hand","trash"], payCost, reduceCost: 1, optional }`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` | "[On Play]: ... then digivolves into Regulusmon for 1 less" (accepted: BT21-077 from hand, printed route cost 4 → 3 paid, memory 9 − 5 − 3 = 1, bonus draw taken); "the Regulusmon digivolve may be declined" (declined from trash: nothing paid, Regulusmon still in trash); `isSelfRef` proved by a second EX10-042 on the board that neither gains cards nor digivolves |
| `[Once Per Turn]` (same gate) | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` → `oncePerTurnKey` on the installed watcher | "[Once Per Turn]: a second same-turn placement offers nothing, and the next own turn resets it" — three effect-driven placements under the SAME GulusGammamon through public `playCard` intents (round 3, see below) |
| `[Your Turn]` (same gate) | `trigger: "YourTurn"` → `withSubTriggerTurnScope` on the installed watcher | "[Your Turn]: an opponent's effect placing cards under it on their turn offers nothing" — BT11-088 Bagramon buries one of my Digimon under GulusGammamon on the OPPONENT's turn; no offer opens |
| Inherited `＜Raid＞` | `Static` inherited effect with `keywords: [{ keyword: "Raid" }]` | "[On Play]" test: after the real placement + digivolve route, the Regulusmon host with EX10-042 in its stack has `Raid`; the standalone copy does not |

`[Your Turn]` and `[Once Per Turn]` are carried from the effect header onto the installed
watcher by `withSubTriggerTurnScope` / `withSubTriggerFrequency`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/effect.ts:398`), so the IR needs no explicit
`turnScope`. That is IR/engine-trace evidence, not behavioural evidence.

#### Defect fixed locally

`EX10-042.ts` — the `[Regulusmon]` reference used `match: "name"`, which is a SUBSTRING
match (`matching/definition.ts:352`). A bracketed `[Name]` is exact, so it is now
`match: "nameExact"`. No card in the current catalog is named `<x>Regulusmon`, so this is a
latent-only correctness fix with IR evidence and no behavioural difference today. The four
`Regulusmon` printings (BT21-077, EX10-053, LM-017, RB1-030) all still match.

The neighbouring "with `[Gammamon]` in its name" clause correctly keeps `match: "name"`
(substring), which is what lets GulusGammamon / BetelGammamon / KausGammamon qualify.

#### Checked and found correct (previous-audit claims verified, not assumed)

- The inner `Digivolve` action's `optional: true` IS honoured: `runAction.ts:560-590` prompts
  generically for `action.optional` on every action kind except `SubTrigger`/`Replacement`,
  and preflights with `canAttemptDigivolve`, so no prompt is opened when no Regulusmon is
  reachable. Proved by the accept and decline paths.
- `sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true }` on an `onAddDigivolutionCards` watcher gates on the
  RECEIVER permanent through `subjectMatchesFilter`, the same subject `triggerFilter` uses
  (`actions/subTrigger.ts:802-826`). The two-copy board proves it behaviourally.
- No `byEffect: true` is needed on the watcher: `onAddDigivolutionCards` is only fired from
  `primitives.ts` `placeUnder` / `placeOwnTopAtStackBottom`, both effect-driven. Ordinary
  digivolution does not fire the bus, so "when EFFECTS add" is already exact.

#### Round 3: the `[Once Per Turn]` and `[Your Turn]` gates, proved behaviourally

The round-2 report closed both gates as structural-only. Round 3 closes them with public
intents. No new card module was written; the two gates are driven by cards that already
exist and already compile.

##### Catalog search for a second public source of placement

The coordinator's suggestions do not reach this card. Every EX10 placer named in the brief
restricts its RECEIVER to something GulusGammamon (Purple/Red Virus Dragonkin) is not:

| Card | Receiver it can place under | Reaches EX10-042? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EX10-025 Sunarizamon | "1 of your [Mineral] or [Rock] trait Digimon" | no |
| EX10-032 Proganomon | "any of your [Sunarizamon]" | no |
| EX10-044 Damemon | "under any of your Tamers" | no |
| EX10-066 Akihiro Kurata | "your Digimon with [Belphemon] in their names" | no |
| EX10-067 Ryoma Mogami | a Digimon that just digivolved into one with ＜Save＞ | no |

A full-catalog scan of `cards.json` for placement clauses (`place ... as/under ... digivolution
card`) whose receiver filter is unrestricted produced three usable ones:

- **BT9-109 X Antibody** (Option, cost 0) — "[Main] Place this card under 1 of your Digimon
  without [X Antibody] in its digivolution cards as its bottom digivolution card." Its own
  `[Static]` waives the colour requirement while you have a Digimon in play. Usable ONCE per
  host, since the second copy is excluded by its own placement.
- **EX6-065 Mythical Arms of Salvation!** (Option, cost 3, Red/Black) — "[Main] You may place
  1 Digimon card with the [Legend-Arms] trait from your trash as 1 of your Digimon's bottom
  digivolution card." Receiver unrestricted. GulusGammamon covers only the red half of its
  colour requirement, so the fixture keeps an inert P-097 Zubamon ([Legend-Arms]) on the board
  to satisfy the card's own waiver.
- **BT11-088 Bagramon** (Lv.6, cost 12) — "[On Play] ... place 1 of your opponent's Digimon
  under 1 of your opponent's other Digimon as its bottom digivolution card." This is the
  opponent-side placement the `[Your Turn]` negative needs: an OPPONENT effect adding a
  digivolution card to MY Digimon on THEIR turn.

So the rubric-ceiling policy is NOT needed for this card; both gates are exercised for real.

##### `[Once Per Turn]` — same-turn refusal and next-own-turn reset

`it("[Once Per Turn]: a second same-turn placement offers nothing, and the next own turn
resets it")` runs the real turn loop with GulusGammamon already on the board:

1. `playCard` BT9-109 → its mandatory `[Main]` placement lands under GulusGammamon → the
   watcher fires → the Regulusmon offer opens → **declined**. Stack `["BT9-109"]`, top card
   still EX10-042.
2. `playCard` EX6-065 → P-097 Zubamon is placed from the trash under the SAME Digimon.
   Stack grows to `["BT9-109", "P-097"]`, so the `onAddDigivolutionCards` event definitely
   fired a second time — and **no second `digivolve` prompt is opened**. The test asserts the
   absence over `s.decisions` sliced from before the second play, not merely a quiet
   `pendingDecision`.
3. `endPhase` → opponent's turn → back to my Main through `advance().waitForMainPhase(0)`.
4. `playCard` the second EX6-065 → ST13-02 Zubamon lands → **the offer is back**, and is
   declined again. Stack `["BT9-109", "P-097", "ST13-02"]`.

Why a declined offer still spends the use: the SubTrigger action itself carries no `optional`
and no `cost`, so `subTrigger.ts:1174`'s decline path never runs; the decline happens on the
INNER `Digivolve` in `runAction.ts:761`, which sets `oncePerTurnActivationDeclined` only when
the action sets `preserveOncePerTurnOnDecline`. This IR does not, so `subtriggers.ts:588`
keeps the provisional `markFired`. That is the rules-correct reading of comprehensive
§15-14-1: the watcher activated, and the player declined the digivolution it offered.

##### `[Your Turn]` — the opponent-turn negative

`it("[Your Turn]: an opponent's effect placing cards under it on their turn offers nothing")`
runs the turn loop to seat 1's Main, then seat 1 plays BT11-088 Bagramon. Its `[On Play]`
buries my inert BT1-009 under GulusGammamon (steered with `preferInstanceIds`). The stack
grows to `["BT1-009"]` and **no `digivolve` prompt is opened**.

Bagramon's cost of 12 pushes the gauge past 0, so the turn passes as soon as the resolution
finishes. The test therefore records the turn seat AT the `cardsMoved` event through the
harness `onEvent` hook and asserts the placement of that exact instance happened while
`turnSeat === 1`, rather than reading `state.turnSeat` afterwards.

#### Remaining gaps

- None on the printed clauses. Both turn gates are now behavioural.
- Engine wart (not this card's defect, no test): `primitives.ts:2075` reports
  `addedDigivolutionCardsPosition: opts?.belowTop ? "bottom" : "top"`, while the same call
  puts a default (`belowTop` false) placement at the stack BOTTOM via `unshiftOnStack`. The
  label looks inverted. EX10-042 does not read that field, but cards using
  `addedDigivolutionCardsPosition` would. Flagged for the coordinator.

#### Q&A ids covered

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[On Play]` delete 1 opposing Lv.3 | `[On Play] played from hand…` (natural `playCard`), `[On Play] with no opposing level-3 Digimon…` | `trigger: "OnPlay"` → `Delete` with `filter.levels: [3]`, `controller: "opponent"`, `count: 1` |
| `[When Digivolving]` delete 1 opposing Lv.3 | `[When Digivolving] over a Purple Lv.3…` (natural `digivolve` from ST6-05), plus the illegal-source negative | `trigger: "WhenDigivolving"` → same `Delete` |
| `[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 2`, link DP | `links only onto an [Appmon] host…` | `compiled.linkRequirement = [{ traits: ["Appmon"], cost: 2 }]`, catalog `linkDp: 3000` |
| Link `[When Attacking]` cost + deletion | `Q5123…`, `Q5125…`, `declining the link effect…`, `the link effect cannot reach a level-5 Digimon…` | `isLinked: true`, `trigger: "WhenAttacking"` → `Delete` with `levelComparison { op: "lte", value: 4 }`, `cost.kind: "trash"` over `zone: "linked"` + `isSelfRef: true`, `optional`, `abortOnDecline` |
| `[All Turns]` gain 1 memory on effect link trash | `gains 1 memory when an effect trashes its own link card, through the natural attack window` | `trigger: "AllTurns"` → `SubTrigger` on `whenLinkTrashed`, `sourceFilter.isSelfRef`, `GainMemory 1` |
| host scope ("this Digimon's link cards") | `gains no memory when an effect trashes ANOTHER Digimon's link card` | `sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true }` |
| `[Once Per Turn]` | `[Once Per Turn]: a second effect link trash in the same turn…`, `[Once Per Turn] resets on the next own turn…` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |

The natural-origin proof of clause 2 is the interesting one: a Sakusimon Digimon hosts a
second Sakusimon as its link card. Attacking fires the LINK copy's `[When Attacking]`
effect, whose cost is a genuine effect trash of the host's link card, so the host's
`[All Turns]` clause pays out through a single public `attack` intent. That test also
re-proves Q5123 from the host side.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-043.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
# 1 file, 16 tests, all passed (logs/EX10-043-focused.log)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck    # clean (logs/EX10-043-typecheck.log)
pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-043.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-043.test.ts
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>
git diff --check
```

Mutation check (module mutated, run, then restored — `logs/EX10-043-mutation.log`):
`levels: [3] → [5]`, `lte 4 → lte 5`, and dropping `sourceFilter.isSelfRef` from the
`[All Turns]` watcher failed 6 of the 16 tests, including all four behavioural
level-boundary tests and the host-scope negative. The module file is byte-identical to
HEAD, so `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` does not drift from this lane.

#### Defects found and fixed

No module defect. The IR already matches every printed clause and every ruling, and the
engine already suppresses `whenLinkTrashed` for rule-driven trashes (`primitives.ts`
`trash`, `opts.byRule`), which is exactly Q5124.

What was wrong was the **evidence**. The committed test file (7 tests) was replaced
(16 tests) because:

1. `[On Play]` was proved with `advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)` —
   injected timing, no behavioural credit. Now proved by playing the card from hand for
   its 5 memory, with the paid cost, the hand, the board and the trash asserted.
2. `[When Digivolving]` had **no test at all**, in either the structural or behavioural
   sense beyond a shape assertion. Now proved through a real `digivolve` intent from
   ST6-05 (inert Purple Lv.3) for 2 memory, asserting the stack identity and the bonus
   draw, with a Red Lv.3 illegal-source negative alongside.
3. The Q5125 fixture was wrong. It seeded **two** link cards on BT21-009 Gatchmon, which
   prints no ＜Link +X＞ and therefore has a link cap of 1. The attack that drove the test
   also runs CR §4-9-5 rule processing (`GameEngine.trashExcessLinkCards`), so the second
   link card was liable to be trashed by rule rather than by the printed cost — the test
   could pass without the cost ever selecting it. The fixture now uses BT26-086 Dantemon
   (＜Link +6＞), so both link cards are legal and only the cost removes one.
4. Q5124 was not covered at all. The old "Q5124" test trashed a *neighbour's* link card,
   which is the host-scope negative, not the ruling. Q5124 is now proved through a real
   `linkCard` intent that overflows the cap.
5. The link effect had no level boundary test, no decline test, and no assertion that
   the deletion is restricted to level 4 or lower. All three are added.

#### Retained reds and remaining gaps

None red. Two rubric ceilings, both fixture-shaped, not card defects:

- **`[Once Per Turn]` same-turn refusal is injected.** Two effect trashes of *this*
  Digimon's link cards in one turn need two link cards on a Sakusimon, and Sakusimon
  prints no ＜Link +X＞. No public intent or Advance Surface verb grants one — engine seam
  queue item 7, `testkit-link-grant-affordance`. The refusal test therefore seeds an
  over-cap board and drives `advance(...).verb.trash`; it runs outside the turn loop so
  rule processing never trims the seeded pair. Structural credit only.
- **The reset half is behavioural.** `[Once Per Turn] resets on the next own turn` runs
  the real turn loop (`startTurnLoop` / `waitForMainPhase` / `endPhase`), re-links a fresh
  EX10-038 through the public `linkCard` intent on the next own turn, and shows the gate
  pay out again. Only the trash itself is verb-driven.

Two observations for the coordinator, neither owned by this lane:

- **Seeded link cards do not contribute their link DP.** A Board Spec `linked: [...]`
  entry with a `linkDp` card leaves the host's `currentDP` at its seeded value; the +3000
  only appears after a real `linkCard` intent (proved in `links only onto an [Appmon]
  host…`). A DP assertion around a seeded link therefore reads the wrong baseline. This
  looks like a harness seeding gap (`harness.ts buildPermanent` calls `linkCard` on the
  state directly and never runs `ledger.recomputeDP`), not a production defect — the
  production link path recomputes correctly. Worth a testkit fix so link-DP assertions
  are possible on seeded boards.
- **An over-cap seeded board cannot enter the turn loop.** `trashExcessLinkCards` raises
  a `selectCards` decision for the excess, and `advance(...).waitForMainPhase` then times
  out on the unanswered decision. Not a defect; recorded so the next lane that seeds
  over-cap links does not lose an hour to it.

#### Catalog discrepancies

- `linkRequirement` for EX10-043 is `"[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 2"` — the U+00A0
  after the bracketed keyword that REVIEW-NOTES already records repo-wide (1488 fields).
  The test compares the requirement with whitespace normalized rather than editing the
  catalog. No other discrepancy: name, colors, level, DP, cost, evoCosts, forms,
  attributes, types, linkDp and the two effect texts all match the official English text.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog / rules | 2 | Every printed clause traced to `cards.json`; all three Q&A ids read from the KB and each mapped to a test; the only catalog wart (U+00A0) is reported, not edited. |
| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a named IR node, including the `whenLinkTrashed` `sourceFilter` and the `abortOnDecline` optional cost; mutation run proves the IR fields are load-bearing. |
| Behaviour | 2 | `[On Play]`, `[When Digivolving]`, the link requirement, the link effect (positive, level boundary, decline), the `[All Turns]` gain and both Q&A negatives all run through public intents with exact endpoints. Only the OPT same-turn refusal is injected, and the reset half of that clause is behavioural. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Evolution proved on a real stack (ST6-05 beneath Sakusimon, stack identity and bonus draw asserted) with an illegal-source negative; the link effect proved on two different hosts (BT21-009 single-link, BT26-086 multi-link) with a neighbouring host as the negative; nothing reaches past `apps/api/src/engine/testkit/`. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

### EX10-044 — Damemon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-044.test.ts`: Bottom placement/Draw, Tuwarmon play boundary, Save/refusal, inherited Draw through real Tuwarmon evolution and Bagra Army negative.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple, Lv.4 Champion, DP 3000, play cost 4, EvoCost Purple Lv.3 cost 2, traits
[Mutant] / [Bagra Army]. Catalog matches the official list; no discrepancy found.

#### Printed clauses

1. `[On Play]` By placing 1 [Bagra Army] trait Digimon card from your hand or trash under
   any of your Tamers, ＜Draw 1＞
2. `[On Deletion]` You may play 1 [Tuwarmon] with a play cost of 7 or less from under your
   Tamers without paying the cost. Then, ＜Save＞
3. Inherited: When effects trash this card from a [Bagra Army] trait Digimon's digivolution
   cards, ＜Draw 1＞

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Covered by |
| --- | --- |
| Q5126 | `[On Play] Q5126: the placed [Bagra Army] card lands at the BOTTOM of the Tamer, then draws 1` — and again by both ＜Save＞ tests, which assert the same bottom rule for clause 2. |
| Q5160 | Not testable through the public seam. See "Remaining gaps". |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 placement cost, source zones, trait gate, draw | `[On Play] Q5126 …` (public `playCard`, memory 4→0, Tamer stack `[material, older1, older2]`, hand `[notBagra, drawn]`, deck empty) | `OnPlay` → `Draw` with `cost.kind: "place"`, `from: ["hand","trash"]`, `nameOrTrait: [{tokens:["Bagra Army"], match:"trait"}]`, `underFilter: {kind:["Tamer"]}`, `position: "bottom"` |
| 1 negative (cost unpayable) | `[On Play]: with no [Bagra Army] card available …` (nothing placed, nothing drawn, trash and deck untouched) | `optional: true, abortOnDecline: true` |
| 2 free play of a cost-7 [Tuwarmon] from under a Tamer | `[On Deletion]: a battle deletion plays the cost-7 Tuwarmon free, then Saves at the BOTTOM` (real `attack` into a 6000 DP wall; BT11-082, play cost exactly 7; memory unchanged at 1) | `OnDeletion` → `PlayWithoutCost`, `from: ["underTamers"]`, `payCost: false`, `playCostLte: 7`, `nameOrTrait match: "name"` |
| 2 ＜Save＞ position | same test, plus `[On Deletion]: with no [Tuwarmon] under the Tamer …` — both assert `stack === [damemon, older1, older2]` | `PlaceUnder`, `isSelf: true`, `underFilter: {kind:["Tamer"], excludeToken:true}`, **`position: "bottom"`** |
| 2 optionality | `[On Deletion]: ＜Save＞ is optional — declining sends Damemon to the trash` (`autoDeclineOptional`) | `optional: true` on both actions |
| 2 name gate | `[On Deletion]: with no [Tuwarmon] under the Tamer …` (only inert cards under the Tamer; nothing is played) | exact-name `match: "name"` |
| 3 inherited draw, host trait gate | `inherited: a real digivolve stack whose [Bagra Army] host effect-trashes this card draws 1` — public `digivolve` of EX10-044 into EX10-045 Tuwarmon on the printed `[Damemon]: Cost 1` route (memory 3→2), whose `[When Digivolving]` pays by trashing a digivolution card of a [Bagra Army] Digimon; hand ends `[spare, evoDraw, inheritedDraw]` (evolution bonus draw + inherited draw) | `Static` `isInherited` → `SubTrigger` `onDigivolutionCardsDiscardedBatch`, `sourceFilter.isSelfRef`, `hostFilter` [Bagra Army] Digimon |
| 3 negative host | `inherited: an effect trash from a non-[Bagra Army] host draws nothing` | same `hostFilter` |

All positive proof runs through public intents (`playCard`, `attack`, `digivolve`). No
`advance.fire*` remains in the file — the previous test proved every clause through injected
timing, which earns no behavioural credit. The single remaining testkit verb is
`trashDigivolutionCards` in the negative-host case; no public route trashes a digivolution
card from an inert host, and the positive case carries the behavioural proof.

#### Defect found and fixed

**＜Save＞ placed Damemon just beneath the Tamer instead of at the bottom of its stack.**
`runPlaceUnder` calls `relocateByEffect(..., { belowTop: action.position !== "bottom" })`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/placeUnder.ts:340`). The compiled
`OnDeletion` `PlaceUnder` carried no `position`, so `belowTop` defaulted to true. Q5126 and
Comprehensive Rules 4-3 require the bottom.

Fix: `position: "bottom"` on the `OnDeletion` `PlaceUnder` in `EX10-044.ts`, matching the
accepted shape in `EX10-026.ts` / `EX10-027.ts`.

Revert check (`logs/EX10-044-revert-check.log`): with the line removed, 3 tests fail,
including `expected [ 'inst-22', 'inst-23', 'inst-18' ] to deeply equal
[ 'inst-18', 'inst-22', 'inst-23' ]` — the saved card lands directly beneath the Tamer.
With it restored, all 8 pass.

The `[On Play]` cost already carried `position: "bottom"`, and there it is inert: the
loose-card place-cost branch hardcodes `belowTop: false`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/costs.ts:2133`). It is kept because it documents
the intent and because a future refactor of that branch would silently change the endpoint.

#### Fixture defects in the previous test (corrected)

- Every clause was fired with `advance.fire*` / `advance.verb.deletePermanent`; no public
  intent was used at all. Replaced with `playCard`, `attack` and `digivolve`.
- The Q5126 test placed under a Tamer holding **one** card, which cannot distinguish
  "bottom" from "just below the top" for the ＜Save＞ action, and it never exercised the
  ＜Save＞ position at all. Both ＜Save＞ tests now use two pre-existing cards.
- The old `[On Deletion]` test asserted only `stack.map(cardId).toContain(CARD_ID)` — order
  unasserted, so the defect above was invisible.
- The old test drew into a deck holding `BT1-010` Agumon, which prints an `[On Play]`
  effect; replaced with inert `BT1-013` / `BT1-014`.
- The `[On Play]` negative asserted a `toContain` on the hand rather than exact zone
  contents, so a partial payment would have passed.

#### Commands and results

```bash
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-044.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
# Test Files 1 passed (1) | Tests 8 passed (8)   -> logs/EX10-044-vitest.log
pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck                 -> logs/EX10-044-typecheck.log
# Clean for EX10-044. The one reported error belongs to another lane:
# src/cards/EX10/EX10-043.test.ts(118,45): TS2339 Property 'permanentId' does not
# exist on type 'CardInstance'. An earlier run, before that file changed, exited 0.
pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-044.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-044.test.ts   # clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>           # All matched files use the correct format
git diff --check                                   # clean
```

#### Remaining gaps

- **Q5160 is not reachable through the public seam and the rule it states is not
  implemented.** The ruling: an inherited effect pending activation lapses when the card
  leaves the area it was in while activation is still pending (trashed from digivolution
  cards, then played out of the trash before the inherited draw activates). The engine has
  no general "pending activation lapses when the source leaves" gate — a search for such a
  guard finds only per-card notes (`apps/api/src/cards/BT20/AUDIT.md` on Q4285), not an
  engine seam. Reproducing the scenario also needs two effects interleaved inside one trash
  batch (an `[All Turns]` trash plus a play-from-trash), which no single public intent
  produces. Left unasserted rather than guessed; flagged for the coordinator as an engine
  question, not an EX10-044 question.
- The `[On Play]` cost's `from: ["trash"]` branch is not separately proved: with a
  [Bagra Army] card in both hand and trash the engine picks one, and there is no ruling
  fixing which. The negative test does show a trash card that fails the trait gate is not
  taken.
- No `[Tuwarmon]` with play cost above 7 exists in the catalog (BT11-082 and EX10-045 are
  both exactly 7, BT12-064 is 5), so the upper side of the `≤7` boundary cannot be shown
  with a real card. The lower side is shown at exactly 7, and the name gate is shown by the
  no-Tuwarmon negative.
- The inherited positive depends on EX10-045 Tuwarmon's `[When Digivolving]` clause, which
  another lane owns. It passes today; if that card changes, this test is the canary.

| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

#### Card summary

DP 7000, Champion, Virus, types `[Mutant]` `[Bagra Army]`, rarity U, max 4 per deck.
EvoCosts: Purple Lv.3 cost 3, Black Lv.3 cost 3.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| `[DigiXros -2] [Damemon] x [ChuuChuumon]` | `digiXrosRequirement: [{ materials: [{names:["Damemon"]},{names:["ChuuChuumon"]}], count: 2 }]` (`count` is the per-material reduction, `xrosLink.ts`) | "one [Damemon] and one [ChuuChuumon] each cut 2 from the play cost" (memory 7 -> 4, both materials become digivolution cards); "a card matching neither named slot is refused as material" |

#### Defect fixed locally

`save-placeunder-position-default` (REVIEW-NOTES seam 16). The `[On Deletion] ＜Save＞` `PlaceUnder`
carried no `position`, and `runPlaceUnder` reads `belowTop: action.position !== "bottom"`, so the
saved card landed directly beneath the Tamer instead of at the bottom of its stack (comprehensive
4-3). Fixed in `EX10-045.ts` with `position: "bottom"`, matching the accepted shape in
`EX10-026.ts` / `EX10-027.ts`. The behavioural test seeds two cards under the Tamer and asserts the
exact resulting order `[tuwarmon, under0, under1]`, so the line is load-bearing.

No other module change was needed: the DigiXros pair, the shared once-per-turn key, the
`fromSelectionRef` binding of the second keyword, the `[Bagra Army]` `hostFilter` on the cost and
the inherited `isSelfRef` scope all matched the printed text under review.

#### Test rewrite

The committed test proved most clauses through the injected timing surface
(`advance.fireForPermanent`, `advance.verb.deletePermanent`, `advance.verb.trashDigivolutionCards`),
which earns no behavioural credit under this brief, and it had no once-per-turn proof at all.
Every clause is now driven by public intents: `playCard`, `digivolve` (with `useAlternateCost` and
`alternateRequirementIndex`), `attack`, `declareBlock`, `playCard` with `digiXros`, plus
`startTurnLoop` for the once-per-turn reset and the duration crossing into the opponent's turn.
`advance` is used only for `waitForMainPhase` / `endMainPhaseIfOpen`, which are turn-loop
navigation, not injected timing.

Fixture notes for reuse:

- There is no inert `[Bagra Army]` Digimon in the catalog. `EX10-026` (SkullKnightmon) and
  `EX10-044` (Damemon) are the quietest: all their printed clauses are `[On Play]` /
  `[When Digivolving]` / `[On Deletion]`, so they sit still on a board and never open a decision.
- `BT12-094` (Yuu Amano) is the neutral ＜Save＞ Tamer: it prints no `[On Deletion]` or battle-time
  clause. `EX10-064` would fire a `[Start of Your Main Phase]` prompt inside a turn loop.
- `EX10-028` (Landramon, `[Mineral]`/`[LIBERATOR]`) is the trait-mix negative for the cost's
  `hostFilter`; it does carry an inherited deletion clause, but that clause never fires here
  because the cost is refused before anything is trashed.

#### Corrected premise (not an engine defect)

A first draft asserted that a SECOND copy of Tuwarmon played in the same turn was blocked by the
shared once-per-turn key. It is not, and should not be: `[Once Per Turn]` is per Digimon, so a
second copy has its own use. The test now proves the shared key the right way — one Digimon,
`[When Digivolving]` then `[When Attacking]` in the same turn, then a reset on its next own turn.

#### Observation for the coordinator (low priority, not a retained red)

An unblocked seat-0 attack declared against `{ kind: "player" }` while the defender holds a
Digimon that ＜Collision＞ makes eligible to block appears to leave the attack mid-resolution after
its security check: with `turnSeat === 1`, `phase === Main`, no `pendingDecision`, the attacker
unsuspended, and after `settle` on the security endpoint plus a 30-tick drain, seat 1's own
`attack` intent was still refused `{ ok: false, reason: "wrong-phase" }`. `validateAttack`
(`apps/api/src/engine/actions/attack.ts:80`) returns `wrong-phase` for `deps.combat.isAttacking`,
which is the only remaining branch consistent with the observed state. `waitForMainPhase` does not
wait on `combat.isAttacking` (it checks `mainEntryPending`, `activeWindowToken` and the two
resolution depths), so a test crossing turns after such an attack has no seam-level way to wait
for the combat to close. Worked around here by sourcing the grant from `[On Play]` instead of
`[When Attacking]` in that one test; the `[When Attacking]` arm is still proved behaviourally in
the once-per-turn test, where the defender has no battle-area Digimon. Suggested owner: engine or
testkit lane, as either a `combat.isAttacking` clause in `waitForMainPhase` or an
`advance(...).waitForCombatIdle()` affordance.

#### Remaining gaps

- ＜Collision＞ is proved through block legality only, which is all the engine exposes
  (`combat/legality.ts hasCollision`); it grants no observable keyword.
- The inherited clause's `[Bagra Army]` `hostFilter` is proved structurally (IR assertion) and
  positively (a Bagra host draws). A behavioural negative would need a non-Bagra host whose
  digivolution cards some effect can trash; Tuwarmon's own cost cannot reach one by construction,
  and no other effect on a legal fixture board does. The `isSelfRef` half of the scope IS proved
  behaviourally (a different card trashed from the same host draws nothing).

     (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-045-vitest-final.log)

  -> clean (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-045-typecheck-final.log)

  -> no findings

`packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` now drifts for this card. Coordinator-owned.

### EX10-046 — Devimon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-046.test.ts`: Start Main/digivolve mill and return threshold, inherited replacement and quota reset.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple Lv.4 Champion, Virus, [Fallen Angel]. Play cost 5, DP 5000,
digivolve from a Purple Lv.3 for 2. Rarity C, max 4 in deck. No Security text.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|---|
| C1 | `[Start of Your Main Phase]` `[When Digivolving]` If your opponent has 10 or fewer cards in their trash, trash the top 2 cards of both players' decks. |
| C2 | Then, if they have 10 or more cards in their trash, you may return 1 card with the [Fallen Angel] or [Undead] trait from your trash to the hand. |
| C3 | Inherited: `[When Attacking]` `[Once Per Turn]` Trash the top card of both players' decks. |

C1 and C2 are two independently gated actions sharing one trigger pair, not a chain
where C2 depends on C1 firing. Both rulings below turn on exactly that.

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Where proved |
|---|---|---|
| Q5127 | Opponent at 8 in trash: C1 mills, and C2 may still be processed (the mill pushes the count to 10). | `Q5127: opponent at 8 in trash …` |
| Q5128 | Opponent at 11 in trash: C1 does not mill, but C2 may still be processed. | `Q5128: opponent at 11 in trash …` |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Test |
|---|---|---|
| C1 (`StartOfYourMainPhase`) | `effects[0].actions[0]` `TrashTopDeck` controller `both`, amount 2, `zoneCount opponent/trash lte 10` | Q5127 (mills, opponent trash 8 -> 10), Q5128 (skipped at 11), boundary at 7 (mills to 9) |
| C2 (`StartOfYourMainPhase`) | `effects[0].actions[1]` `Return` to `hand`, `optional: true`, `zoneCount opponent/trash gte 10`, target `zone: trash`, `controller: mine`, `nameOrTrait [{tokens:["Fallen Angel","Undead"], match:"trait"}]`, `count: 1` | Q5127 (Fallen Angel returned, [Undead] and non-matching left behind), Q5128 ([Undead] branch), boundary at 9 (no offer), decline case |
| C1 + C2 (`WhenDigivolving`) | `effects[1]`, identical body | `[When Digivolving]: … public Lv.3 Purple digivolve route`, plus an illegal-source negative |
| C3 | `effects[2]` `TrashTopDeck` both/1, `isInherited`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` | real-stack test (Lv.3 -> Devimon -> Lv.5, then attack) and the once-per-turn test |

Notes on the IR shape:

- `match: "trait"` is exact, which is right — the text uses `[Fallen Angel]` / `[Undead]`
  brackets, not "in any of its traits".
- The two triggers are compiled as two separate effects with identical bodies. That is the
  correct reading of the printed `[Start of Your Main Phase] [When Digivolving]` pair: two
  timings, one body, no shared once-per-turn gate (none is printed).
- `controller: "both"` on the mill matches "both players' decks"; the condition seat is
  `opponent`, matching "your opponent"/"they".

#### Behavioural coverage

All ten cases drive production windows. No `advance.fire*` injected timing is used
anywhere in this file; the previous version of the test proved C1/C2 and the once-per-turn
reset with `fireForPermanent`, which earns no behavioural credit under this brief.

- Start-of-main timing: `s.engine.startTurnLoop()` + `waitForMainPhase(0)`; the trigger
  fires on its own.
- `[When Digivolving]`: public `digivolve` intent from a Lv.3 Purple base, with stack
  identity and the 2-memory cost asserted, plus a rejected illegal-source digivolve off a
  Red Lv.3 that leaves the board and the opponent's trash untouched.
- Inherited clause: public `attack` intents. Same-turn refusal is proved by unsuspending
  the same host with a public `playCard` of BT1-095 and attacking a second time — a real
  second attack window, not an injected fire. Reset is proved across the real turn loop
  (own turn -> opponent turn -> own turn).
- Deck lengths, not trash lengths, are the mill's endpoints in the attack tests, because a
  security check moves the checked card to the trash and would mask the count.

Fixtures are inert: BT1-009/BT1-013/BT1-014 (no text at all), BT6-076 Feresmon (inert,
[Fallen Angel]), BT2-075 Myotismon (inert, [Undead]), ST6-02 DemiDevimon (inert Purple
Lv.3), BT4-085 Phantomon (inert Purple Lv.5, 10000 DP). No Digi-Egg in deck or security,
no numeric `security: <n>` form. BT1-095 Brave Shield is the one non-inert fixture; its
only side effect is a ＜Blocker＞ grant on the attacker, which nothing here asserts.

#### Load-bearing check

The module was mutated (`Return` condition `gte 10` -> `gte 0`, and the inherited
`frequency` `OncePerTurn` -> `Unlimited`) and re-run: 3 of 10 tests failed — the IR shape
guard, the 7-in-trash boundary, and the once-per-turn case. The module was then restored
byte-for-byte. Log: `logs/EX10-046-mutation.log`.

The Q5127 and Q5128 cases survive that particular mutation by construction (their gates are
true either way); the boundary case is the one that pins the threshold.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-046.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 10 passed (10)   [logs/EX10-046-vitest.log]

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        -> exit 0  [logs/EX10-046-typecheck.log]
pnpm exec oxlint <module> <test>          -> exit 0, no findings  [logs/EX10-046-lint.log]
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <module> <test>   -> all files correctly formatted
git diff --check                          -> clean
```

#### Defects

None in the module. `EX10-046.ts` is unchanged by this lane; the compiled IR already
matches all three clauses and both rulings. The defect was in the evidence, not the
implementation: the previous test proved C1/C2 and the once-per-turn reset only through
injected timing, used the noisy EX10-053 Regulusmon as the inherited-clause host, and had
no threshold boundary, no optional-decline branch, no `[When Digivolving]` route and no
evolution-stack proof.

#### Catalog discrepancy

`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` -> `EX10-046.effectText` reads
"… tash the top 2 cards of both players' decks …". "tash" should be "trash". Cosmetic —
the IR is compiled correctly — but it is a text mismatch against the official list.
Not edited (catalog is coordinator-owned).

#### Remaining gaps

- The inherited clause's once-per-turn refusal needs an unsuspend to open a second attack
  window, so BT1-095 Brave Shield is in the fixture. That is a public play, but it does mean
  the same-turn case is not reachable with an all-inert board.
- No proof of the clause when a deck is empty (no cards to mill). The text has no such
  branch and no ruling covers it, so nothing is asserted.
- The `[Start of Your Main Phase]` and `[When Digivolving]` copies are proved separately;
  there is no case where both fire in one turn (Devimon would have to be on the board at
  start of main and then be digivolved into again, which the printed card cannot do).

| Behavior | 2 | All three clauses proved through production windows only, with threshold boundary, optional decline, trait-filter negative, once-per-turn refusal and reset. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Real Lv.3 -> Devimon -> Lv.5 stack built by public digivolves with source-stack identity and DP asserted, inherited clause fired from that stack, plus an illegal-source negative and a mixed-trait trash pool. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

### EX10-047 — Arukenimon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-047.test.ts`: DP-budget deletion, optional hand cost, Myotismon-text Tamer filter, duplicate-name exclusion and evolution.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

#### Card summary

Purple/Red Lv.5 Ultimate, [Dark Animal], Virus, 6000 DP, play cost 6.
Digivolve: Purple Lv.4 for 4, or Red Lv.4 for 4. No inherited text, no security text.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play]` By trashing 1 card in your hand, delete up to 6000 DP total worth of your
   opponent's Digimon.
2. `[On Deletion]` You may play 1 Tamer card with [Myotismon] in its text from your trash
   without paying the cost. This effect can't play cards with the same name as any of your
   Tamers.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Question | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5129 | What "X in its text" refers to (name, traits, effects, inherited, rule, requirements) | `[On Deletion] Q5129: a losing attack plays the [Myotismon]-in-text Tamer from trash for free` and the negative case below it. BT16-089 "Arukenimon & Mummymon" has no [Myotismon] in its **name** — only in its effect text — and is still a legal candidate. ST3-12, which has [Myotismon] nowhere, is not. |

The engine side of Q5129 is `matchNameOrTrait` in
`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:296`: for `match: "text"` it
unions name, trait and the concatenation of `effectText`, `inheritedEffectText`,
`securityEffectText`, `linkEffect`, `linkRequirement`, `dualEffect`, `optionEffect` — the
union Q5129 describes.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[On Play]` trigger | `trigger: "OnPlay"` | `[On Play] played from hand: ...` — fired by the public `playCard` intent, not injected timing |
| "By trashing 1 card in your hand" | `cost: { kind: "trash", target: { filter: { zone: "hand", controller: "mine" }, count: 1 } }` | same test asserts the exact hand instance moved to my trash and the hand is empty |
| "delete up to 6000 DP total worth" | `DeleteByDPBudget`, `baseBudget: 6000`, `upTo: true` | 3000 + 3000 deleted, 4000 survives (budget exactly filled); 7000 alone never fits |
| "your opponent's Digimon" | `target.filter { controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon"] }`, `count: "all"` | my own board is untouched in every case; the interpreter re-reads the target with `count: "all"` (`interpreter/actions/removal.ts:434`), so the printed count is the budget, not a card count |
| "up to" is optional | `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true` | `[On Play] declining ...`: hand card kept, opposing 3000 kept |
| `[On Deletion]` trigger | `trigger: "OnDeletion"` | fired by a real losing `attack` (6000 into a suspended 20000 wall), not `verb.deletePermanent` |
| "You may play 1 Tamer card ... from your trash without paying the cost" | `PlayWithoutCost`, `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: false`, `count: 1` | eligible Tamer leaves the trash and appears on the board as that exact instance; memory unchanged (Tamer play cost 4 not paid) |
| "with [Myotismon] in its text" | `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Myotismon"], match: "text" }]` | ST3-12 (no [Myotismon]) is never played |
| "can't play cards with the same name as any of your Tamers" | `excludeSameNameAsOwnTamers: true` | BT8-093 "Yukio Oikawa" stays in the trash while EX10-065 "Yukio Oikawa" is on my field; enforced at `interpreter/actions/play.ts:490` |
| "You may" | `optional: true` | `[On Deletion] is a 'may': declining leaves the eligible Tamer in the trash` |
| Printed digivolution route (Red Lv.4 for 4) | catalog `evoCosts[1]` — no IR, the engine's own route check | `digivolves onto a Red Lv.4 source for 4 memory, ...`: public `digivolve` intent onto inert BT1-014, memory 4 -> 0, same `permanentId`, `Permanent.stack` = the single source instance, `currentDP` 6000 (not the source's 4000), evolution bonus draws the aliased top-deck card, `[On Play]` correctly does not fire |
| Illegal source | same | `refuses an illegal source: a Red Lv.3 does not satisfy either printed Lv.4 route`: BT1-013 (Red Lv.3) with 10 memory is rejected, so the refusal is the source requirement and not the cost; board, hand, deck and memory all unchanged |

Fixture pool for the Tamer filter (mixed, per the skill's trait-pool rule): EX10-065 on the
field, and three Tamers in the trash — BT16-089 (eligible), BT8-093 (text match, blocked by
name), ST3-12 (no match at all).

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file proved every behavioural clause through injected timing:
`advance(s.engine).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)` for `[On Play]` and
`advance(s.engine).verb.deletePermanent([...], "byEffect")` for `[On Deletion]`. Under this
run's rules that is structural proof only and earns 0 in the Behavior column, so the
previous 10/10 claim was not supported. It also had no negative for the text filter (only
the same-name exclusion), no "may" refusal on `[On Deletion]`, no memory assertion, and no
empty-board case.

Rewritten: `[On Play]` now runs through `applyIntent(0, { type: "playCard", ... })` with the
play cost taken from memory (11 -> 5), and `[On Deletion]` through a real losing attack. The
module (`EX10-047.ts`) needed no change — the IR was already correct.

#### Defects found

- **Catalog (report only, not edited):** `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` stores
  `EX10-047.effectText` with a non-breaking space (U+00A0) after `[Myotismon]` in the
  `[On Deletion]` line. Harmless for matching (the compiled token is `"Myotismon"`), but it
  makes any exact-text comparison fail invisibly. The catalog test spells the codepoint out
  with a comment so the quirk is recorded rather than hidden.
- **Behaviour worth recording, not a defect:** using the `[On Play]` effect with no opposing
  Digimon at all still pays the hand card (`DeleteByDPBudget` returns early only after the
  cost has been paid). That is consistent with "up to" allowing a selection of zero, so the
  test asserts it rather than treating it as a bug. Same for the 7000 DP boundary case: the
  cost is a processing condition of a used effect and is paid even when the greedy budget
  pass selects nothing.

No test is retained red. No engine change was needed, so no seam is reported.

#### Remaining gaps

- The Purple Lv.4 route is not exercised separately. Both printed routes cost 4 and differ
  only in the source colour, and the Red route is proved end to end, so the untested half is
  the catalog's colour field rather than any card-specific logic.
- The `[On Deletion]` proof uses combat deletion only. Deletion by an opponent effect, and
  deletion while a would-leave replacement is in play, are untested.
- `DeleteByDPBudget`'s budget bonuses (`dpDeleteBudgetBonus`, `deletionMaxDpBonus`) are read
  by the shared action but are not exercised from this card; they belong to the engine
  capability suite (`capabilities.test.ts` CAP-A1).

pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>  # all matched files use the correct format

### EX10-048 — Myotismon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated

across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple, Lv.5 Ultimate, Virus, [Undead]. Play cost 7, DP 7000, digivolve Purple Lv.4 cost 3.

#### Printed clauses

1. **Play-cost replacement** — "When this card would be played, by deleting 1 of your Digimon
   with [Myotismon] in its text, reduce the play cost by 4."
2. **[On Play] [On Deletion]** — "Until your opponent's turn ends, 1 of your purple Digimon
   gains ＜Blocker＞ and ＜Retaliation＞" (one Digimon gains both).
3. **Inherited [On Deletion]** — "You may play 1 purple Tamer card from your trash suspended
   without paying the cost."

No Security effect.

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5130 | "[X] in its text" spans name, traits, effects, inherited effects, (Rule), digivolution / DNA / DigiXros / burst / App Fusion / Link / Assembly requirements | Covered behaviourally. The cost target uses `match: "text"`, which `definitionMatches` resolves as the union of name, traits and every text field, and `permanentMatchesFilter` widens it to live inherited text. Proved with EX1-056 DemiDevimon, which carries "[Myotismon]" only in its **effect** text, never in its name. Gap noted below. |
| Q5131 | The cost deletion's [On Deletion] and this card's [On Play] trigger simultaneously; the turn player chooses which resolves first | **Retained red** (`it.fails`). Engine seam — no `orderTriggers` decision is raised. See "Retained red". |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cost reduction, "in its text" positive | "Q5130: deletes a Digimon holding [Myotismon] only in its EFFECT text and pays 3 instead of 7" — public `playCard` intent, memory 7 -> 4, EX1-056 the only card in the trash | `Static` -> `Replacement { event: "wouldBePlayed", mode: "reduceCost", amount: 4, cost: deleteOwn, sourceFilter.isSelfRef }` |
| Cost reduction negative | "without a [Myotismon]-text Digimon the reduction is unavailable: the full cost 7 is paid" — a purple Digimon is on the board but none carries the text, so memory 7 -> 0 and nothing is trashed | same |
| [On Play] grant, same target, colour gate | Both tests above — the chosen purple ally holds ＜Blocker＞ **and** ＜Retaliation＞; the red Digimon holds neither; Myotismon itself was not the target | `OnPlay` -> two `GainKeyword`, second with `sameTarget: true` |
| Grant duration | "the grant lasts through the opponent's turn and expires at its end" — real turn loop (`startTurnLoop`, `waitForMainPhase(0/1/0)`): granted on my turn, still active through the opponent's whole turn, gone at my next Main | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` |
| [On Deletion] grant on this card | "[On Deletion] on the card itself: losing a battle grants the pair to a purple Digimon" — public `attack` intent into a suspended 20 000 DP wall | `OnDeletion` (non-inherited) -> two `GainKeyword` |
| Inherited [On Deletion] positive | "the inherited [On Deletion] plays a purple Tamer from trash suspended and for free" — host BT2-067 carrying EX10-048 loses a battle; BT3-096 Mimi Tachikawa arrives suspended, memory unchanged at 0 (printed cost 2 never charged) | `OnDeletion` `isInherited` -> `PlayWithoutCost { from: ["trash"], payCost: false, suspended: true, optional: true }` |
| Inherited [On Deletion] negative | "the inherited [On Deletion] refuses a non-purple Tamer in the trash" — BT1-085 Tai Kamiya (red) stays in the trash | same |
| Q5131 observable half | "the cost deletion resolves and the play still completes even though the ordering window is missing" — EX10-047 Arukenimon is deleted as the cost, memory 7 -> 4, [On Play] resolves | — |
| IR shape | "compiles the printed clauses with no residual" | full compiled record |
| Catalog | "records the exact catalog" | — |

Every behavioural test drives public intents (`playCard`, `attack`, `respondDecision`) plus the
production turn loop. No `advance.fire*` structural proof is used anywhere in this file; the
previous version proved the [On Play] clause with `advance.fireForPermanent` and the inherited
clause with `advance.verb.deletePermanent`, both of which earn no behavioural credit.

#### Retained red

`it.fails("Q5131: the cost deletion's [On Deletion] and this card's [On Play] are ordered by the
turn player")`.

- **Seam**: `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/replacement.ts`, `runReplacement`,
  the `mode === "reduceCost"` subscription's `activate` callback — it calls `payCost` for the
  `kind: "deleteOwn"` cost inside the `wouldBePlayed` replacement.
- **Expected**: playing EX10-048 and deleting EX10-047 Arukenimon (which prints its own
  [On Deletion]) raises an `orderTriggers` decision for seat 0 with two keys, one per source.
- **Actual**: no `orderTriggers` decision is raised at all. The cost deletion happens outside
  the play's trigger batch, so the deleted Digimon's [On Deletion] never joins the batch the
  decision is built from. Arukenimon's [On Deletion] did not appear as a trigger key and did not
  resolve (its purple Tamer stayed in the trash).
- **Proof the batching itself works**: with EX10-065 Yukio Oikawa seeded in the trash, the same
  play does produce a two-key `orderTriggers` decision — Myotismon's `OnPlay` plus Oikawa's
  `[All Turns]` subtrigger. Only the cost deletion is missing from the batch.
- Not fixable in a card module: it is the shared replacement-cost path.

#### Other findings for the coordinator

1. **Catalog discrepancy (do not edit)**: `EX10-048.effectText` contains a NON-BREAKING SPACE
   (U+00A0) at offset 78, after "[Myotismon]" in "…with [Myotismon] in its text…". The official
   list has a plain space. It does not affect matching (the token is bracket-delimited), but the
   catalog test has to normalize it. `inheritedEffectText` is clean.
2. **Engine seam, unrelated card**: EX10-065 Yukio Oikawa's `[All Turns]` watcher ("When any of
   your Digimon with [Myotismon] in their names are played…") fires while the Tamer is in the
   **trash**. Observed as trigger key
   `inst-3::subtrigger/6/When any of your Digimon with [Myotismon] in their names are played…`
   for an instance seeded in seat 0's trash. A Tamer in the trash has no effects. Belongs to
   whoever owns EX10-065 / the subtrigger installation seam.
3. **Harmless IR noise**: the `Replacement` carries `abortOnDecline: true`. For `mode:
   "reduceCost"` the engine never reads it (declining `activate` simply skips the reduction and
   the play continues at full cost, which is correct). Left in place so the persisted
   `effects.json` record does not drift for a no-op.
4. **Q5130 breadth**: `definitionMatches`'s "text" union covers name, traits, `effectText`,
   `inheritedEffectText`, `securityEffectText`, `linkEffect`, `linkRequirement`, `dualEffect` and
   `optionEffect`. The ruling also names digivolution / DNA / DigiXros / burst / App Fusion /
   Link / Assembly **requirements** as text. Requirements are structured fields
   (`evoCosts`, DigiXros specs), not text fields, so a card that names [Myotismon] only in a
   digivolution requirement would not match today. No EX10 fixture exposes it; recorded as a
   general engine gap rather than a card defect.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q5131's ordering window (retained red above).
- The Q5130 requirement-field breadth described in finding 4.
- The inherited clause is proved on a hand-seeded stack (EX10-048 under BT2-067), not through a
  full breeding-to-battle evolution route; EX10-048 is a Lv.5 main-deck card, so the stack is the
  natural carrier shape.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog / rules | 2 | Every printed field and both Q&A ids traced to the catalog and the KB; the NBSP discrepancy is reported, not edited. |
| IR trace | 2 | Each clause mapped to a compiled action, with the replacement, cost, target filters, `sameTarget`, duration and inherited flag all read against the engine code that consumes them. |
| Behaviour | 2 | All three printed clauses proved through public intents and the real turn loop, with positives, negatives, exact endpoints (memory, zones, suspended state, keyword presence) and duration expiry. No injected timing. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Colour and text discriminators proved against a mixed board (purple text-carrier, inert purple, red); the inherited clause proved on a real digivolution stack deleted by battle, with a non-purple Tamer negative; the cross-card Q5131 batch tested against EX10-047 and EX10-065. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

### EX10-049 — SkullSatamon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-049.test.ts`: Blocker, both mill/delete timings, threshold/post-mill ceiling, inherited replacement versus Security Attack and reset.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`, base `46810fe1e`.

#### Card summary

Purple Lv.5 Ultimate, Virus, [Undead]/[Fallen Angel], 8000 DP, play cost 8,
digivolve from a Purple Lv.4 for 3. Rarity U, max 4 per deck. No Security effect.

Printed clauses:

| # | Clause |
|---|---|
| C1 | ＜Blocker＞ |
| C2 | [When Digivolving] [On Deletion] If your opponent has 10 or fewer cards in their trash, trash the top 3 cards of both players' decks. |
| C3 | Then, delete 1 of your opponent's level 3 or lower Digimon. |
| C4 | If your opponent has 10 or more cards in their trash, add 2 to this effect's level maximum. |
| C5 | Inherited: [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] This Digimon gains ＜Security A. +1＞ for the turn. |
| C6 | Inherited: If your opponent has 10 or fewer cards in their trash, instead trash the top 2 cards of both players' decks. |

#### Rules basis

- **Q5395** — the part after "then" is processed even when the first "if" is not met.
  C3 must run when the opponent has more than 10 cards in their trash and no mill happened.
- **Q5132** — "instead" replaces the standard processing. C6 and C5 are mutually
  exclusive, not additive.
- **Comprehensive §15-6-2** — "Different processes in the same effect don't have to meet
  the processing conditions for other processes." A processing condition is read when its
  own process is performed. So C4's threshold is read *after* C2's mill has already put 3
  more cards into the opponent's trash. That is why 8 cards in trash still reaches the
  raised maximum (8 + 3 = 11).
- §15-6-3 is not engaged: C2 is not the effect's only process, so the effect still
  activates when the opponent's trash is above 10.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | "＜Blocker＞ into a bigger attacker" — `declareBlock` intent redirects a real attack; `observe().hasKeyword(..., "Blocker")` after the digivolve too | `Static` effect, `keywords: [{ keyword: "Blocker" }]` |
| C2 | "[When Digivolving] at a low trash count" (both decks lose 3), "Q5395" (neither deck loses any), "＜Blocker＞ … [On Deletion]" (both decks lose 3 on deletion) | `TrashTopDeck { controller: "both", amount: 3, condition: zoneCount opponent trash lte 10 }` on `WhenDigivolving` and on `OnDeletion` |
| C3 | "[When Digivolving] at a low trash count" — opponent's Lv.3 dies, their Lv.5 survives | `ConditionalBranch.ifFalse -> Delete { filter: levelComparison lte 3, count 1 }` |
| C4 | "re-reads the trash after the mill (§15-6-2)" — 8 + 3 milled reaches 11, the Lv.5 dies; "Q5395" — at 11 the Lv.5 dies while the Lv.6 survives, proving the maximum is 5 and not 7 | `ConditionalBranch.condition: zoneCount opponent trash gte 10 -> Delete { levelComparison lte 5 }` |
| C5 | "Q5132 … above 10" — 2 security cards checked in one attack; "inherited [Once Per Turn]" — grant is exactly 1, holds at 1 on a second same-turn attack, and is re-applied to 1 next own turn | `WhenAttacking`, `isInherited`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `GainKeyword SecurityAttack +1 duration forTheTurn`, `condition: zoneCount opponent trash gt 10` |
| C6 | "Q5132 … at 10 or fewer" — both decks lose 2, `keywordAmount(SecurityAttack) === 0`, exactly 1 security card checked | `TrashTopDeck { controller: "both", amount: 2, condition: zoneCount opponent trash lte 10 }` |
| negative | "no legal target" — opponent has only a Lv.6 at 11 trash: nothing dies, no pending decision | mandatory `Delete` with an empty candidate set |

All behavioural tests drive the engine through public intents only: `digivolve`,
`attack`, `declareBlock`, `endPhase` and the real `startTurnLoop()`. No
`advance.fire` / `fireSubTrigger` / `fireTiming` remains in this file.

The one seam call left is `advance.verb.unsuspend` in the [Once Per Turn] test: no public
intent returns an attacker to unsuspended mid-turn, so it is the only way to make the *same
carrier* attack twice in one turn, which is what a per-turn gate has to refuse. The attack
itself is still the public intent, and the next-turn reset leg uses the real turn loop with
no seam call at all.

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file scored the card 10/10 on evidence that was entirely structural:

- three of its four behavioural tests fired the timing directly with
  `advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.WhenDigivolving | OnUseAttack, ...)`. Per the
  brief that earns no behavioural credit. Nothing proved the card actually digivolves, is
  actually deleted, or actually attacks.
- ＜Blocker＞ (C1) had no test at all — only an IR shape assertion.
- [On Deletion] (C2/C3/C4 on that timing) had no test at all — only the shape assertion.
- The [Once Per Turn] gate on the inherited effect (C5) had no test at all.
- No level boundary was proved in either direction: nothing showed a Lv.4 or Lv.6 surviving,
  so a wrong `levelComparison` value would have passed.
- The fixtures used `EX10-030` and `EX10-053` as opponent boards. Both carry their own
  effect text (EX10-053 prints ＜Rush＞, ＜Blocker＞, an [On Play] and an inherited
  [Once Per Turn] clause), which the brief's fixture rule excludes.
- `EX10-030` was used as the deletion target of a "level 5 or lower" clause while the test
  called it `level5`, but it is a Black/Yellow card — no colour or level boundary was
  actually being asserted.

No module defect was found. The IR is faithful to the printed text and to the three rules
sources above, so `EX10-049.ts` is unchanged and `effects.json` does not drift.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-049.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
-> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 10 passed (10)     [logs/EX10-049-vitest-2.log]

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
-> 1 error, in another lane's file:
   src/cards/EX10/EX10-048.test.ts(237,10): error TS2304: Cannot find name 'loop'.
   No error in EX10-049.ts or EX10-049.test.ts.        [logs/EX10-049-typecheck.log]

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-049.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-049.test.ts
-> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>   -> all matched files use the correct format
git diff --check                           -> clean
```

An earlier run (`logs/EX10-049-vitest-1.log`) failed 3 tests with
`{ ok: false, reason: "wrong-phase" }` on `declineBlock`. Cause: a block window only opens
when the defender controls a legal ＜Blocker＞, and those fixtures gave the opponent none.
Fixed in the test, not worked around: the attack now settles straight through into the
security check and the comment says why.

#### Retained reds

None. No `it.fails` / `it.skip` in this file.

#### Catalog notes

- The catalog `effectText` prints "＜Blocker＞" with a trailing space before the newline and
  the inherited text uses "＜Security A. +1＞" while the IR's `raw` string spells it
  "＜Security Attack +1＞". Cosmetic only — the compiled keyword is `SecurityAttack`.
- No functional discrepancy found between the catalog entry and the implementation.

#### Remaining gaps

- C4's "add 2 to this effect's level maximum" is read here as a level-5 ceiling applied at
  the moment the delete is processed, i.e. after C2's mill. That follows §15-6-2 and there
  is no ruling on this card contradicting it, but no ruling confirms it directly either.
  Under the alternative reading (both thresholds snapshotted at effect resolution start),
  the "re-reads the trash after the mill" test would be the one to flip. Flagged as an
  interpretation, not a defect.
- The [Once Per Turn] same-turn refusal needs `advance.verb.unsuspend` because no public
  intent unsuspends an attacker mid-turn. This is a seam affordance gap, not a card gap; it
  would be closed by a fixture card that unsuspends, or by a testkit intent for it.
