| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a named IR action, including both threshold conditions, the branch's two level maxima and the inherited OPT frequency. |
| Behaviour | 2 | 8 behavioural tests through public intents only: real digivolution, real block-and-deletion, real attacks, real turn loop; both threshold branches, both level boundaries, and an empty-target negative. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Proved on a real digivolution stack (Bakemon -> SkullSatamon, stack identity asserted) and as an inherited card under a real carrier across three turns of the production turn loop, including "for the turn" expiry. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

**Total** | **8/10**

### EX10-050 — Baalmon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-050.test.ts`: Wizard evolution, both public mill/grant routes/expiry, Q5133 source trash, exact Beelzemon name and inherited DP scaling.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Worker lane. Base 46810fe1e271215d173854b82513f4ce30508b01, branch audit-ex10-reaudit.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Purple/Black Digimon, Lv.5,
play cost 7, 7000 DP, evoCosts Purple Lv.4 / Black Lv.4 at 4 memory, form Ultimate,
attribute Free, types [Wizard] [Bagra Army], rarity C, max 4 per deck. No security text.
The catalog matches the official card; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[Wizard] trait: Cost 3` — alternate digivolve route.
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Trash the top 3 cards of your deck. Then, if you have 5
   or more cards in your trash, this Digimon gains ＜Reboot＞ and ＜Blocker＞ until your
   opponent's turn ends.`
3. `[On Deletion] If you have 10 or more cards in your trash, you may play 1 [Beelzemon]
   from your trash without paying the cost.`
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] For every 10 cards in your trash, this Digimon gets +1000 DP.`

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5133 | The deleted Digimon and its digivolution cards reach the trash before the `[On Deletion]` condition is checked, so 6 trash + a 4-card stack satisfies "10 or more". | Behavioural test `Q5133: the deleted stack itself carries the trash to 10 and Beelzemon is played`. Deletion is caused by a real attack into a bigger suspended Digimon, not injected timing. |

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-050` lists Q5133 as the only ruling for this card.
KB Q1231/Q1232 (quoted in `matching/definition.ts`) supplied the exact-name rule used
for the defect below.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[Wizard]: Cost 3` | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 4, traits: ["Wizard"], cost: 3, isAlternate: true }]` | `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[Wizard]: Cost 3 beats the printed Cost 4, ...` — ST10-10 Wizardmon base, `useAlternateCost: true` from memory 3 lands at 0; the same route with `useAlternateCost: false` needs memory 4; BT1-014 (Lv.4, Red, non-Wizard) is refused on both routes. |
| Mill top 3 | `TrashTopDeck { controller: "mine", amount: 3 }` on `OnPlay` and `WhenDigivolving` | `[On Play] played from hand: ...` asserts exact trash order, remaining deck, memory 8 -> 1, hand. The digivolve test asserts the same mill on the `WhenDigivolving` branch. |
| "then, if 5+ trash, gains ＜Reboot＞ and ＜Blocker＞" | two `GainKeyword` with `condition: zoneCount mine trash gte 5` | positive in both play and digivolve tests; boundary in `[On Play] boundary: 4 cards in the trash ... grants neither keyword`. |
| "until your opponent's turn ends" | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` (a real `EffectDurationRef`) | `the granted keywords survive the opponent's turn and expire when it ends` — real `startTurnLoop`, asserted present in the opponent's main phase and absent in my next main phase. |
| `[On Deletion]` 10+ trash, may play 1 [Beelzemon] free | `PlayWithoutCost { from: ["trash"], payCost: false, optional: true, condition: zoneCount mine trash gte 10, target.filter.nameOrTrait [{ tokens: ["Beelzemon"], match: "nameExact" }] }` | Q5133 test (positive); `[On Deletion] boundary: 9 cards in the trash ...` (negative, one short); `[On Deletion] is a 'may': declining ...` (`autoDeclineOptional`); `[Beelzemon] is an exact name ...` (BT19-074 Beelzemon: Blast Mode refused). |
| Inherited `+1000 DP per 10 trash` | `AllTurns`, `isInherited`, `ModifyDP { amount: 1000, duration: "permanent", scaling: { per: 10, unit: "trash", filter: { zone: "trash", controller: "mine" } } }` | `inherited: built through a real stack, ...` — ST10-10 -> EX10-050 (alternate route) -> BT2-111 Beelzemon by public `digivolve` intents; +2000 at 23 trash, and +3000 after 7 more cards enter the trash, proving the bonus is continuous rather than a snapshot. |

#### Defect found and fixed

`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-050.ts`, `[On Deletion]` target filter:
`match: "name"` -> `match: "nameExact"`.

`matchNameOrTrait` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:349`)
implements `"name"` as substring and `"nameExact"` as equality, with the code's own
comment citing KB Q1231/Q1232 ("Cerberusmon: Werewolf Mode" does not match
"Cerberusmon"). `[Beelzemon]` is a bracketed exact-name reference, so the shipped IR
also offered `Beelzemon: Blast Mode` (BT19-074, BT19-074/EX2-074/ST14-10) and
`Beelzemon (X Antibody)` (BT12-085) from the trash for free.

Mutation proof: reverting the single token to `"name"` and rerunning only the
exact-name test fails with the Blast Mode card on the battle area
(`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-050-mutation.log`).

#### Test coverage rewritten

The committed test proved the two triggered clauses only through
`advance(...).fireForPermanent` and the deletion only through
`advance(...).verb.deletePermanent` — injected timing, which the brief scores 0 for
behaviour. It also had no negative case, no optional-refusal case, no duration proof, no
digivolve route and no exact-name case, and its inherited test asserted a static board
rather than a stack built by intents.

The new file drives every clause through public intents: `playCard`, `digivolve`
(both routes plus two refusals), `attack` into a suspended larger Digimon for the
deletion, and `startTurnLoop` for the duration. One structural test is retained and
labelled as such: it fires `WhenDigivolving` directly on a seeded permanent to show the
two trigger branches share one clause body, and it earns no behavioural credit.

Fixtures: ST10-10 Wizardmon is a fully inert Lv.4 [Wizard] (no main, inherited or
security text); BT2-111 is the only [Beelzemon] with no `[On Play]` clause, so playing
it from the trash cannot open a decision; BT1-009/BT1-013/BT1-014 are the inert
main-deck Digimon; no Digi-Egg and no numeric `security: <n>` form is used.

```

  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
-> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 12 passed (12)      logs/EX10-050-vitest.log

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
-> 1 error, in another lane's file:
   src/cards/EX10/EX10-048.test.ts(237,10): error TS2304: Cannot find name 'loop'.
   No error in EX10-050.ts or EX10-050.test.ts.            logs/EX10-050-typecheck.log

pnpm exec oxlint <both files>   -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <both>  -> clean (formatted once, then re-verified)
git diff --check                -> clean
```

Mutation run (temporary, reverted):
`... vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-050.test.ts -t "exact name"` -> 1 failed, as
intended (`logs/EX10-050-mutation.log`).

#### Remaining gaps

- ＜Reboot＞ is proved as a granted keyword through `observe(...).hasKeyword`, not by
  observing an unsuspend during the opponent's unsuspend phase. The grant expires at the
  end of the opponent's turn, so the keyword can never reach the controller's own
  unsuspend phase; its only in-window use is combined with an opponent-turn ＜Blocker＞
  block. No behavioural refinement is possible inside the printed duration.
- ＜Blocker＞ is proved as granted, not exercised by a `declareBlock`. The block path is
  engine-shared and already proved by EX10-002; the card-specific fact is the grant and
  its condition.
- `effects.json` will drift for EX10-050 because the compiled IR changed. The coordinator
  owns the sync; the file was not touched.

No red tests retained. No engine seam gap found for this card.

#### Card summary

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Name | Mummymon |
| Colors | Purple, Black |
| Kind / Level / Form | Digimon, Lv.5, Ultimate |
| Play cost / DP | 6 / 6000 |
| Digivolution | Purple Lv.4 cost 4; Black Lv.4 cost 4 |

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play] By trashing 1 card in your hand, ＜De-Digivolve 1＞ 1 of your opponent's Digimon.`
2. `[On Deletion] You may play 1 Tamer card with [Myotismon] in its text from your trash without paying the cost. This effect can't play cards with the same name as any of your Tamers.`

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | How it is covered |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5134 | "[X] in its text" spans name, traits, effects, inherited effects, (Rule), digivolution / DNA / DigiXros / burst / App Fusion / Link / Assembly requirements. | Test "Q5134 [On Deletion] in battle…": EX10-065 Yukio Oikawa carries [Myotismon] **only inside its effect text** and is played; BT2-087 Kari Kamiya has it nowhere and is never played. The IR uses `match: "text"`, which `matchNameOrTrait` resolves as name ∪ traits ∪ (effect, inherited, security, link, link requirement, dual, option) text. |

Supporting rules read: comprehensive §15-7-1..5 (optional processing conditions, "by X, Y"),
§16 (＜De-Digivolve＞ / stack trashing), and `deDigivolve`'s `levelFloor = 3`.

Q5134 also names digivolution / DigiXros / Link requirements as part of "text". The catalog keeps
those requirements inside `effectText` (for example EX10-053's `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[Gammamon] in
name`), and `matchNameOrTrait` additionally reads `linkRequirement`, so the ruling's full reach is
covered by the fields the matcher joins. No Tamer in the catalog is *named* Myotismon, so the
name half of the union cannot be exercised on this card; it is proved by the existing
`matchNameOrTrait` unit coverage instead.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[On Play]` trigger | `trigger: "OnPlay"` | "[On Play] from the hand…" — public `playCard` intent, memory 10 -> 4 |
| "By trashing 1 card in your hand" (optional processing condition) | `cost: { kind: "trash", target: { filter: { zone: "hand", controller: "mine" }, count: 1 } }`, `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true` | "[On Play] from the hand…" (exactly 1 named hand card trashed); "CR 15-7-2: declining…" (nothing trashed, no demotion); "[On Play] with an empty hand…" (no payable cost, no demotion) |
| "＜De-Digivolve 1＞ 1 of your opponent's Digimon" | `kind: "DeDigivolve"`, `amount: 1`, `target.filter { controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon"] }`, `count: 1` | "[On Play] from the hand…" — the opposing Lv.4 top goes to its owner's trash, the Lv.3 source becomes the top, `currentDP` recomputes to 3000, stack empty |
| `[On Deletion]` trigger | `trigger: "OnDeletion"` | Every deletion test fires it through a real losing battle (6000 DP attacker into a suspended 20000 DP wall), never through injected timing |
| "You may play 1 Tamer card … from your trash without paying the cost" | `kind: "PlayWithoutCost"`, `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: false`, `optional: true`, `filter.kind: ["Tamer"]` | "Q5134 [On Deletion] in battle…" (played, memory unchanged); "'You may': declining…" (stays in trash) |
| "with [Myotismon] in its text" | `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Myotismon"], match: "text" }]` | "Q5134 …" positive (EX10-065) and "[On Deletion] negative…" (BT2-087) |
| "can't play cards with the same name as any of your Tamers" | `excludeSameNameAsOwnTamers: true` | "Q5134 …": BT16-089 is in the trash and also on the board as a Tamer, so the trashed copy is skipped and EX10-065 is chosen instead |
| Digivolution requirements (Purple Lv.4 / Black Lv.4, cost 4) | catalog `evoCosts` | "evolution routes…" (Black route via BT10-062, cost 4, off-color Red Lv.4 BT1-014 refused) and "evolution stack…" (Purple route via BT4-080, stack identity, digivolution draw, then `[On Deletion]` from the stack) |

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-051.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 10 passed (10)
     log: docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-051-vitest.log

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck   -> clean (exit 0)
     log: docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-051-typecheck.log
pnpm exec oxlint  <module> <test>    -> no findings
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <module> <test> -> correctly formatted
git diff --check                     -> clean (exit 0)
```

Fails-when-reverted proof: with `effects: []` in the module, 4 of the 10 tests fail
(the IR-shape test plus all three behavioural clause tests). Log:
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-051-fails-when-reverted.log`. The module was restored
byte-for-byte afterwards (`diff` reports the files identical).

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file proved both printed clauses only through injected timing —
`advance(s.engine).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)` and
`advance(s.engine).verb.deletePermanent(…)`. Neither earns behavioural credit under this
run's rules, so the prior 10/10 claim rested on structural evidence for every clause.
It also had no negative for the `[Myotismon] in its text` filter, no "you may" refusal for
`[On Deletion]`, no empty-hand path for the optional cost, no memory assertion proving the
Tamer was played *without paying the cost*, and no evolution-route coverage at all.
Its `[On Play]` fixture used EX10-053 / EX10-042 as the opposing stack; both carry their own
`[On Play]`/`[When Digivolving]` text, so the fixture was not inert.

#### Defects found

None in the module. The compiled IR matches both printed clauses and the engine implements
them correctly:

- `optional: true` + `abortOnDecline: true` is the right encoding for an optional processing
  condition (CR 15-7-2: declining the condition cancels everything after it).
- `match: "text"` is exactly Q5134's union in `matchNameOrTrait`
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts`).
- `excludeSameNameAsOwnTamers` filters against Tamers in the controller's battle area
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/play.ts:490`), which is what "any of your
  Tamers" means.

No test is retained red; no `it.fails` / `it.skip` in this lane.

#### Catalog discrepancy (reported, not edited)

`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` stores EX10-051's `effectText` with a
**non-breaking space (U+00A0) at offset 147**, between `[Myotismon]` and `in its text`.
Every other space in the field is a plain space. It does not change behaviour — the compiled
token is `Myotismon` and the phrase is parsed at authoring time — but it makes an exact-text
assertion surprising, and it suggests the scraped text for this card (and possibly its
siblings) carries stray NBSPs. The catalog test encodes it as ` ` with a comment rather
than silently normalizing it.

#### Remaining gaps

- No Tamer in the catalog is *named* Myotismon, so the "name" half of Q5134's union cannot be
  exercised through this card's own effect. Covered indirectly by `matchNameOrTrait`'s own
  unit tests.
- ＜De-Digivolve＞'s `levelFloor = 3` (a Lv.3 top is never demoted) is engine-level behaviour
  shared by every ＜De-Digivolve＞ card and is not re-proved here; the fixture uses a Lv.4 top
  over a Lv.3 source, which is the legal case for this card.
- The `[On Deletion]` clause is proved on deletion in battle. Deletion by an opponent's effect
  or by the rule sweep is the same trigger path and is not separately exercised.

#### Score

| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog / rules | 2 | Every catalog field asserted exactly, including the U+00A0 discrepancy; Q5134 and CR §15-7 read and applied |
| IR trace | 2 | Both clauses mapped to the compiled IR, and each IR key followed into the engine (`matchNameOrTrait`, `excludeSameNameAsOwnTamers`, `deDigivolve`/`peelStackTops`) |
| Behavioural proof | 2 | Every clause driven from public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`); positive, negative, decline and unpayable-cost paths; exact zone, memory, DP, hand and stack endpoints after settle; fails when the IR is reverted |
| Peer / stack proof | 2 | Both printed digivolution routes exercised (Purple and Black, cost 4, source identity in `stack`, digivolution draw) with an off-color illegal-source refusal; `[On Deletion]` proved from a real evolution stack; a mixed trash pool of three Tamers separates text-match, same-name exclusion and non-match |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

### EX10-052 — Lucemon: Chaos Mode

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-052.test.ts`: Clause cost, both evolution routes, attack, departure/recovery/decline, Q5135/Q6039 and once-per-turn reset.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Card: EX10-052, Lv.5 Purple/Yellow Digimon, Ultimate, Virus, [Demon Lord]/[Seven Great
Demon Lords], play cost 13, DP 13000, digivolve 7 from a Purple or Yellow Lv.4.
Not an ACE: the catalog carries no `isAce` and no `overflowMemory`, so there is no
＜Blast Digivolve＞ or Overflow clause to prove.

#### Printed clauses

| Id | Printed text |
| --- | --- |
| C1 | `[Digivolve] [Lucemon]: Cost 5` |
| C2 | `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] By trashing 1 card in your hand, your opponent may delete 1 of their Digimon or Tamers. If this effect didn't delete, ＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞` |
| C3 | `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon would leave the battle area, your opponent may delete 1 of their Digimon or Tamers. If this effect didn't delete, it doesn't leave.` |

The catalog text matches the official list. No discrepancy found.

#### Q&A coverage

| Q&A | Substance | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5135 | The opponent picks a deletion target, the deletion does not happen, so "this effect didn't delete" holds and the Lucemon does not leave | Two tests: the opponent controls nothing to delete, and the opponent declines the deletion. Both are driven by a real lost battle. The Q&A's own mechanism (＜Armor Purge＞ on P-153 MagnaGarurumon stopping the chosen deletion) is not reproduced: it needs a second card's printed prevention, which is outside this lane's allowed files. The engine-visible fact under test is identical: the replacement's Delete produced no deletion. |
| Q6039 | The opponent does delete one of their own Digimon, so the Lucemon is deleted all the same | `Q6039: leaves the battle area when the opponent's deletion did delete` (real battle, opponent trashes their own fodder Digimon, Lucemon goes to the trash). The memory the asker gains belongs to the OTHER card in that ruling, not to EX10-052; EX10-052 has no memory clause. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | `takes the [Lucemon] Cost 5 route from a Lv.3 named Lucemon` (BT4-115, memory 5 -> 0, stack identity asserted) plus `refuses the Cost 5 route from ...` for a Purple Lv.4 and a Purple Lv.3 that are not named Lucemon | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ names: ["Lucemon"], cost: 5, isAlternate: true }]` |
| C2 (WhenDigivolving) | `digivolves for 7 from a Purple Lv.4 and recovers when the opponent has nothing to delete`; `does not recover when the opponent deletes one of their own Digimon`; `keeps the trigger silent when the opponent declines the deletion but still recovers` | `WhenDigivolving` -> `Delete` (controller `opponent`, optional, `cost: trash 1 from hand`, `allowCostWithoutTarget`) then `SecurityManipulation addTop` from `deck` under `ifThisEffectDidNotDelete` |
| C2 (WhenAttacking) | `fires on a declared attack and recovers when the opponent has nothing to delete` (public `attack` intent); `still skips the deletion offer when the hand cost cannot be paid` | same action pair under `WhenAttacking` |
| C3 | `Q5135: ... when the opponent has nothing to delete`; `Q5135: ... when the opponent declines`; `Q6039: leaves ... when the opponent's deletion did delete`; `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]: the per-turn use resets on the next own turn` | `AllTurns` / `frequency: OncePerTurn` -> `Replacement` `wouldLeavePlay`, `optional: false`, `sourceFilter.isSelfRef`, inner `Delete` (opponent, optional) then `Prevent` `leavePlay` under `ifThisEffectDidNotDelete` |

All C2 and C3 proofs run through public intents (`digivolve`, `attack`, `declareBlock`-free
security battles, `startTurnLoop`). The only injected-timing test left is the same-turn
second departure, marked structural below.

#### Defects fixed in this lane

1. **Declining the opponent's deletion suppressed ＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞.** The `Delete`
   action on both triggers carried `abortOnDecline: true`, so a declined prompt aborted the
   whole clause and the following `ifThisEffectDidNotDelete` recovery never ran. Printed text
   says a decline is exactly the "didn't delete" case. Removed on both triggers. The peer with
   the same wording, `apps/api/src/cards/EX6/EX6-054.ts`, has never carried it.
   Evidence: `logs/EX10-052-run1.log` (red before), `logs/EX10-052-final.log` (green after).
2. **The leave replacement asked its controller for permission.** Without `optional: false`
   on the `Replacement` action, `preventCheck` in
   `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/replacement.ts` prompts "Prevent leaving
   the battle area?" and a decline lets the removal through. EX10-052's printed "may" belongs
   to the opponent's deletion, not to the prevention, so the prevention is mandatory.
   Added `optional: false` to the `Replacement` (the prompt gate reads the replacement action,
   not the nested `Prevent`; the nested `optional: false` alone did not help — see
   `logs/EX10-052-run5.log`). Evidence: `logs/EX10-052-run5.log` (red), `run6` (green).

#### Retained red

`it.fails("skips the whole clause when the hand cost cannot be paid")`.

- Seam: `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/runAction.ts` (`Action.cost`) and
  `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/effect.ts` (`runEffect`). `Action.cost` gates only
  the action carrying it; a later action in the same `CardEffect` still resolves.
  `CardEffect` (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/card.ts`) has `condition` but no whole-clause
  `cost`, so the IR cannot express "if the cost is unpayable, none of this happens".
- Expected with an empty hand: no deletion offer AND no ＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞
  (comprehensive rules on activation costs — an unpayable cost stops the activation).
- Actual: the deletion offer is correctly skipped, but the card still recovers 1 card for free.
- The alternative IR shape (a leading mandatory `Trash` action, as `BT18/BT18-034.ts` compiles
  the identical wording) is strictly worse: it lets the opponent delete without any trash at
  all (`logs/EX10-052-run3.log`). The current shape is the closer of the two.
- A companion green test, `still skips the deletion offer when the hand cost cannot be paid`,
  pins the half of that behaviour that is already right.

#### Structural-only test

`[Once Per Turn]: the second departure in the same turn is not prevented (structural)` uses
`advance().verb.deletePermanent`. Two departures of the SAME permanent inside one turn cannot
be produced by public intents here: the only public leave window this card can survive is one
where the opponent controls no Digimon or Tamer (otherwise they simply delete one and the
Lucemon leaves), which rules out any opponent attack, and a Digimon may attack only once per
turn. No behavioural credit is claimed for it. The per-turn RESET is proved publicly instead,
across a real turn loop, in `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]: the per-turn use resets on the next
own turn`.

#### Commands

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-052.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 15 passed | 1 expected fail (16)   [logs/EX10-052-final.log]
pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck                 -> clean            [logs/EX10-052-typecheck.log]
pnpm exec oxlint <both files>                      -> no findings
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <both files>               -> correct format
git diff --check                                   -> clean
```

#### Remaining gaps

- The unpayable-cost clause gate (retained red above).
- Q5135's exact ＜Armor Purge＞ interaction needs P-153 on the board; a second card's module is
  outside this lane.
- No test plays EX10-052 from hand for 13 memory; the card has no `[On Play]` clause, so
  nothing printed depends on that route.
- Compiling the module changed its IR, so `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` is now
  stale for EX10-052. The coordinator owns the sync.

#### Score

| --- | --- | --- |

| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a named IR node; two IR defects found and fixed; a third gap traced to the IR schema itself and documented. |
| Behavior | 2 | Both triggers, both branches of "didn't delete", the leave replacement in three outcomes, the alternate route with two negatives and the per-turn reset all run through public intents. Only the same-turn second departure is structural, and it claims no credit. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Real evolution stacks on both routes with source identity, cost and bonus draw asserted; an illegal-source negative on each level; the leave replacement proved in a battle and in a security battle across a real turn loop; compared against the same-wording peers EX6-054 and BT18-034. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |

**Total** | **8/10**

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|--------|

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Where proved |
|----|--------|--------------|
| Q5136 | The `[On Play]/[When Digivolving]` deletion resolves even when no card is placed as a digivolution card. | Two tests: the optional placement DECLINED with a legal candidate in trash, and an empty candidate set. In both the opponent's Digimon is still deleted and the stack stays empty. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Test |
|--------|----|------|
| C1 | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 4, names: ["Gammamon"], cost: 5, isAlternate: true }]` | "over EX10-042 GulusGammamon on the Lv.4 w/[Gammamon] route" (positive, `alternateRequirementIndex: 0`, 6 - 5 = 1 memory, bonus draw asserted) and "refuses an illegal digivolution source" (green Lv.4; Lv.3 [Gammamon] on the alternate route). `names` is the SUBSTRING gate (`cardData.ts`, `req.names` -> `name.includes(n)`), which is what "w/[Gammamon] in name" prints — so GulusGammamon qualifies. Verified in the source, not assumed. |
| C2 | `Static` + `keywords: [Rush]` | Keyword observation, plus a real ＜Rush＞ attack: the card is played from hand and attacks the same turn (summoning sickness would otherwise refuse it). |
| C3 | `Static` + `keywords: [Blocker]` | `declareBlock` on the opponent's turn through the production turn loop: the attack is redirected, the 3000 DP attacker dies, security is untouched. |
| C4 | `PlaceUnder` `{ count: 5, upTo: true, distinctNames: true, from: ["trash"], position: "bottom", optional: true }` on both `OnPlay` and `WhenDigivolving` | `[On Play]` test places all five differently named cards and leaves the sixth same-named card and the non-[Gammamon] card in trash. The `position: "bottom"` claim is proved exactly on the digivolve route, where the base already had two digivolution cards: the placed pair sits below both of them and below the base card. |
| C5 | `Delete` `{ controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon"], dp: { op: "lte", relativeToSource: true }, count: 1 }`, no `optional` | Positive: a 10000 DP Digimon is deleted while an 11000 DP one survives. Strict negative: with ONLY an 11000 DP Digimon on the opposing board, the placement still resolves and nothing is deleted. Q5136 tests prove the delete is not gated on the placement. |
| C6 | `EndOfYourTurn` / `OncePerTurn` / `Attack { withoutSuspending: true, optional: true, condition: selfDigivolutionCountAtLeast 5 }` | Real turn loop. With 5 digivolution cards the end-of-turn prompt appears, the attack checks 1 security and the Digimon stays UNSUSPENDED. With 4 cards no prompt and no security check. Not offered at the opponent's end of turn; offered again on the next own turn (once-per-turn reset). |
| C7 | `YourTurn` inherited `SubTrigger onDeletionOf { controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon"] } -> GainMemory 1`, `OncePerTurn` | Real carrier stack: EX10-053 under a battle-area Digimon. Two REAL battle deletions of opposing Digimon in the same turn; memory goes up by exactly 1. |

#### Commands and results

```

  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 12 passed (12)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        -> clean
pnpm exec oxlint <module> <test>          -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <module> <test>   -> clean (test file reformatted once)

```

Log: `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-053-vitest.log`,
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-053-typecheck.log`.

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test claimed 10/10 but proved almost nothing behaviourally:

1. `[On Play]` and `[When Digivolving]` were fired only through `advance().fireForPermanent`
   (injected timing). Neither the real `playCard` route nor any digivolve route was exercised,
   so C1 was never tested at all — no positive route, no cost, no bonus draw, no illegal-source
   negative.
2. `＜Rush＞` and `＜Blocker＞` were asserted only as keyword flags. Neither was played.
3. The end-of-turn clause was fired with `advance().fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnEndTurn, ...)`,
   so it never proved the clause reaches a real end of turn, never proved it is limited to the
   OWNER's turn, and never proved the once-per-turn reset.
4. The inherited memory watcher used `advance().verb.deletePermanent(...)` — an injected
   deletion, not a real one.
5. The DP bound was proved only by a biased `preferInstanceIds` pick; there was no case where
   the ONLY opposing Digimon is over the bound.
6. Fixtures used `BT1-010` (an `[On Play]` card) as digivolution-stack filler and
   `EX10-053`/`EX10-052` as opposing bodies, both of which carry their own effects.
   The new fixtures use the five differently named [Gammamon] cards whose OWN inherited text
   is empty (P-058, BT8-013, BT9-023, BT10-050, RB1-029) plus inert BT1-009/013/014.
7. Q5136 was covered only by the "empty trash" case, which is the trivial branch. The ruling is
   about declining the placement; that case is now covered too.

The module's IR needed no change — it was already faithful. Every gap was in the proof.

#### Remaining gaps

- **Order within the placed group.** The engine places the selected cards one at a time at the
  stack bottom, so the placed group ends up in reverse selection order and the player is never
  asked to order them. The printed text only fixes that they go to the BOTTOM (which is proved
  exactly), and the comprehensive rules let the player order simultaneously placed cards. This
  is a fidelity nit in the shared `PlaceUnder` primitive, not an EX10-053 defect, and it is not
  something a card-lane edit may fix. No test is red for it.
- **C6 same-turn once-per-turn refusal is structurally unprovable.** A turn has exactly one
  "end of your turn", so no sequence of public intents can offer this clause twice in one turn.
  The reset is proved instead across three consecutive turns through the real turn loop. No
  structural `advance.fire` substitute was added.
- No engine seam is missing and no test is retained red.

| Behavior | 2 | All seven clauses proved through public intents and the production turn loop; positive and negative DP bound; declined-optional and empty-set Q5136 branches; end-of-turn positive, negative and reset. No injected timing anywhere in the file. |

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Where it is proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5137 | "`[X]` in its text" spans name, traits, effects, inherited effects, (Rule), and every requirement line — not just the name. | Two behavioural tests: the cost is paid with EX10-047 Arukenimon, whose only `[Myotismon]` is in its **effect text**; and the whole `[Trash] [Main]` effect is not offered when the only Lv.5 Digimon on board is BT10-079 Sandiramon (no `[Myotismon]` anywhere). Also asserted on the IR: `match: "text"`, not `"name"`. |
| Q5138 | The suspend half and the "can't unsuspend" half may land on **different** cards. | Behavioural test: two standing opposing Digimon are suspended while two already-suspended opposing Tamers are the ones restricted, then seat 1's real unsuspend phase wakes the first pair and leaves the second pair suspended. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test (all through public intents) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Trash] [Main]` cost + reduced play | `Main` effect, `isFromTrash: true`, `PlayWithoutCost` `{ from: ["trash"], payCost: true, reduceCostBy: 7 }` with `cost.kind: "deleteOwn"`, filter `{ controller: "mine", kind: ["Digimon"], levels: [5], nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Myotismon"], match: "text" }] }` | "Q5137 pays with a level 5 Digimon whose only `[Myotismon]` is in its effect text": `activateEffect` on the trashed card inside a real turn loop; memory 5 -> 0 (`memoryChanged … reason: "playCard"`, i.e. 12 - 7); Arukenimon in trash, Sandiramon untouched; the new permanent has an empty stack and 12000 DP. |
| same, negative | same | "Q5137 refuses the trash route …": with only Sandiramon on board the effect is **not listed** on the trashed card, and activating it by key is refused with memory unchanged. |
| `[On Play]` suspend 2 | `OnPlay` `Suspend { count: 2, optional: true }`, filter `{ controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon","Tamer"] }` | "Q5138 suspends 2 and restricts 2 DIFFERENT cards …": `playCard` from hand for 12 memory in a real turn loop; both chosen opposing Digimon end suspended. |
| `[On Play]` "Then, 2 … can't unsuspend until their turn ends" | `OnPlay` `Restrict { restriction: "unsuspend", count: 2, duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd" }` with its **own** target | same test: the two restricted (already-suspended) Tamers survive seat 1's unsuspend phase while the unrestricted pair wakes; two turns later the restriction is gone and they unsuspend. Duration expiry is proved through the turn loop, not `ledger.sweep`. |
| `[When Digivolving]`, both halves | `WhenDigivolving` effect, identical actions | "[When Digivolving] refuses a Lv.4 source and fires on the public Lv.5 Green evolution route": a Lv.4 source is refused (memory unchanged), then a legal Green Lv.5 digivolve fires both halves. Plus "digivolves on the Lv.5 Purple route …" for the second printed route. |
| evolution routes / stack identity | `evoCosts` `[{Purple,5,4},{Green,5,4}]` | Both digivolve tests assert `Permanent.stack` holds exactly the Lv.5 source instance, `currentDP` 12000, memory 4 -> 0, and the digivolution bonus draw. |
| optional "may" declined | `optional: true` on `Suspend` only | "declining the optional suspend …": nothing is suspended, but the restriction after "Then," still applies. See "Rules note" below. |
| `[On Deletion]` delete 1 opposing suspended Digimon | `OnDeletion` `Delete { count: 1, filter: { controller: "opponent", suspended: true, kind: ["Digimon"] } }` | "[On Deletion] deletes the attacker that suspended itself, and nothing standing": seat 1 really attacks a 1000 DP VenomMyotismon; the attacker suspends itself to attack, wins the battle, and is then deleted by the trigger. A standing opposing Digimon and a suspended opposing **Tamer** both survive, proving the `suspended` and `kind: ["Digimon"]` halves of the filter. |

No test in this file uses injected timing. `advance.fire` / `fireForPermanent` /
`fireSubTrigger` / `verb.deletePermanent` are gone; every trigger is reached through
`activateEffect`, `playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, and the real turn loop
(`startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase` + `endMainPhaseIfOpen`).

##### Rules note: the "Then," half after a declined "may"

CR 15-7-2 makes the text after "Then," conditional only when the preceding text is an
*optional processing condition* ("By X, Y"). "You may suspend 2 …" is optional **processing**,
not a processing condition, and CR 15-6-2's worked example has exactly this shape ("you may
play 1 [Kiriha Aonuma] … Then, ＜Save＞" — ＜Save＞ still processes). The implementation
therefore applies the restriction even when the suspend is declined, and the test records
that as the intended reading. No Q&A on this card settles it directly; flagged as the one
remaining interpretation risk.

#### Defects found

1. **Test suite rewritten (the real finding).** The committed test proved the `[On Play]`
   clause with `advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)` and both `[On Deletion]`
   cases with `advance(...).verb.deletePermanent(…)` — injected timing, which earns no
   behavioural credit. It also reached outside the Test Seam with
   `(s.engine as unknown as { mainPhase: … })` and called `s.engine.recomputeContinuousEffects()`
   directly. It never proved: the "in its text" filter (no negative candidate at all), Q5138's
   independence (it asserted only *counts* of 2 and 2, which a `sameTarget` implementation
   would also satisfy), that the unsuspend restriction actually blocks an unsuspend phase, the
   duration boundary, or either evolution route. Its `[On Deletion]` fixtures used EX10-040 /
   EX10-043 / EX10-064 / EX10-065, all of which carry live triggers (`[Start of Your Main
   Phase]`, an `[All Turns]` `[Myotismon]` watcher), so they were not neutral fixtures.
2. **Catalog discrepancy (reported, not edited).** `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`
   stores a **non-breaking space (U+00A0)** between `[Myotismon]` and `in its text` in
   EX10-054's `effectText`. Harmless for matching (the compiled token is `Myotismon`), but it
   makes exact-text comparison fail confusingly; the test documents it explicitly.

No defect was found in `EX10-054.ts` itself. Its IR is unchanged by this lane.

#### Verification that the tests are not vacuous

Mutation check: flipping the module's `match: "text"` to `match: "name"` fails two tests —
the IR assertion and the Q5137 behavioural test (Arukenimon stops being a legal cost).
Log: `logs/EX10-054-mutation-match-name.log`. The module was restored immediately
(`git status` shows `EX10-054.ts` unmodified).

#### Remaining gaps and seams for the coordinator

- **Engine seam, `matchNameOrTrait` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts`,
  ~line 337).** The `match: "text"` blob is built from `effectText`, `inheritedEffectText`,
  `securityEffectText`, `linkEffect`, `linkRequirement`, `dualEffect`, `optionEffect`, plus
  name and traits. Q5137 also lists **DNA digivolution, DigiXros, burst digivolve, App Fusion
  and Assembly requirements**. Those are stored structurally, not in any of those strings, so a
  card whose only `[Myotismon]` mention lives in a structured requirement field would be
  missed. In today's catalog `[Digivolve] …` headers are inlined into `effectText`, so no
  current card exposes the gap; it is latent. Not fixed here (outside this lane's allowed
  files). Note that `definitionMatches`'s separate `textContains` path has the same shape.
- **No inert Tamer exists in the catalog.** Every Tamer carries printed text, so the opposing
  Tamer fixtures (ST1-12, ST3-12) were picked for clauses that cannot fire in these boards
  (`[Your Turn]` on the non-turn seat; a Security-Digimon DP static that is never checked).
  Worth a coordinator note if a future brief wants a strictly inert Tamer fixture.
- Not covered, and not applicable to this card: DNA, DigiXros, App Fusion, ACE / Blast
  Digivolve, ＜Save＞, Link, Digi-Egg routes, Security text, inherited text, once-per-turn gates.
- No test is retained red; there is no `it.skip` / `it.fails` in the file.

### EX10-055 — Tactimon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-055.test.ts`: Play/digivolve sacrifice-level deletion, replacement recipients/two-source cost, quota/reset and Bagra Army DigiXros trait boundaries.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`, base `46810fe1e`.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Lv.6 Purple/Black Digimon, Mega,
Data, types `["Wizard", "Bagra Army"]`, play cost 12, DP 12000, evolution costs
Purple Lv.5 / Black Lv.5 for 4, rarity R, max 4 per deck. No inherited text, no security text.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] You may choose 1 of your Digimon. Delete the chosen Digimon
   and 1 of your opponent's Digimon with as high or lower a level as it.`
2. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When any of your [Bagra Army] trait Digimon would leave the
   battle area by effects, by trashing any 2 of this Digimon's digivolution cards, they don't leave.`
3. `[DigiXros -2] 2 Digimon cards w/[Bagra Army] trait`

The catalog text matches the official list. No discrepancy found.

#### Rulings

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-055` returns four Q&A, all dated 2025-09-05.

| Q&A | Substance | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5139 | The replacement prevents ALL simultaneously departing [Bagra Army] Digimon; no choice is made | "Q5139 prevents every simultaneous [Bagra Army] departure for one 2-card payment" (structural simultaneity, see gaps) |
| Q5140 | Trashing only 1 digivolution card cannot satisfy the "by" condition | "Q5140 cannot pay the replacement with only 1 digivolution card" |
| Q5141 | Choosing this card itself for clause 1 still lets the [All Turns] effect activate | "[On Play] via DigiXros: Q5141 keeps the self-chosen Tactimon by trashing its 2 materials" |
| Q5169 | A card that GAINED Tactimon's [All Turns] effect (via EX10-059) triggers it and prevents its own departure | Substance covered: the replacement is proved firing for the very Digimon that is leaving. The grant path itself belongs to EX10-059 and is out of this lane's allowed files. |

Rule used for the cost: comprehensive §7-2-2-1 — "the play cost is reduced by the amount shown
in the DigiXros requirements **for each card placed**". So `-2` is per material, not a flat total.
§7-2-2-4 allows placing any number of the specified cards (at least 1); §7-2-2-5 caps the recipe.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR (`EX10-055.ts`) | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1, `[On Play]` | `trigger: "OnPlay"`, `optional: true`, `SelectBind{mine Digimon, count 1, bindAs "A"}` + `Delete{fromSelectionRef "A"}` + `Delete{opponent Digimon, relativeTo level lte A}` | "[On Play] via DigiXros: Q5141 …" — real `playCard` with a DigiXros declaration |
| 1, `[When Digivolving]` | same actions under `trigger: "WhenDigivolving"` | "[When Digivolving] deletes the chosen own Digimon and only an opponent Digimon at or below its level" — real `digivolve` intent from an inert Black Lv.5 |
| 1, level boundary | `relativeTo: { attr: "level", op: "lte", selectionRef: "A" }` | same test: a Lv.4 sacrifice leaves the opponent's Lv.5 EX10-031 untouched and deletes only their Lv.3 |
| 2, trait scope + "all" | `Replacement{event wouldLeavePlay, mode prevent, affectsAll, count "all", nameOrTrait [{tokens ["Bagra Army"], match "trait"}]}` | "Q5139 …" (all/simultaneous, structural) and "does not protect an own Digimon without the [Bagra Army] trait" (negative, public `digivolve`) |
| 2, `by effects` only | `leaveCause: "byEffect"` | covered indirectly: every prevention in this file is driven by an effect deletion |
| 2, cost | `cost: { kind: "trash", target: { filter: { isSelfRef: true, zone: "digivolutionCards" }, count: 2 } }` | Q5141 test (2 trashed, Tactimon stays) and Q5140 test (1 card, no prevention) |
| 2, `[Once Per Turn]` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` | "the opponent's two Option plays in one turn…" — **public intents**: two `playCard` intents by seat 1 under the real turn loop, plus the next-turn reset. The older injected-timing test is kept as a structural companion |
| 3, recipe + cap | `digiXrosRequirement: [{ materials: [{ traits: ["Bagra Army"] }], count: 2, costReduction: 2, maxMaterials: 2 }]` | "refuses a third material even when it matches the trait" and "refuses a non-[Bagra Army] material" |
| 3, `-2` per material | engine `validateDigiXros` per-material reduction | "DigiXroses from hand …" (2 materials, 12 -> 8) and "DigiXroses with a single material for exactly 2 less" (1 material, 12 -> 10). A flat `-2` reading would leave memory at 4 in the second case |
| 3, material sources | engine `resolveMaterial` (hand / battle area) | hand test above; "takes a [Bagra Army] material from the battle area, removing that permanent whole" (the field permanent is consumed whole, its own digivolution card shed to trash per §7-2-2-7). Trash is NOT a legal source for this card (no trash allowance in its IR and no expander Tamer), so no trash case exists |

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file proved the [On Play] clause with `advance.fireForPermanent` and both
replacement cases with `advance.verb.deletePermanent` — injected timing, which earns no
behavioural credit. It also had no non-matching-material refusal (its only DigiXros negative
used a third **matching** [Bagra Army] card, so it proved the cap, not the trait gate), no
battle-area material source, no single-material case pinning the reduction as per-material,
no real `digivolve` route for `[When Digivolving]`, and no coverage of Q5141 or Q5169.

Everything above is now proved through `playCard` / `digivolve` intents.

[When Digivolving] sacrifice and the delete never happened — correctly, because SkullKnightmon
is itself [Bagra Army] and Tactimon's own replacement saved it. The final fixture uses the
inert non-[Bagra Army] BT1-014 so the sacrifice clause is asserted in isolation.

#### Remaining gaps

1. ~~`[Once Per Turn]` is structural only.~~ **Closed.** The gate is now proved through public
   intents (see "Round 3" below).
2. **Q5139's simultaneity is structural.** The "all, without choosing" reading needs two of my
   Digimon leaving at the same instant; the injected deletion is the only way to arrange it here.
   The single-target prevention and its payment are proved publicly.
3. **Q5169's grant path is out of scope.** Testing it needs EX10-059's "gains this [All Turns]"
   grant, which is another lane's file.
4. **Latent engine seam (not a defect today).** `validateDigiXros`
   (`apps/api/src/engine/actions/digiXros.ts`) computes
   `perMaterialReduction = requirement.count === "∞" ? (requirement.costReduction ?? 1) : requirement.count`,
   so for a numeric `count` the `costReduction` field is **ignored** and `count` doubles as the
   per-material reduction. EX10-055 (and EX10-056 / EX10-058) happen to have `count === costReduction === 2`,
   so the result is right by coincidence. I checked `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json`:
   no card currently has a numeric `count` differing from its `costReduction`, so nothing is
   wrong right now — but a future `[DigiXros -1] 2 cards …` recipe would silently overcharge or
   undercharge. Engine file, not mine to change.

#### Round 3: the `[Once Per Turn]` gate through public intents

##### Catalog search for a second public by-effect departure

Filter over `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: `kinds` contains `Option`,
`effectText` contains `[Main]` and `Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon`, `playCost <= 4`.
That returns 16 cards. Candidates and why they were or were not used:

| Card | Cost / color | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **BT2-091 Volcanic Flare** | 3 / Red | **Chosen.** Single clause, no rider: "Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 4000 DP or less". Both [Bagra Army] allies are exactly 4000 DP, so both are legal targets on every play |
| BT8-096, EX2-067 | 3 / Red | Same core clause but each carries a conditional second clause that would add noise |
| BT12-099, BT14-089, BT6-094, BT9-093, BT15-089 | 3–4 / Red | Riders (DP buff, extra deletion, security scaling, follow-up digivolve) |
| BT10-096, BT7-093, BT12-108 | 2–4 | Need a named/trait Digimon of the *player's* own to set the DP bound |
| BT3-098, EX1-067, BT4-110, BT26-101, ST22-08 | 3–4 | Wrong bound (13000+ DP), extra ＜Blocker＞ requirement, play-cost bound, or unrelated text |

No trash/security route was needed: three copies of BT2-091 fit inside `maxCountInDeck: 4`.

##### The proof

`"the opponent's two Option plays in one turn: the first departure is prevented, the second is
not, and the gate resets next turn"` runs the real turn loop
(`s.engine.startTurnLoop()` + `advance.waitForMainPhase` / `endMainPhaseIfOpen`) across four
turns. Seat 1 holds a Red source (BT1-013 Muchomon, an inert Lv.3 at 5000 DP — it satisfies the
Option's printed color requirement, checked by `GameEngine.printedColorRequirementMet`, and is
itself above that Option's own 4000 DP bound). Endpoints asserted:

| Step | Intent | Assertion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Opponent's turn opens | turn loop | `turnSeat === 1`; Tactimon's stack has 4 cards |
| 1st departure | seat 1 `playCard` BT2-091 | Both allies still on my board (`battleArea` length 3); stack 4 -> 2 (the 2-card payment was made) |
| 2nd departure, same turn | seat 1 `playCard` BT2-091 | `battleArea` length 2 — one ally left the battle area; stack still 2, so the payment was affordable and simply not taken. The gate, not the cost |
| Next own turn, then opponent's turn again | turn loop | `turnSeat === 1` |
| 3rd departure | seat 1 `playCard` BT2-091 | The survivor is still on my board; stack 2 -> 0. The gate reset and paid again |

Because the replacement is `affectsAll` with `count: "all"`, the assertion does not depend on
which of the two equal-DP allies the Option's selection picks: after the second play exactly one
ally is gone, whichever it was, and that survivor is the one protected on the next turn.

The earlier injected-timing test (`advance.verb.deletePermanent`) is retained unchanged as a
structural companion; every assertion in this file is kept.

The coordinator's rubric-ceiling policy was **not** needed — the gate is exercised twice in one
turn by public intents.

Q5139's simultaneity remains the one structural element |

### EX10-056 — Bagramon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-056.test.ts`: Public DigiXros/digivolve placement, security trash/reset, source shedding/order, Q5142/Q5148 and Bagra Army negatives.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple, Lv.6 Mega, [Demon Lord]/[Bagra Army], 13000 DP, play cost 13, EvoCost Purple Lv.5 / 5.

#### Printed clauses

1. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] You may place 1 of your opponent's Digimon as any of their other
   Digimon's bottom digivolution card or under any of their Tamers.`
2. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When any of your opponent's Digimon or Tamers digivolve or effects
   place cards under them, by trashing any 2 of this Digimon's digivolution cards, trash your
   opponent's top security card.`
3. `[DigiXros -2] 2 Digimon cards w/[Bagra Army] trait`

Catalog matches the official text. No discrepancy found.

#### Defects found and fixed

| # | Defect | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | The placement moved the victim's WHOLE stack under the host. Q5145 says the placed Digimon leaves the battle area and its own digivolution cards are trashed at the same time. | `shedOwnCards: true` on both `PlaceUnder` actions. |
| 2 | The digivolve watcher's `sourceFilter.kind` listed only `Digimon`. The text reads "Digimon **or Tamers** digivolve", and the engine's `tamerDigivolvedGate` (`actions/subTrigger.ts`) withholds a watcher that does not name Tamer. | `kind: ["Digimon", "Tamer"]`. |
| 3 | The placement watcher accepted ANY digivolution-card addition. The text restricts it to "**effects** place cards under them". | `byEffect: true` on the `onAddDigivolutionCards` `sourceFilter`. |

The prior audit's "two-material DigiXros limit" correction is confirmed correct: `maxMaterials: 2`
holds, and the per-material reduction is proved to be 2 (13 → 11 with one material, 13 → 9 with
two), matching comprehensive rules §7-2-2-1 ("reduced by the amount shown … for each card placed").

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DigiXros recipe, 2 hand materials, cost | "places two hand materials and pays 13 - 2 per material = 9 memory" | `digiXrosRequirement` |
| Per-material reduction | "reduces the cost per material: one material costs 11, not 9" | `count: 2` (per-material reduction) |
| Battle-area material source | "takes a material from the battle area, shedding that permanent's own digivolution cards" | engine §7-2-2-7 path |
| Non-matching material | "refuses a material without the [Bagra Army] trait, spending nothing" | `materials[0].traits` |
| Material cap | "rejects a third material without spending memory or moving cards" | `maxMaterials: 2` |
| `[On Play]` placement | "[On Play] via a real DigiXros …" (public `playCard` + `digiXros`) | `OnPlay` → `PlaceUnder` |
| `[When Digivolving]` placement | "[When Digivolving] via a real digivolve places under an opponent Tamer…" (public `digivolve` from BT10-081) | `WhenDigivolving` → `PlaceUnder` |
| `[All Turns]` digivolve branch + cost + OPT reset | "trashes the opponent's top security card … once per turn" (real turn loop) | `SubTrigger whenOneOfYoursDigivolves` |
| `[All Turns]` effect-placement branch | "fires when an opponent's EFFECT places a card under their Tamer" (opponent plays EX10-044) | `SubTrigger onAddDigivolutionCards`, `byEffect` |
| "by trashing any 2" all-or-nothing | "Q5142: 1 remaining digivolution card cannot pay …" | `cost.target.count: 2` |
| Opponent-only scope | "does not react to its own controller digivolving" | `sourceFilter.controller: "opponent"` |

#### Q&A coverage

| Q&A | Covered by |
| --- | --- |
| Q5142 (all-or-nothing 2-card cost) | "Q5142: 1 remaining digivolution card cannot pay the 'by trashing any 2' condition" — behavioural, through the turn loop. |
| Q5143 (new card goes to the bottom of an occupied Tamer) | "[When Digivolving] … places under an opponent Tamer, at the bottom" — the Tamer's two existing cards keep their order and the placed card lands at `stack[0]`, the true bottom. |
| Q5144 (unaffectable host is not a legal destination) | "Q5144: cannot place an opposing Digimon under a host that isn't affected by effects". Structural: the restriction is armed through `advance().ledgers.continuous` because no card in scope grants "unaffected by your opponent's effects" to an OPPOSING permanent. Test Seam, not a raw cast. |
| Q5145 (placed Digimon leaves; its digivolution cards are trashed) | "[On Play] via a real DigiXros …" — the victim's own digivolution card ends in the opponent's trash and only its top card is under the host. Drove defect 1. |
| Q5146 (a Tamer does not gain the placed card's inherited effect) | Partially. The placement under a Tamer is proved; the "a Tamer never gains inherited effects" rule lives in the engine's inherited-effect resolution, not in this module, and the placed card in the fixture is vanilla, so no card-level assertion distinguishes it. Documented, not asserted. |
| Q5147 (BT17-087 treated as a Digimon DOES gain it) | Untestable here: it depends on BT17-087's "treated as a Digimon" grant and on inherited-effect resolution, both outside this card. Belongs to BT17-087's lane. |
| Q5148 (linking does not trigger) | "Q5148: does not trigger when a card is linked to an opponent's Digimon" — public `linkCard` intent on the opponent's turn. The link verb (`engine/actions/link.ts`) never posts `onAddDigivolutionCards`, so the watcher stays silent. |
| Q5166 (a material placed for a DigiXros is already in the stack when the granted effect resolves → triggers) | Not EX10-056's clause. Both rulings describe **EX10-059 DarknessBagramon**, whose `[All Turns]` gains the `[All Turns]` effects of Lv.6 [Bagra Army] cards in its digivolution cards. EX10-056 is only the granted card. Coordinator: route to the EX10-059 lane. |
| Q5167 (a copy added from trash DURING the same effect does not trigger) | Same as Q5166 — EX10-059's grant timing, not this module. |

#### Remaining gaps and seams for the coordinator

- **Engine seam (offered but impossible effect).** `canAttemptPlaceUnder`
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/placeUnder.ts`, the
  `action.targetIsPermanent === true` early return) answers `true` unconditionally, so the "you may"
  prompt is published even when the opponent controls no Digimon at all. Expected: no prompt when no
  legal source/destination pair exists. Actual: a `dec-1 optional` decision the test must decline.
  Worked around in the fixtures with `autoDeclineOptional`; not a card-level bug.
- **Tamer digivolve branch (defect 2) is structural only.** No public route reaches an opponent
  Tamer digivolving with the cards in this set, so the fix is asserted on the compiled IR, not on
  board state.
- **Q5146/Q5147** need inherited-effect assertions that belong to the engine and to BT17-087.
- `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` will drift from the three IR changes above; the
  coordinator owns the sync.

### EX10-057 — Piedmon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-057.test.ts`: Hand/Main reduction, play/attack/digivolve, restrictions, face-up security, delayed deletion/order and Q5149–Q5156 peers.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Worker lane, card-only. Base `46810fe1e271215d173854b82513f4ce30508b01`, branch
`audit-ex10-reaudit`.

#### Card summary

Purple, Digimon, Lv.6 Mega, [Wizard]/[Dark Masters], Virus, play cost 11, 11000 DP,
`evoCosts: []` (no printed digivolution requirement), R, 4 per deck. No inherited effect.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Hand] [Main]` If you don't have any Digimon other than Digimon with [Dark Masters] in
   their texts, you may play this card with the play cost reduced by 5. At turn end, delete
   the Digimon this effect played.
2. `[On Play]` `[When Attacking]` Delete 1 of your opponent's unsuspended Digimon.
3. `[All Turns]` This Digimon can only digivolve into [Apocalymon].
4. `[On Deletion]` If you have no purple face-up security cards, place this Digimon face up as
   the bottom security card.
5. `[Security]` If this card was face-up, you may play 1 level 5 or lower card with
   [Dark Masters] in its text from your hand or trash without paying the cost.

Catalog check: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` matches the official text for every
field asserted in the test. No discrepancy found. The empty `evoCosts` is correct — the card
prints no digivolution requirement, exactly like its sibling EX10-035 Machinedramon — and it
means **there is no legal route that digivolves INTO Piedmon**; it can only be played.

#### Q&A coverage (11 ids)

| Q&A | Ruling | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5149 | "[X] in its text" covers name, traits, effects, inherited, requirements | `plays this card from hand for 11 − 5 with only a [Dark Masters]-text Digimon out (Q5149)` — BT15-072 Vilemon carries "[Dark Masters]" only in its effect text and still satisfies the gate; reused as the `[Security]` free-play target |
| Q5150 | Cards placed face up in security stay revealed, otherwise normal | `places itself face up as the BOTTOM security card after losing a real battle (Q5150)` — exact stack order plus `faceUp` true for the placed card and false for the pre-existing two |
| Q5151 | A security check on a face-up card runs with the card left revealed | `plays a level 5 or lower [Dark Masters]-text card from hand and then battles` — the face-up EX10-057 is checked and the check resolves normally |
| Q5152 | A `[Security]` effect does trigger on a face-up card's check | same test — the free play resolves off the face-up check |
| Q5153 | Shuffling a security stack re-hides every face-up card | `is turned face down again when a peer effect shuffles the security stack (Q5153)` — EX3-029 Airdramon's `[On Play]` shuffle, driven by a real `playCard` intent |
| Q5154 | The `[Hand] [Main]` effect activates with no Digimon at all | `activates with no Digimon at all (Q5154)` |
| Q5155 | Delayed delete vs. the played card's own turn-end effect: player picks the order | `still deletes the played host at turn end after it digivolved, the turn player choosing the order (Q5155/Q5740)` — a real `orderTriggers` decision, offered to seat 0, with more than one trigger key |
| Q5156 | "If this card was face-up" means face-up in security when checked | `does nothing when the card was checked face down (Q5156)` |
| Q5739 | The Digimon played by the `[Hand] [Main]` effect is deleted at turn end | `deletes the Digimon this effect played at its own turn end through the real turn loop (Q5739)`, with `leaves a normally played copy alive through its own turn end` as the gate |
| Q5740 | The turn-end trigger and the delayed delete are simultaneous pending processing | same test as Q5155 |
| Q6513 | The `[Security]` effect activates, then the card battles | `plays a level 5 or lower [Dark Masters]-text card from hand and then battles (Q5151/Q5152/Q6513)` — Vilemon is played free, then the 11000 DP security Digimon deletes the 3000 DP attacker and goes to the trash |

No id is left untestable.

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | IR node | Behavioural test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Hand] [Main]` reduced-cost play | `Main` + `isFromHand: true` + `youHaveNone` (`excludeNameOrTrait` `Dark Masters`) → `PlayWithoutCost{ isSelf, from:["hand"], payCost:true, reduceCostBy:5 }` | reduced-cost play (memory 6 → 0), no-Digimon activation, refusal with BT1-009 out (memory unchanged, card still in hand) |
| …at turn end delete the played Digimon | `DelayedDeletePlayed` | real turn loop: played copy gone at turn end, seeded bystander alive; normally played copy survives |
| `[On Play]` delete 1 unsuspended opposing Digimon | `OnPlay` → `Delete{ controller:"opponent", unsuspended:true, kind:["Digimon"], count:1 }` | `playCard` at full cost 11; the unsuspended body is trashed, the suspended one is the only survivor |
| `[When Attacking]` same delete | `WhenAttacking` → same `Delete` | real `attack` intent at the player; unsuspended body trashed, security checked, attacker survives |
| …no legal target | same | attack into a board of only suspended Digimon deletes nothing |
| `[All Turns]` only into [Apocalymon] | `AllTurns` → `RestrictDigivolveInto{ isSelf, into: name "Apocalymon", duration:"permanent" }` | `digivolve` intent refuses BT2-083 Millenniummon (Purple Lv.6 cost 6, otherwise legal) and accepts BT15-102 Apocalymon; resulting stack is `[EX10-057]` |
| `[On Deletion]` face-up bottom security | `OnDeletion` → `SecurityManipulation{ op:"placeAsSecurity", toTop:false, faceUp:true, condition: youHaveNone purple face-up security }` | battle loss into a 20000 DP suspended wall: exact 3-card security order, placed card face up; trash instead with a purple face-up BT10-071 present; still placed when the only face-up security card is Red |
| `[Security]` free play | `Security` + `isSecurity` → `PlayWithoutCost{ level ≤ 5, nameOrTrait "Dark Masters" match:"text", from:["hand","trash"], payCost:false, condition: sourceWasFaceUpSecurity, optional }` | from hand (then battles), from trash, nothing on a face-down check, refuses a Lv.6 [Dark Masters] card (EX10-058) and a Lv.3 with no such text (BT10-071) |

#### Peer and stack evidence

- **Sibling comparison.** EX10-035 Machinedramon prints the same five clause shapes in Black.
  The two modules were compared node by node: identical `Main`/`AllTurns`/`OnDeletion`/
  `Security` IR apart from the colour token in the `[On Deletion]` condition, and the
  card-specific middle clause (`Delete` here, `DeDigivolve` there). The colour token is proved
  in both directions — a purple face-up card blocks the placement, a red one does not.
- **Evolution stack.** Piedmon digivolves into BT15-102 Apocalymon through the public
  `digivolve` intent and the resulting permanent's `stack` is asserted to be exactly
  `["EX10-057"]`; the delayed delete then removes that whole permanent at turn end, which is
  the Q5155 identity claim ("the Digimon this effect played" survives a digivolve).
- **No route into Piedmon.** `evoCosts` is empty, so no legal digivolution or DNA route reaches
  this card; the only entries are `playCard` and its own `[Hand] [Main]` play, both exercised.
- **Cross-card.** EX3-029 Airdramon (an unrelated Yellow peer) supplies the only public
  security-shuffle route in the pool and is used to prove Q5153 against a face-up EX10-057.

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed `EX10-057.test.ts` claimed 10/10 but:

- proved `[All Turns]` only by reading `advance(engine).ledgers.continuous.digivolveIntoAllowed`
  — a ledger reach, not a `digivolve` intent, and no stack identity;
- proved `[On Play]` only through `advance.fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)` — injected
  timing, which the brief scores 0 in the Behavior column;
- proved `[On Deletion]` only through `advance.verb.deletePermanent` — again injected, and it
  never asserted the BOTTOM position (`toTop: false`) or that the other security cards stay
  face down;
- had **no test at all** for `[When Attacking]`, for the turn-end `DelayedDeletePlayed`
  (Q5739/Q5155/Q5740), for the face-down `[Security]` negative (Q5156), for the level and
  text bounds on the `[Security]` free play, for the trash source, or for Q5153;
- asserted `s.state.memory` as `-6` from a hand-rolled `memory = 0` start rather than showing
  the reduction against the printed cost.

The module IR itself was already hand-corrected and is **correct as committed** — I changed
nothing in `EX10-057.ts`.

```
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <the two files> => all matched files use the correct format
git diff --check                     => clean
```

##### Revert confirmation

One extra run with four IR levers broken at once
(`reduceCostBy: 5 → 0`, both `unsuspended: true` deleted, `Apocalymon → Millenniummon`,
`colors: ["Purple"] → ["Red"]`) turned **9 of the 19 tests red**
(`logs/EX10-057-revert-confirm.log`), each for its documented reason. The module was restored
byte-for-byte from a scratch copy and `git status` confirms `EX10-057.ts` is unmodified.

Caveat found by that run: the two positive `Delete` tests stayed green with `unsuspended: true`
removed, because the harness auto-selector happened to pick the unsuspended candidate anyway.
The lever that actually binds the filter is
`deletes nothing when every opposing Digimon is suspended`, which does go red. Noted rather
than papered over.

#### Remaining gaps

- The `[On Play]`/`[When Attacking]` delete is not proved against a board where the *opponent*
  has an eligible ＜Blocker＞ or a would-leave replacement; the interaction is generic engine
  behaviour, not card-specific, and would need another card's clause to drive.
- Q5153 is proved on a face-up EX10-057 that was **seeded** face up in security rather than
  placed there by its own `[On Deletion]` first. Chaining a battle loss and then a shuffle in
  one fixture adds no ruling coverage, so the shorter arrangement was used deliberately.
- No engine seam is missing and no test is retained red.

### EX10-058 — Lilithmon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-058.test.ts`: Public DigiXros/end-turn deletion, two-source cost creating trash target Q5160, purple level-four filters, Q5168 chain and quota reset. The new public battle-deletion then same-turn
play test proves the shared cross-event quota; separating the watcher keys makes
the remaining-source assertion fail (0 instead of 2).
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Base `46810fe1e271215d173854b82513f4ce30508b01`, branch `audit-ex10-reaudit`.
The prior `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/AUDIT.md` claim of 10/10 is treated as unverified.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Purple, Digimon, Lv.6, play cost 11,
DP 11000, EvoCost Purple/Lv.5/cost 3, form Mega, attribute Virus, traits `["Demon Lord",
"Bagra Army"]`, rarity R, max 4 per deck. No inherited text, no Security text, not an ACE, no
overflow. The catalog row matches the official text; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` — Until your opponent's turn ends, give 1 of their Digimon or
   Tamers "[End of Your Turn] Delete 1 of your Digimon."
2. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` — When any of your opponent's Digimon are played or deleted, by
   trashing any 2 of this Digimon's digivolution cards, you may play 1 level 4 or lower purple
   Digimon card from your trash without paying the cost.
3. `[DigiXros -2] 2 Digimon cards w/[Bagra Army] trait`.

Rules anchors: comprehensive §7-2-1 (materials come from hand and/or battle area),
§7-2-2-1 (the play cost is reduced by the printed amount **for each** card placed),
§7-2-2-4 (any number of the specified cards, never zero), §7-2-2-7 (a battle-area material
contributes only its top card; its own digivolution cards are shed).

#### Q&A coverage

| Q&A | Covered by | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5157 | `Q5157 trashes exactly 2 of its own sources …`, `Q5157 cannot pay with only 1 digivolution card` | Pass. A "by" cost of 2 sources is all-or-nothing: with one source under Lilithmon nothing is trashed and nothing is played. |
| Q5158 | `Q5158 gives the turn-end deletion to a Digimon unaffected by effects, and it does not trigger` (new) | Pass. The grant IS installed on a `beAffected`-immune permanent (subscription armed), and firing the real `endOfTurn` bus deletes nothing. A second engine in the same test acts as the control: without the immunity the identical driver does delete, so the assertion is about the immunity, not a dead timing. |
| Q5159 | `Q5159 installs the granted turn-end deletion on an OPPOSING permanent …`, `Q5158/Q5159 grants the turn-end deletion through a public DigiXros play …` (new) | Pass. Through a public DigiXros `playCard` and the real turn loop, the opponent deletes one of **their own** Digimon at the end of **their** turn; the caster's board is untouched. |
| Q5160 | `Q5160 can play a source trashed as the activation cost …`, `Q5160 still fires the inherited watcher for another qualifying source left in trash` | Result matches the ruling, mechanism does not. See "Retained gap — seam 21" below. |
| Q5168 | `Q5168 chains [All Turns] off a deletion its own [On Play] grant caused …` (new) | Pass. The granted end-of-turn deletion is itself "any of your opponent's Digimon are deleted", so the `[All Turns]` clause triggers off the board change this card's own effect caused, pays both DigiXros materials and replays EX10-027 from the trash for free. |

#### Clause → test → IR mapping

| Clause | IR (`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-058.ts`) | Behavioural proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` grant | `GainTriggeredEffect` on `{controller:"opponent", kind:["Digimon","Tamer"]}`, `gainedTrigger:"EndOfYourTurn"`, `gainedActions:[Delete {controller:"mine", kind:["Digimon"]}]`, `duration:"untilOpponentTurnEnd"`; registered under both `OnPlay` and `WhenDigivolving` | Public DigiXros play → grant armed on the opponent's chosen permanent only → real turn loop → opponent deletes one of their own Digimon at their own turn end. Immunity case per Q5158. |
| `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` watcher pair | one `AllTurns` effect, `frequency:"OncePerTurn"`, two `SubTrigger`s (`whenPlayed`, `onDeletionOf`) over `{controller:"opponent", kind:["Digimon"]}` sharing `oncePerTurnKey:"EX10-058/all-turns"` | New turn-loop test: opponent's first play of their turn pays 2 sources and returns a card; their second play the same turn pays nothing; on their **next** turn the use has reset and pays again. The shared-key sameness across the two event forms stays structural (`fireSubTrigger`). |
| the "by trashing any 2" cost | `cost: {kind:"trash", target:{filter:{isSelfRef:true, zone:"digivolutionCards"}, count:2}}` | Q5157 pair (exact stack and trash endpoints), plus the decline path and the play-prohibition path. |
| the payoff | `PlayWithoutCost` from `["trash"]`, `{controller:"mine", kind:["Digimon"], colors:["Purple"], levelComparison:{op:"lte", value:4}}`, `payCost:false`, `optional:true` | Positive path, and a negative fixture where a Lv.5 purple (EX10-047) and a Lv.4 non-purple (AD1-001) are both refused, with the stack and trash unmoved. |
| `[DigiXros -2] 2 Digimon cards w/[Bagra Army] trait` | `digiXrosRequirement: [{materials:[{traits:["Bagra Army"]}], count:2, costReduction:2, maxMaterials:2}]` | See DigiXros section. |
| no printed `[Digivolve]` header | `digivolutionRequirement` deliberately absent | Asserted; `matchingAlternateDigivolutionRequirement` reads every entry as an ALTERNATE route, so restating the printed EvoCost row would register an unprinted route. |

#### DigiXros evidence (the coordinator's focus)

- **Material sources.** Hand is proved twice (one- and two-material plays). Battle area is proved
  by a new test: the material permanent leaves the battle area, only its top card lands in
  Lilithmon's stack, and the card beneath it goes to the trash (§7-2-2-7). Trash is proved to be
  **refused** — this card's IR declares no trash allowance and no expander Tamer is in play, so a
  hand + trash declaration returns `{ok:false, reason:"invalid-material"}` with memory, board and
  trash unchanged.
- **Reduced cost per material.** One material: 11 − 2 = 9 (memory 11 → 2). Two materials:
  11 − 4 = 7 (memory 11 → 4). The two runs together prove the discount is per placed card
  (§7-2-2-1), not a flat reduction. This is the gap the previous audit left: it only ran the
  two-material case, which a flat "−4" implementation would also pass.
- **Material cap.** Three `[Bagra Army]` materials are refused with an explicit
  `reason:"invalid-material"` (was `.ok === false`), memory and hand unchanged. This is
  `maxMaterials: 2` doing the work; without it `materialsSatisfyRecipe`'s single-slot branch
  would accept any number.
- **Non-matching material.** EX10-040 DemiDevimon is purple but carries `[Evil]`, not
  `[Bagra Army]`. The declaration is refused with `reason:"invalid-material"` (was a weak
  `.not.toEqual({ok:true})`), and the refusal is now asserted to be inert — memory 11, empty
  battle area, hand still 4.

#### Changes made in this lane

`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-058.ts` — **unchanged**. No IR defect found, so no
`effects.json` drift from this lane.

`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-058.test.ts`:

1. Replaced the `advance(engine).ledgers.subTriggers` read in the Q5159 test with
   `observe(engine).subscriptions(...)`. `advance.ledgers` is documented as the arming door
   ("Reads belong in `observe()`, not here"); the assertion is a read.
2. Tightened both DigiXros refusals from `.ok === false` / `.not.toEqual({ok:true})` to
   `toMatchObject({ok:false, reason:"invalid-material"})`, and added the no-state-change
   assertions to the non-matching case.
3. Added the per-material cost proof (single material, cost 9).
4. Added the battle-area material-source proof, including the shed digivolution card.
5. Added the trash-material refusal.
6. Added Q5158 (grant to an unaffected Digimon, plus a control engine so the negative is not
   vacuous — the first attempt used `fireGlobal(EffectTiming.EndOfTurn)`, which never reaches
   the `endOfTurn` SubTrigger bus and made the assertion pass for the wrong reason).
7. Added the Q5158/Q5159 end-to-end proof: public DigiXros play → grant → real turn loop →
   opponent's own turn-end deletion.
8. Added the Q5168 chain proof (the same board with the optional accepted).
9. Added the `[Once Per Turn]` proof through the real turn loop: same-turn refusal after the
   first use, then reset on the opponent's next turn, with the intermediate stack and board
   counts asserted at each step.
