#### Retained gaps

1. **Seam 21 — `pending-activation-lapses-when-source-leaves` (Q5160).** The two committed
   Q5160 tests produce the ruling's outcome in both directions: the EX10-044 [Damemon] that is
   trashed as the cost and then replayed from the trash does **not** draw, while a second
   [Damemon] left in the trash does. But the engine has no general gate that cancels a pending
   triggered effect whose source left its area (REVIEW-NOTES.md item 21). EX10-044's inherited
   clause is a `Static` `SubTrigger` on `onDigivolutionCardsDiscardedBatch`, armed only while the
   card sits under a `[Bagra Army]` Digimon, so the right answer here falls out of continuous
   re-derivation plus in-window play ordering — not out of the rule Q5160 states. The tests stay
   green because the observable endpoints are correct; the engine-wide gate is still missing and
   remains the coordinator's item.
2. **The shared once-per-turn key across the two event forms is structural.** The reset and the
   same-turn refusal are now proved through the real turn loop with public `playCard` intents.
   What is still injected is the specific claim that a *played* event and a *deleted* event drain
   the **same** budget (`fireSubTrigger("whenPlayed", …)` after a public deletion). Arranging both
   forms publicly in one turn needs an opponent deletion and an opponent play in the same window,
   which no single legal line here produces.
3. **Latent engine seam, not a defect today (same finding as EX10-055).**
   `validateDigiXros` computes
   `perMaterialReduction = requirement.count === "∞" ? (requirement.costReduction ?? 1) : requirement.count`.
   For a numeric `count`, `costReduction` is **ignored** and `count` doubles as the per-material
   reduction. EX10-058 has `count === costReduction === 2`, so the field is dead but harmless. I
   left it in place: removing it is a no-op behaviourally and would drift `effects.json` for
   nothing. A future `[DigiXros -1] 2 cards …` recipe would silently mischarge.
4. **Q5168's other half is out of scope.** The ruling is written from the perspective of the card
   that *gains* EX10-058's `[All Turns]`; that grant lives in another lane's module. What is
   provable here — that EX10-058's own `[All Turns]` triggers off a deletion its own effect
   caused — is proved.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| Catalog / rules | 2 | Every catalog field asserted and matched against the official text; all 5 assigned Q&A addressed; the per-material cost reading grounded in comprehensive §7-2-2-1 and the material sources in §7-2-1 / §7-2-2-7 |
| IR trace | 2 | Every printed clause mapped to a compiled action, including the self-ref two-source cost, the level/colour payoff filter, the shared `oncePerTurnKey`, the deliberate absence of `digivolutionRequirement`, and the dead-but-harmless `costReduction` field |
| Behavior | 2 | All three clauses proved through public intents: DigiXros `playCard` for the recipe, sources, per-material cost, cap and two refusals; the real turn loop for the granted end-of-turn deletion, the Q5168 chain, and the once-per-turn refusal and reset. Only the cross-event-form shared key stays structural |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Mixed pool distinguishing `[Bagra Army]` (EX10-026/027/045) from a near-miss purple `[Evil]` (EX10-040) and inert non-matching fixtures; battle-area material relocation with the shed source asserted; the payoff filter proved against a Lv.5 purple and a Lv.4 non-purple; the EX10-044 inherited-watcher interaction proved on a real digivolution stack in both directions |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

### EX10-059 — DarknessBagramon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-059.test.ts`: DigiXros/digivolve, hand placement, exact three Bagra Army sources, source-bearing deletion and inherited conferrals/EX7-061 peer.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`. Files touched:
`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-059.test.ts` (rewritten) and this report. The module
`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-059.ts` needed no change.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Lv.7, Purple/Black, Mega, Virus,
[Composite] [Bagra Army], play cost 16, DP 16000, EvoCost Purple Lv.6 : 6 and Black Lv.6 : 6,
SR, max 4.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` Choose 1 card in your opponent's hand without looking and
   place it as any of their Digimon's bottom digivolution card or under any of their Tamers.
2. Then, **by placing 3 [Bagra Army] trait Digimon cards from your trash as this Digimon's top
   digivolution cards**, delete 1 of their Digimon or Tamers **with cards under it**.
3. `[All Turns]` This Digimon gains all `[All Turns]` effects on all level 6 [Bagra Army] trait
   Digimon cards in its digivolution cards.
4. `[DigiXros -3] [Bagramon] x [DarkKnightmon]`.

The catalog text matches the official list. No catalog discrepancy found.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5161 | A "by" condition cannot be part-paid: all 3 cards or nothing | Two behavioural tests: the 3-card payment resolves and deletes; the 2-card board places nothing and deletes nothing |
| Q5162 | A card placed under a Tamer that already has cards goes to the **bottom** | Behavioural: Tamer with 2 seeded cards, exact 3-card order asserted |
| Q5163 | A host that isn't affected by effects cannot receive the card | Behavioural: suspended BT15-047 as the opponent's only permanent; the card stays in hand |
| Q5164 | A Tamer does **not** gain the placed card's inherited effect | Behavioural pair: EX10-026 (inherited ＜Blocker＞) under a Tamer opens no block window; under a Digimon it does |
| Q5165 | A [Marcus Damon] treated as a Digimon **does** gain it | **Untestable through public intents** — see below |
| Q5166 | A [Bagramon] that was a DigiXros material fires for this card's own placement | Behavioural: DigiXros with EX10-056, opponent's top security trashed |
| Q5167 | A [Bagramon] placed **by** this effect does **not** fire for the earlier placement | Behavioural: digivolve route, EX10-056 among the 3 paid cards, security untouched |
| Q5168 | A [Lilithmon] placed by this effect **does** fire for the deletion that follows | Behavioural: EX10-058 among the 3, BT4-080 played free from trash |
| Q5169 | A [Tactimon] placed by this effect prevents this card from leaving in EX7-061's interrupt | Behavioural: EX7-061 with [Lilithmon] under it; this card stays, EX7-061 is deleted, and 2 digivolution cards paid the prevention |

##### Q5165 — why it is untestable here

BT17-087 Marcus Damon grants itself `kinds: ["Digimon"]` only from its own `[On Play]`, with
duration `untilOpponentTurnEnd`, so the opponent must play it on **their** turn. The ruling's
only observable consequence is BT12-038 GeoGreymon's inherited effect, printed
`[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] If one of your yellow or red Tamers becomes suspended, …` — "your
turn" is the Tamer controller's turn. This card can place the material only on **our** turn, and
by the Tamer controller's next turn the treated-as-Digimon grant has expired (Q5165 itself says
the inherited effects are lost at that moment). The two windows never overlap, and no public
intent suspends an opponent's Tamer on their own turn. The half that is provable is proved:
a Digimon host gains the placed card's inherited effect, a plain Tamer host does not (Q5164).
The reason is recorded in a comment at the same place in the test file.

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Blind choice from the opponent's hand, placed at the bottom under their Digimon or Tamer | "Q5161 places the hidden hand card…", "Q5162…", "Q5163…", "Q5164…" | `PlaceUnder` with `blind: true`, `target.filter.isOpponentHand`, `underFilter { controller: "opponent", kind: ["Digimon","Tamer"] }`, `position: "bottom"` |
| "by placing 3 [Bagra Army] trait Digimon cards from your trash as this Digimon's top digivolution cards" | "Q5161 places…" / "Q5161 refuses to part-pay…" | `PlaceUnder` `count: 3`, `from: ["trash"]`, `position: "top"`, `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true`, `nameOrTrait [{ tokens: ["Bagra Army"], match: "trait" }]` |
| "delete 1 of their Digimon or Tamers with cards under it" | "Q5161 places…", "deletes only a permanent that has cards under it" | `Delete` with `hasDigivolutionCards: true`, `kind: ["Digimon","Tamer"]` |
| `[All Turns]` conferral from level 6 [Bagra Army] stack cards | Q5166, Q5167, Q5168, Q5169 | `GrantStatic` `grant: "effects"`, `copyTrigger: "AllTurns"`, `filter { kind: ["Digimon"], levels: [6], nameOrTrait [Bagra Army trait] }` |
| `[DigiXros -3] [Bagramon] x [DarkKnightmon]` | 5 DigiXros tests (hand source, battle-area source, refusals, cost proof, empty declaration) | `digiXrosRequirement [{ materials: [{names:["Bagramon"]},{names:["DarkKnightmon"]}], count: 3, costReduction: 3 }]` |

Every behavioural test reaches the card through a public intent — `playCard` with a `digiXros`
plan, or `digivolve` onto a seeded Lv.6 purple source (BT3-089 Boltmon, no printed text and not
[Bagra Army], so it contributes nothing and confers nothing). The previous test file drove every
clause through `advance(...).fireForPermanent` and read conferrals out of
`ledgers.continuous.listStackEffectConferrals()`; neither earns behaviour or peer/stack credit
under this brief, so both were replaced.

##### DigiXros evidence

- **Material sources.** Hand (both materials) and battle area (the material permanent is
  relocated whole and leaves the battle area) are proved separately.
- **Reduced cost per material.** 16 printed − 2 × 3 = 10 paid: memory 16 → 6. Proved again
  against a memory total of 0, where the printed play is rejected `insufficient-memory` and the
  DigiXros play succeeds and lands memory at −10.
- **Material cap.** A third material is rejected `invalid-material`: the recipe has exactly two
  named slots and `materialsSatisfyRecipe` refuses `materials.length > slots.length`.
- **Non-matching material refused.** A card matching no slot (BT1-009), a second [Bagramon] that
  cannot occupy the [DarkKnightmon] slot, and a trash-sourced material (this card prints no
  trash allowance and no expander Tamer is in play) are all `invalid-material`. An empty
  declaration is `no-materials`. The board is unchanged after every refusal.

```
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 19 passed (19)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck            -> clean
pnpm exec oxlint <module> <test>              -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <module> <test>       -> clean
git diff --check                              -> clean
```

Logs: `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-059-vitest.log`,
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-059-typecheck.log`,
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-059-baseline.log` (the pre-existing file, 12 passed).

#### Defects and notes

- **No behavioural defect found in the module.** Every printed clause resolves correctly through
  public intents, including the three conferral orderings the KB distinguishes (Q5166 / Q5167 /
  Q5168) and the EX7-061 interrupt chain (Q5169).
- **Cosmetic IR note, not fixed.** `digiXrosRequirement[0].costReduction: 3` is dead: `count` is
  the per-material reduction whenever it is a number, and `costReduction` is read only when
  `count === "∞"` (`apps/api/src/engine/actions/digiXros.ts`, `perMaterialReduction`). It is
  harmless because both values are 3, and removing it would drift `effects.json`, which this
  lane must not touch. Flagged for the coordinator rather than changed.
- **`maxMaterials` is absent and does not need to be present.** The two distinct named slots
  already cap the play at two materials; `maxMaterials` exists for the single-slot "1 [A], 1 [B],
  or 1 [C]" form. Proved by the rejected 3-material declaration.
- No retained reds. No `it.fails` / `it.skip` in this file.
- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` shows modified in the worktree; that is the
  coordinator's catalog fix and was not touched.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q5165 is documented untestable (above) rather than proved.
- The `copyTrigger: "AllTurns"` narrowing (a level-6 [Bagra Army] stack card's `[On Play]` must
  **not** be conferred) is asserted structurally only. The previous file proved it by firing
  `[On Play]` through the injected-timing seam and reading `ledgers.subTriggers`, which earns no
  credit under this brief; a public-intent proof would need a turn loop reaching the end of the
  opponent's turn to observe EX10-058's granted `[End of Your Turn]` watcher never appearing.
- Material stacking order after a DigiXros is asserted by membership, not sequence: loose hand
  materials each land at the bottom of the growing stack, so the declared order is reversed,
  while a battle-area material is relocated and keeps declaration order. §7-2-2-7 leaves the
  order to the player, so neither is a rules violation, but the two paths are inconsistent with
  each other and a coordinator may want one convention.

| C3 | `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] Your opponent may delete 1 of their Digimon or Tamers. If this effect didn't delete, trash their top security card and this Digimon unsuspends.` |

The catalog text matches the official list. No discrepancy found.

#### Q&A covered

| Q5171 | The `[Once Per Turn]` use is consumed even when the opponent declines and nothing is deleted. | `Q5171: When Attacking with nothing to delete trashes security, unsuspends, and burns the use` — first attack trashes security and unsuspends; the second attack in the same turn only checks security. |

#### Clause → test → IR

| Clause | IR | Behavioural test (public intents) |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ names: ["Lucemon: Chaos Mode"], cost: 6, isAlternate: true }]` | `digivolves for 6 from the exactly named Lucemon: Chaos Mode…` — `digivolve` intent with `useAlternateCost: true` from BT7-111: source card kept in `stack`, memory 10 → 4, bonus draw asserted against the deck order. `digivolves for 6 from a plain Purple Lv.6 and refuses an illegal source` — BT3-089 route succeeds, a Red Lv.5 (BT1-020) is rejected with memory and hand unchanged. |
| C2 | `OnPlay` / `WhenDigivolving` → `PlayWithoutCost` (`from: ["trash"]`, `breeding: true`, `requiresEmpty: "breedingArea"`, `optional`, `abortOnDecline`) then `Delete` (`superlative: "highestLevel"`, `count: "all"`, `condition: ifThisEffectActed`) | `On Play from hand…` (only the Lv.5 dies, the Lv.4 and Lv.3 survive; Larva instance moves trash → breeding; cost 16 paid); `On Play deletes every Digimon tied for the highest level` (two Lv.4s die, Lv.3 survives — proves `count: "all"`); `On Play does nothing when the breeding area is occupied`; `CR 15-7-4: declining the Larva play aborts the deletion`. The `[When Digivolving]` copy is proved in the Q5170 test through the real digivolve intent. |
| C3 | `WhenDigivolving` and `WhenAttacking`, both `frequency: OncePerTurn` with `sharedUseKey: "ir-shared-0"`: `Delete` (`controller: "opponent"`, `optional`, kinds Digimon+Tamer) then `SecurityManipulation trashTop` and `Unsuspend`, both gated by `ifThisEffectDidNotDelete` | `When Digivolving: the opponent may delete a Tamer…` (a level-less Tamer is a legal target and no security is trashed); `When Digivolving: the opponent declines, so the top security card is trashed` (exact remaining security order asserted); `Q5171…` (attack path: security trashed, attacker unsuspended after its own attack, second same-turn attack only checks security); `the [Once Per Turn] use is shared…` (a digivolve spends the use, the same turn's attack cannot fire it); `the shared [Once Per Turn] resets on the next own turn, through the real turn loop`. |

#### What changed

Only `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-060.test.ts`. The module was already correct; no change to

The previous test file proved C2 and C3 with `advance().fireForPermanent(...)` injected timing,
which earns no behavioural credit. Every behavioural case is now driven by `playCard`,

### EX10-061 — Apocalymon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-061.test.ts`: Alternate evolution/illegal source, play, distinct-name Dark Masters face-up security, source-stack play, Rush and delayed deletion.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`.
Files touched: `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-061.test.ts` (rewritten), this report.
`apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-061.ts` needed no change; the compiled IR already matches the
printed text and every ruling in scope.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): White, Digimon, Lv.7, play cost 17,
DP 16000, no `evoCosts`, form Mega, attribute Unknown, type `Unidentified`, SR, max 4.
The catalog text matches the official list; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.6 w/[Dark Masters] trait: Cost 5` — the ONLY evolution route (`evoCosts`
   is empty), carried as `digivolutionRequirement[0]` with `isAlternate: true`.
2. `When this card would be played, by placing 1 of each face-up [Dark Masters] trait card
   with different names from your security stack under this card, reduce the play cost by 4
   for each card placed.`
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] You may play 1 of each [Dark Masters] trait card with
   different names from this Digimon's digivolution cards without paying the costs.`
4. `Then, all of your [Dark Masters] trait Digimon gain ＜Rush＞ for the turn.`
5. `At turn end, delete the Digimon this effect played.`

Note on clause 4: Apocalymon's own type is `Unidentified`, so it never grants ＜Rush＞ to
itself. That is asserted, not assumed.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Q&A | Ruling | Where it is proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5741 | The Digimon played by the effect is deleted at end of turn | tests 2, 4, 6 — deletion observed after a real `endPhase` + turn hand-off, never `fireGlobal` |
| Q5742 | That deletion and another end-of-turn processing are simultaneous; the turn player orders them | test 6 — a second, independent end-of-turn delete (Puppetmon's own `[Hand] [Main]`) is armed in the same turn and an `orderTriggers` decision with more than one key is raised to seat 0 |
| Q5783 | 1 face-up [Dark Masters] card in security may be placed on its own | test 2 — one face-up card, cost 13 paid |
| Q5784 | With 2 different names you must place both; declining the effect places nothing | tests 3 and 4 — both names placed for cost 9, and the engine never offers a smaller subset; the decline branch places nothing and pays the printed 17 |
| Q5785 | 1 [Dark Masters] card in the digivolution cards may be played on its own | tests 2 and 5 |
| Q5786 | With 2 different names in the digivolution cards you must play both; declining plays nothing | tests 3 and 4 |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate digivolve Lv.6 [Dark Masters] cost 5 | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` | test 5 — public `digivolve` intent from an EX10-012 host: memory 5 paid, host becomes EX10-061, bonus draw asserted (deck -1, hand size unchanged), plus an illegal-source negative on BT2-064 HiAndromon (Lv.6, no [Dark Masters] trait) refused with the board and memory unchanged |
| Cost-reduction replacement | `Static` -> `Replacement wouldBePlayed` with `cost.kind: "place"`, `optional: true`, target `{zone: security, faceUp: true, trait [Dark Masters], count "all", distinctNames}`, `underFilter isSelfRef`, nested `Replacement reduceCost amountPerPlaced: 4` | tests 2 (1 placed, 17-4=13), 3 (2 placed, 17-8=9), 4 (declined, 17) |
| one card per NAME, not per card | `distinctNames: true` | test 3 — a second [MetalSeadramon] (BT15-031) stays in security while EX10-012 is placed; the face-down security card is never a candidate |
| `[On Play]` play from digivolution cards | `OnPlay` -> `PlayWithoutCost from ["digivolutionCards"], payCost false, optional, count "all", distinctNames` | tests 2, 3, 6 |
| `[When Digivolving]` same body | `WhenDigivolving` -> identical action list | test 5, reached through the real `digivolve` intent (the previous suite never exercised this timing at all) |
| ＜Rush＞ to all your [Dark Masters] Digimon for the turn | `GainKeyword Rush, count "all", duration "forTheTurn"` | test 5 — granted to the played Digimon AND to a pre-existing [Dark Masters] Digimon, not to a plain Digimon, not to Apocalymon itself; the grant is gone after the turn ends through the real loop |
| Turn-end delete of what this effect played | `DelayedDelete` | tests 2, 5, 6 — only the played Digimon leaves; the pre-existing [Dark Masters] Digimon and the plain Digimon survive |

#### What changed and why

The committed suite scored the two `[On Play]`-shaped cases and the turn-end deletion with
`advance(...).fire(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)` and `advance(...).fireGlobal(EffectTiming.OnEndTurn)`.
Injected timing is structural only, so every behavioural claim in the previous audit rested on
it, and `[When Digivolving]` was never reached at all. The rewritten suite drives the card
entirely through public intents:

- `playCard` from hand inside `startTurnLoop()`, with the cost reduction measured on memory;
- `digivolve` onto a Lv.6 [Dark Masters] host for the alternate cost, plus an illegal-source
  negative;
- `endPhase` and the hand-off to the opponent's Main for every end-of-turn assertion;
- `activateEffect` on EX10-020's own `[Hand] [Main]` play to create the second simultaneous
  end-of-turn processing Q5742 describes.

The `[Hand] [Main]` effect key is read through `observe(s.engine).cardSource(...)`, the Test
Seam affordance, rather than the `engine as unknown as { cardSourceOf }` cast that the
EX10-012 suite uses.

Fixture notes: a blue and a green face-up security card (BT1-038, BT1-080) are seeded in the
turn-loop scenarios so the played [Dark Masters] Digimon's own `[On Deletion]` "place yourself
face up as the bottom security card" clause stays dormant and the turn-end deletion can be
asserted in the trash. Memory is set explicitly before each play: a play is refused when its
cost exceeds `memory - MEMORY_MIN`, and a play that pushes memory below 0 hands the turn over,
which would hide the end-of-turn window under test.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Where proved |

Coverage note: the ruling is phrased about a card "linked by an effect". The test drives
the same replacement through the public `linkCard` intent, which reaches the identical
rule-check sweep (`GameEngine.trashExcessLinkCards`, CR §4-8-5 / §17-1-3-2-5). That sweep is
the single suppression point — `primitives.ts` deliberately emits no `whenLinkTrashed` for a
by-rule link removal — so the effect-driven variant cannot behave differently.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start of Your Main Phase, +1 memory, gated on an opponent Digimon | `gains exactly 1 memory ...` (real turn loop, `runTurn(0)`), `gains nothing when the opponent's only permanent is a Tamer`, `stays silent at the start of the OPPONENT's Main phase` (`runTurn(1)`) | `trigger: "StartOfYourMainPhase"` -> `GainMemory {amount:1, condition: opponentHas kind:["Digimon"]}` |
| All Turns, effect-trashed link card of YOUR Digimon, suspend this Tamer to Draw 1 | `suspends the Tamer and draws 1 ...` (seat plays BT25-073 Dragomon; its [On Play] cost is a genuine link-card trash), `draws nothing ... when the suspend cost is declined`, `does not fire for an OPPONENT's Digimon's link card`, Q5172 negative | `trigger:"AllTurns"` -> `SubTrigger {event:"whenLinkTrashed", sourceFilter:{controller:"mine", kind:["Digimon"]}}` -> `Draw {amount:1, cost: suspend isSelfRef, optional, abortOnDecline}` |
| End of Your Turn, Once Per Turn, one of your Digimon may app fuse into a hand Digimon | `app fuses one Digimon into a hand card at the end of its controller's turn, once per turn` (production `startTurnLoop`: own end of turn fuses one pair only; the opponent's end of turn fuses nothing; the next own turn fuses the second pair) | `trigger:"EndOfYourTurn", frequency:"OncePerTurn"` -> `AppFuse {source: mine Digimon, into: mine Digimon, from:["hand"], optional}` |
| Security, play without paying | `[Security] plays itself into the battle area without paying its 3 cost` (real attack on the player; the security check plays the Tamer) | `trigger:"Security", isSecurity:true` -> `PlayWithoutCost {isSelfRef, payCost:false}` |
| A Tamer is inert outside the battle area (not printed; rules baseline) | `is inert while it sits in the trash / hand` (parameterised) | n/a — no watcher may install from trash or hand |

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test proved every clause through injected timing: `advance.fireForPermanent`,
`advance.fireForInstance` and `advance.verb.trash`. Under the re-audit rubric that scores 0
for behaviour. The rewrite removes every `advance.fire*` / `advance.verb.*` call. The file
now uses only:

- public intents `playCard`, `linkCard`, `attack`, `respondDecision`;
- the production turn machine (`advance.runTurn`, `startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase` +
  `endMainPhaseIfOpen`), which are turn-loop drivers, not injected timings.

Three facts the old test could not have caught, now pinned:

1. The link-card trash is driven by a real card (BT25-073 Dragomon's [On Play] cost), so the
   clause is proved against a producing effect rather than a raw primitive call.
2. Q5172 now has a behavioural negative: two `linkCard` intents onto a base-limit-1 host,
   the old link trashed by the rule sweep, and no prompt, no draw, no suspend.
3. The [Once Per Turn] app fusion is proved across three real turns, including the fact that
   the OPPONENT's end of turn fuses nothing.

Also recorded, because it is a non-obvious endpoint: after the fusion, the stack under
EX10-019 Warudamon holds only the consumed link card EX10-043, because Warudamon's own
[When Digivolving] re-links EX10-017 out of the digivolution cards. The old test never
looked at the stack.

Revert lever (proof the assertions are engine-produced, not harness-produced): deleting the

and the IR contract test RED (`logs/EX10-062-revert-check.log`, 2 failed / 10 passed). The
module was restored immediately; `git diff` on `EX10-062.ts` is empty.

#### Defects fixed locally

None in the module. Test-side fixes made while rebuilding the proof:

- the old file's whole behaviour surface was injected timing (replaced);
- the seat-1 fixture must set `state.memory` positive, not negative: the gauge is
  turn-relative (`memoryFor(seat) = seat === turnSeat ? memory : -memory`);
- any turn-loop test with this Tamer on the field must answer its own [End of Your Turn]
  app-fusion prompt, or the loop never closes (`autoDeclineOptional` on the start-of-main
  boards).

#### Retained reds / engine seams

None. No `it.skip` and no `it.fails` in the file.

Seam observations for the coordinator (no action requested):

- The harness's optional auto-responder is all-or-nothing. A flow that raises two optional
  prompts needing different answers (the driver card's [On Play] and this Tamer's
  suspend-to-draw) has to be answered by hand; the file carries a local `answerOptionals`
  helper that only uses `s.decisions` and the public `respondDecision` intent. A scripted
  responder option on `setupEngine` would remove that duplication across lanes.

#### Remaining gaps

- The [All Turns] clause has no printed "may", but the engine models it as optional because
  the suspend cost is optional under CR cost rules. Both branches are proved; if a ruling
  ever says the draw is mandatory, the `optional: true` flag is the single place to change.
- Q5172 is proved through a player-declared link rather than an effect-declared one (same
  sweep; see the coverage note above).

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

#### Card summary

Black Tamer, play cost 3, [LIBERATOR], `maxCountInDeck` 4. The catalog entry matches the
official text; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Start of Your Main Phase]` By returning this Tamer to the bottom of the deck, you may

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5173 | The "then" tail runs only if you actually returned this Tamer. | `Q5173/Q5743 returns itself…`, `Q5173 declining the "by" cost…`, `without a [Close] in hand…` |
| Q5743 | The [Close] this effect plays does not get its own `[Start of Your Main Phase]` window. | `Q5173/Q5743 returns itself…` (exactly one Tamer in the deck, the new one still on the board) |

Supporting rule: CR 3-4-5-8 — information on cards in the breeding area cannot be
referenced, so "if you don't have a Digimon" reads the battle area only.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 return cost + free play from hand | `Q5173/Q5743 returns itself…`, `Q5173 declining…`, `without a [Close] in hand…` | `StartOfYourMainPhase` action 0: `PlayWithoutCost` `from: ["hand"]`, `payCost: false`, `optional`, `abortOnDecline`, `cost: {kind:"return", to:"deckBottom", isSelf:true}`, name match `nameExact` |
| 2 tail, gated on the cost and on having no Digimon | `Q5173 declining…` (no cost -> no tail), `"if you don't have a Digimon"…`, `a Digimon in BREEDING…` | action 1: `PlayWithoutCost` `from:["trash"]`, `condition: allOf[ifThisEffectActed, youHaveNone{kind:["Digimon"], zone:"battleArea"}]` |
| 3 memory gain on an effect-trash | `suspends this Tamer and gains 1 memory…`, `[All Turns] on the OPPONENT's turn…`, `does not fire when the host Digimon has neither…`, `is inert in the HAND`, `is inert in the TRASH`, `cannot gain memory when the Tamer is already suspended`, `[All Turns]: the watcher is still armed…` | `AllTurns` -> `SubTrigger whenDigivolutionTrashed`, `sourceFilter` = host Digimon `controller:"mine"` + trait `Mineral`/`Rock` (`match:"trait"`), `GainMemory 1` with `cost:{kind:"suspend", isSelfRef}` |
| 4 free play from security | `[Security] plays itself for free when a real attack checks it` | `Security` effect, `isSecurity: true`, `PlayWithoutCost` `isSelfRef`, `payCost: false` |
| peer: the `[Close]` name other cards read | `peer EX10-032: this Tamer is the [Close] that opens Proganomon's [Hand] [Main] route` | EX10-032's `Main` `isFromHand` `condition: youHave{kind:["Digimon","Tamer"], name "Close"}` -> `DigivolveViaPlacement` |

#### What changed

##### Test rewrite (the substantive finding)

The committed test proved every clause through **injected timing**: `advance.fireForPermanent`
for the start-of-main window, `advance.fireSubTrigger("whenDigivolutionTrashed", …)` twice, and
`advance.fireForInstance(EffectTiming.SecuritySkill, …)` for the Security effect. Under the
rubric that is structural evidence only, so the card had no behavioural proof at all.

The file is now 15 tests (13 after round 1, 2 added in round 2), none of which use `advance.fire*`:

- the start-of-main window comes from `s.engine.startTurnLoop()` + `advance.waitForMainPhase(0)`;
- the digivolution-card trash comes from `playCard` on EX10-028 Landramon, whose `[On Play]`
  cost is a genuine effect-trash of a [Mineral] card from one of my Digimon's stacks;
- the Security effect comes from a real `attack` on the player that checks security.

New coverage the committed test did not have:

- Q5743 at all (the newly played Close must not chain its own start-of-main window);
- the "you don't have a Digimon" gate, positive and negative;
- the breeding-area reading of that gate (CR 3-4-5-8);
- the host-trait negative (a [Mineral] card trashed from a non-[Mineral]/[Rock] host does not
  fire the watcher);
- the Tamer being inert in the hand and in the trash (requested by the coordinator);
- the unpayable-cost case (an already suspended Tamer gains nothing);
- `[All Turns]` proved by the watcher still being armed after the turn changes, observed
  through the real turn loop.

##### Module

One change, in `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-063.ts`: the `youHaveNone` filter now names
`zone: "battleArea"` explicitly, with a comment citing CR 3-4-5-8.

Honest scope: this is **documentation strength, not a behaviour fix**. `countMatching`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/scaling.ts:87-107`) only adds the breeding permanent
when `filter.zone` is an array containing `"breeding"`, so the previous zone-less filter already
counted the battle area only. The `a Digimon in BREEDING…` test passes both with and without
the edit. The edit states the intent instead of relying on a default, which the worker brief
asks for. It does drift `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json`; the coordinator's sync
covers that.

#### Round 2: the opponent-turn firing, proved

##### Does ＜De-Digivolve＞ count as "effects trash … digivolution cards"? No.

Checked in the engine, not assumed. `deDigivolve` is `peelStackTops`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:1849-1929`). It moves the permanent's current
**top card** to the trash and promotes the card beneath it, then emits
`whenDigimonTopTrashed` (line 2828 region, inside the same function). It never calls
`trashDigivolutionCards`.

The two emitters of this Tamer's event are both in the same file:

- `trashDigivolutionCards` — fires `onDigivolutionCardsDiscardedBatch` (line 2769) and then,
  per card, `onDigivolutionCardDiscarded` and `whenDigivolutionTrashed` (line 2796);
- `trashDigivolutionCardsAtomic` — same batch/per-card pair (lines 2882 / 2901).

So an opponent's ＜De-Digivolve＞ (EX8-049 Golemon, EX7-046 Jazarichmon) does **not** arm
EX10-063. That matches the printed text: ＜De-Digivolve＞ trashes the top card, which is the
Digimon itself, not one of its digivolution cards.

##### The route that does work

`BT15-019` Crabmon — blue Lv.3, play cost 3 — prints `[On Play] Trash the bottom digivolution
card of 1 of your opponent's Digimon`, compiled as `TrashDigivolution` with
`target.filter.controller: "opponent"`, `amount: 1`, `fromTop: false`. Played by **seat 1 on
seat 1's turn** through the public `playCard` intent, it routes into `trashDigivolutionCards`
and therefore fires `whenDigivolutionTrashed` with my `[Mineral]` host as
`subjectPermanentId`.

`[All Turns] on the OPPONENT's turn: their BT15-019 trashes my [Mineral] Digimon's
digivolution card` drives exactly that, through `startTurnLoop()` (no `advance.fire*`):

| Endpoint | Value |
| --- | --- |
| turn seat | 1 (asserted before and after) |
| my host's stack | `[topFuel]` — the bottom card left |
| my trash | contains `bottomFuel` |
| my Tamer | `isSuspended === true` |
| gauge (`state.memory`, turn-relative) | 6 -> 3 (seat 1 pays for Crabmon) -> **2** (seat 0's Tamer gains 1) |
| opponent hand | one card lighter; Crabmon's own "then" draw did not run |
| pending decision | none |

The gauge assertion is the cross-seat proof. `state.memory` is turn-relative
(`MemoryGauge.value`, `apps/api/src/engine/MemoryGauge.ts:53-56`), so on seat 1's turn a
positive value is seat 1's memory. The `GainMemory` action calls
`gainMemoryForSeat(ctx.source.ownerSeat, …)`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/resources.ts:44-65`) — the **controller's**
seat, not the turn player's — so the gauge moves 3 -> 2, i.e. one memory to seat 0 while seat
1 is the turn player. A turn-player implementation would have moved it to 4.

This also closes the round-1 controller gap: the trashed cards belong to seat 0's Digimon and
seat 0's Tamer is the one that fired, on a turn seat 0 does not own.

##### Peer comparison with EX10-032 Proganomon

`peer EX10-032: this Tamer is the [Close] that opens Proganomon's [Hand] [Main] route` runs the
same board twice. With this Tamer in play, Proganomon exposes exactly one activatable effect,
the `activateEffect` intent places Landramon from the trash as the bottom digivolution card of
BT21-055 Sunarizamon and Sunarizamon digivolves into Proganomon (stack order asserted:
`[landramon, suna]`); this Tamer is still on the board afterwards. Remove the Tamer and
`activatableEffectsJson` is empty — the `youHave [Close]` gate is closed. This is the
cross-card proof that the Tamer's printed **name** is load-bearing for its own archetype.

```

  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 15 passed (15)
     docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-063-vitest.log

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck   -> exit 0 (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-063-typecheck.log)
pnpm exec oxlint <both files>        -> exit 0, no findings (…/logs/EX10-063-lint.log)
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <both files> -> "All matched files use the correct format."
git diff --check                     -> exit 0
```

#### Two fixture facts worth recording

- The first turn player does **not** draw, so a turn-loop test that asserts deck identity must
  compare against the untouched deck, not `deck.slice(1)`.
- A permanent in the breeding area parks the turn loop in the Breeding phase until the turn
  player acts; `advance.waitForMainPhase(0)` then throws. The public way through is to settle
  on `Phase.Breeding` and send `endPhase` for that seat before waiting for Main.

#### Remaining gaps

- Round 1's two open items (opponent-turn firing and the cross-seat controller gate) are both
  closed by the BT15-019 test above; the armed-subscription test is kept as the structural
  companion.
- ＜De-Digivolve＞ still cannot reach this clause, and should not: `peelStackTops` emits
  `whenDigimonTopTrashed`, a different event. Recorded above so a later reader does not retry
  that route.
- Clause 1 with a [Close] in hand but the play otherwise illegal is not separately explored;
  the "no [Close] in hand" case covers the reachable version.

No retained red tests. No engine seam defect found.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 place cost + draw | `StartOfYourMainPhase` -> `Draw` with `cost.kind: "place"`, `from: ["hand","trash"]`, `nameOrTrait` `match: "trait"` | hand-payment, trash-payment, trait-gate negative, decline, opponent-turn negative, inert-in-hand/trash negative |
| 2 zone expansion + suspend cost | `AllTurns` -> `Replacement` `wouldBePlayed` -> `DigiXrosMaterialZoneExpansion { zones: ["underTamers","trash"], cost: suspend }`; plus the shared registry entry in `packages/shared/src/cards/zoneExpanders.ts` | five DigiXros tests through `playCard`+`digiXros` and through the effect-play route (BT26-006 inherited play), the suspended-copy refusal, the off-trait refusal, the Tamer-in-trash refusal |
| 3 security play | `Security` -> `PlayWithoutCost { payCost: false }` | `[Security] plays itself for free out of a real security check` |

#### What the previous audit missed

1. **Injected timing.** Both endpoints of clause 1 and clause 3 were proved with
   `advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnStartMainPhase, …)` and
   `fireForInstance(EffectTiming.SecuritySkill, …)`. Neither earns behavioural credit.
   Clause 1 now runs through `startTurnLoop()` + `waitForMainPhase(0)`; clause 3 now runs
   inside a real security check driven by an `attack` intent at `{ kind: "player" }`.
2. **Vacuous draw assertion.** The old Q5174 test settled on "the drawn card is in hand" and
   asserted nothing else, so a second draw, a lost hand card, or a missing trash payment would
   all have passed. The replacements assert the whole hand, the whole deck, the whole trash and
   `pendingDecision === undefined`, and the three negative variants leave `deckA` on the deck —
   which is what makes the single draw load-bearing.
3. **No trait gate proof.** Nothing proved the `[Bagra Army]`/`[Twilight]` and `kind: ["Digimon"]`
   halves of the place cost. Added: a fixture holding only an off-trait Digimon (BT1-014) and an
   on-trait Digi-Egg (BT26-006) opens no prompt and draws nothing.
4. **No "may" refusal.** Added a decline path: the card stays in hand and no draw happens
   (`abortOnDecline`).
5. **No turn-ownership proof.** Added: the payment armed after my own main phase is untouched at
   the start of the opponent's main phase.
6. **No zone-inertness proof** (coordinator request). Added two negatives: copies sitting in hand
   and trash open no prompt and draw nothing across a real turn loop, and a DigiXros trash
   material is refused (`invalid-material`) while the Tamer is in the trash rather than in play.
7. **Bottom placement was under-specified.** The old fixture had one card under the Tamer, so
   "bottom" and "directly beneath the Tamer" were indistinguishable in a two-element array. The
   fixture now seeds two cards and asserts the exact three-element order.
8. **`Q5177` had no coverage at all.** Now partly covered — see gaps.
9. **`Q6961` had no coverage at all.** Now covered by a control/target pair.

#### Findings

- **`cost.position: "bottom"` is inert on this path (engine seam, no behaviour change).**
  Deleting the field from the module, or flipping it to `"top"`, leaves all 20 behavioural tests
  passing; only the IR-shape test notices. `costs.ts` reads `belowTop: cost.position !== "bottom"`
  for permanent-source and security placements, but the loose-card "place under the source Tamer"
  branch never reaches that read, and its default placement is already the bottom of the Tamer's
  cards. So Q5174 holds behaviourally and the field is documentation. Reported, not changed —
  fixing it belongs in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/costs.ts`, not in a card lane.
- **The `playCard` DigiXros route does not consult this module.** `apps/api/src/engine/actions/digiXros.ts`
  reads the static `DIGIXROS_ZONE_EXPANDERS` table in `packages/shared/src/cards/zoneExpanders.ts`
  (`underTamerMax: 1`, `trashMax: 1`, trait gate `[Bagra Army]`/`[Twilight]`). Only the effect-play
  route (`interpreter/actions/play.ts`) reads the compiled `DigiXrosMaterialZoneExpansion`. Both are
  exercised here, but a reader should know that the `playCard` tests grade the shared table, not the
  card module. The registry comment already says so.
- **Q6961 is implemented correctly.** With EX10-044 Damemon trashed from the [Bagra Army] attacker's
  digivolution cards by BT26-006's cost, its pending ＜Draw 1＞ fires when Damemon stays in the trash
  (control) and is lost when EX10-064's expansion takes Damemon as a DigiXros material — exactly the
  ruling. No defect.
- No red tests retained. No engine gap blocked a clause.

#### Remaining gaps

- **Q5177 / Q5179 "multiples of the specified cards would be played" is not reachable through a
  public intent.** `playCard` plays exactly one card, so there is no way to have two DigiXros cards
  in one would-be-played window. What is proved instead: `refuses a second activation of an already
  suspended copy in the same turn` shows the suspend cost is real and per-activation, and the two-copy
  tests prove quotas add. The genuine simultaneous case stays unproved.
- The [Security] test asserts memory is unchanged by the play; a hypothetical bug that both charged
  and refunded 4 memory would slip past. The printed cost of 4 is asserted alongside it.

#### Commands

Fixture correction applied before acceptance: the `Q5175/Q5176 effect-play path …` board seeded
seat 1's security with BT1-001, a Digi-Egg, which the brief forbids. It now uses BT1-009
Monodramon, a truly inert main-deck Digimon, with the `security` alias and every assertion
unchanged. Re-run after the change:

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-064.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
-> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 21 passed (21)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
-> errors only in src/cards/EX10/EX10-065.test.ts (another lane's file); none in EX10-064

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-064.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-064.test.ts  -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same files>                                                            -> clean
git diff --check                                                                                -> clean
```

Logs: `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-064-vitest.log`,
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-064-typecheck.log`, and the committed baselines
`EX10-064-committed-test.log` / `EX10-064-committed-module.log`.

| Behaviour | 2 | Clause 1 and 3 now run through the real turn loop and a real security check; positives, trait-gate negative, decline, turn-ownership and zone-inertness negatives all assert full zone endpoints. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | Real peers throughout: EX10-063 as the other Tamer host, BT26-006's inherited play as the effect-play route, EX10-044's inherited trash trigger for Q6961, two-copy quota addition, off-trait DigiXros refusal. All inside the test seam. |

### EX10-065 — Yukio Oikawa

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-065.test.ts`: Start Turn, Myotismon play pool, deletion/self-sacrifice, Rush/memory, Security and trash-residency negative.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple Tamer. Play cost 4, DP 0. No forms, attributes or types. No inherited text.

#### Printed clauses

1. **[Start of Your Turn]** — "If you have 2 or less memory, set it to 3."
2. **[All Turns]** — "When any of your Digimon with [Myotismon] in their names are played, by
   deleting this Tamer, 1 of those Digimon gains ＜Rush＞ for the turn. Then, gain 1 memory."
3. **[Security]** — "Play this card without paying the cost."

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5180 | You cannot take the part after "Then" without meeting the "by" condition. If you do not delete this card, the [All Turns] effect gives no memory. | Covered behaviourally. `autoDeclineOptional` declines the cost on a real `playCard`: the Tamer stays on the battle area, the played Myotismon has no ＜Rush＞ and cannot attack, and memory moves by the play cost only (10 → 4), never 10 → 5. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Start of Your Turn], gate met | "[Start of Your Turn] sets memory to 3 from 2 through the real turn loop" — `startTurnLoop()` armed with memory 2, stopped in the open Main phase at 3 | `StartOfYourTurn` -> `SetMemory { value: 3, condition: memoryAtMost 2 }` |
| [Start of Your Turn], gate not met | "[Start of Your Turn] leaves memory above the gate untouched" — memory 4 in, 4 out | same |
| [All Turns] full path | "deletes itself to give the played Myotismon ＜Rush＞ and then gains 1 memory" — public `playCard` of BT2-075; the Tamer moves battle area -> trash, memory 10 - 6 + 1 = 5, and the freshly played Digimon then **attacks in the same turn** and takes a security card | `AllTurns` -> `SubTrigger { event: "whenPlayed", sourceFilter: mine / Digimon / nameOrTrait "Myotismon" match "name" }` -> `GainKeyword Rush` (cost `deleteOwn` isSelfRef, `optional`, `abortOnDecline`) then `GainMemory 1` |
| "1 of **those** Digimon" | "grants ＜Rush＞ to the Myotismon that was PLAYED, not to one already on the board" — an established BT2-075 sits first on the board; only the played one gains ＜Rush＞ | `target.sourceRef: "triggerSubject"` |
| Q5180 / declined cost | "Q5180: declining the delete cost grants no ＜Rush＞ and gains no memory" — Tamer stays, no keyword, `attack` refused, memory 10 - 6 only | `optional: true, abortOnDecline: true` on the keyword action, with `GainMemory` after it |
| Name filter, substring | "triggers on a name that only CONTAINS Myotismon (VenomMyotismon)" — BT2-079 played publicly, Tamer trashed, ＜Rush＞ granted, memory 20 - 12 + 1 = 9 | `match: "name"` is `names.some(name => name.includes(token))` in `matching/definition.ts:352` — correct for "with [Myotismon] in their names" |
| Name filter, negative | "does not trigger on a played Digimon without [Myotismon] in its name" — BT1-014 played, Tamer untouched, no memory | same |
| Controller filter | "does not trigger on the OPPONENT's Myotismon (controller: mine)" — seat 1 plays BT2-075 on its own turn; seat 0's Tamer is untouched and no memory is gained | `sourceFilter.controller: "mine"` |
| Zone residency | "is inert once it is in the trash: a later Myotismon play does nothing" and "is inert from the trash at the start of the game too" — see "Priority finding" below | — |
| Duration | "＜Rush＞ lasts only for the turn" — granted in seat 0's Main, gone after the turn is passed through the real loop into seat 1's Main | `duration: "forTheTurn"` |
| [Security] | "[Security] plays itself from the security stack without paying its cost" — seat 0 attacks the player, seat 1's security check plays the Tamer onto seat 1's battle area, memory gauge unchanged (its cost of 4 was never charged) | `Security` (`isSecurity`) -> `PlayWithoutCost { payCost: false, isSelfRef }` |
| Catalog + IR shape | "matches every catalog field and the complete compiled contract" | full compiled record, `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` |

Every behavioural test drives public intents (`playCard`, `attack`, `endPhase`, `surrender`)
plus the production turn loop. No `advance.fire*` / `fireSubTrigger` / `fireTiming` remains in
the file; the committed version proved all four clauses that way and earned no behavioural
credit for any of them.

**Falsifiability check**: removing `target.sourceRef: "triggerSubject"` from the module fails
both the IR test and "grants ＜Rush＞ to the Myotismon that was PLAYED" (the grant lands on the
established decoy instead). The module was restored immediately; it is unchanged in the diff.

#### Priority finding: the trash-residency report does NOT reproduce

The EX10-048 lane reported this Tamer's `[All Turns]` watcher firing while the Tamer sat in the
trash (trigger key `inst-3::subtrigger/6/...`). Driven through public intents it does not
reproduce, and no module or engine change was needed:

- **Deleted mid-game, then a second Myotismon is played** ("is inert once it is in the trash"):
  the first play trashes the Tamer and gains 1 memory (10 -> 5); the second BT2-075 play from
  the same hand grants no ＜Rush＞ and gains no memory (10 -> 4).
- **Seeded in the trash from the start** ("is inert from the trash at the start of the game
  too"): playing BT2-075 grants no ＜Rush＞, gains no memory, and `s.decisions` is exactly `[]` —
  no optional prompt, no target prompt, no ordering prompt is raised for the trash-resident
  Tamer.
- **Direct reproduction attempt of the EX10-048 scenario** (scratch probe, removed afterwards):
  seat 0 with EX10-065 in the trash, BT2-067 on the board and EX10-048 in hand, played with
  `autoOrderTriggers: false`. The only decisions raised were EX10-048's own ("Reduce the play
  cost." and its [On Play] `chooseTargets`). **No `orderTriggers` decision appeared at all**, so
  the trash-resident Tamer never entered the trigger batch.

Conclusion for the coordinator: the observation in `EX10-048.md` "Other findings" item 2 is not
a live defect on the public path. Either it came from injected timing, or the Oikawa instance in
that fixture had been played out of the trash before the key was seen. Nothing to fix in
`EX10-065.ts`, and no retained red is warranted. If the coordinator wants the ordering-batch
claim rechecked, the exact probe fixture is written out above.

intermediate runs).

#### Defects and gaps

- **Module**: none. `EX10-065.ts` is unchanged — the compiled IR already matches all three
  printed clauses, including the substring name test, the trigger-subject target, the
  all-or-nothing "by" cost and the `forTheTurn` duration.
- **Test file**: fully rewritten. The committed version proved the [Start of Your Turn] clause
  with `advance.fireForPermanent`, both [All Turns] branches with `advance.fireSubTrigger` and
  the [Security] clause with `advance.fireForInstance`, so none of them exercised a real play,
  a real turn or a real security check. It also asserted nothing about zone residency, the
  opponent's Myotismon, the name-substring boundary or the duration.
- **Catalog discrepancy (do not edit)**: `EX10-065.effectText` holds a NON-BREAKING SPACE
  (U+00A0) at offset 53 of the second line, after `[Myotismon]` in "…with [Myotismon] in their
  names…". The official list has a plain space. It does not affect matching (the token is
  bracket-delimited), so the catalog test normalizes whitespace and says so. The same artefact
  was reported by the EX10-048 lane on that card — it looks systemic in this set's Purple text.
- **Untested edge, deliberate**: memory exactly 3 at the start of your turn. The gate is "2 or
  less", so 3 is outside it and setting 3 to 3 has no observable difference; the falsifiable
  boundary pair 2 (fires) / 4 (does not) is tested instead.
- **No evolution-stack proof**: this is a Tamer. It has no digivolution stack, no inherited text
  and no evolution route. The peer work is the mixed Myotismon pool instead: an inert
  "Myotismon", a "VenomMyotismon" (substring), a non-Myotismon Digimon, an opponent-controlled
  Myotismon and a second board-resident Myotismon as a decoy.

### EX10-066 — Akihiro Kurata

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-066.test.ts`: Start Turn, Security, End Turn hand boundary, Belphemon host/name, bottom cost, trash evolution/refusal; current catalog text is corrected.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple Tamer, play cost 4, DP 0, no evolution costs, max 4 per deck, rarity U.

#### Printed clauses

1. `[Start of Your Turn]` If you have 2 or less memory, set it to 3.
2. `[End of Your Turn]` If you have 6 or fewer cards in your hand, by placing this Tamer as
   the bottom digivolution card of any of your Digimon with `[Belphemon]` in their names,
   that Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with `[Belphemon]` in its name in the
   trash without paying the cost.
3. `[Security]` Play this card without paying the cost.

No inherited effect.

#### Q&A ids covered

KB-INDEX lists 0 ids for this card. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-066` confirms it:
"(no knowledge-base entries)". The one rules fact the card needs beyond its own text comes
from the Official Rule Manual §5 — "without paying the cost. (The digivolution requirements
have to be met.)" — which is what the requirement negative below asserts.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity and printed text | `matches the catalog and the compiled IR` | whole `compiled` |
| 1 memory set, positive | `sets memory to 3 at the real start of my turn from 2 or less, and leaves 4 alone` | `StartOfYourTurn` -> `SetMemory value: 3` |
| 1 memory set, negative (gauge at 4) | same test, second own turn | `condition: memoryAtMost 2` |
| 2 place as bottom digivolution card + free digivolve from trash + bonus draw | `plays for 4, then at my real turn end places itself under Belphemon and digivolves it from the trash` | `EndOfYourTurn` -> `Digivolve` with `cost.kind: "place"`, `position: "bottom"`, `bindHostAs: "belphemonHost"`, `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: false` |
| 2 "may" (optional refusal) | `is optional: declining at my turn end leaves the Tamer on the board and the trash untouched` | `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true` |
| 2 hand-size gate | `does nothing at turn end with 7 cards in hand` | `condition: zoneCount hand lte 6` |
| 2 host filter and trash-target filter | `refuses a non-Belphemon host and an empty trash: the Tamer is never placed` | `cost.underFilter` / `into.nameOrTrait` `[{ tokens: ["Belphemon"], match: "name" }]` |
| 2 digivolution requirements still apply | `only digivolves into a trash Belphemon whose digivolution requirement the host meets` | `Digivolve` with `payCost: false` (cost waived, requirement not) |
| Tamer inert outside the battle area | `is inert in the trash and in the hand: no memory set, no turn-end digivolution` | permanent-scoped triggers |
| 3 security play for free | `plays itself for free from a real security check opened by the opponent's attack` | `Security` -> `PlayWithoutCost`, `isSecurity: true` |

Both name filters use `match: "name"`, which
`interpreter/matching/definition.ts matchNameOrTrait` implements as a substring match on the
normalized name. That is correct for "with [Belphemon] in their names" / "in its name";
`nameExact` would be wrong here.

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test file proved every clause with injected timing only:
`advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnStartTurn | OnEndTurn, ...)` and
`fireForInstance(EffectTiming.SecuritySkill, ...)`. Under this run's evidence standard that
is structural proof and scores 0 behaviourally, so the previous 10/10 claim was not
supported. It also had these gaps:

- No real turn loop anywhere: neither trigger was ever proved to fire on its own.
- The `[Security]` clause was proved by injecting the timing on a face-down security card,
  not by a security check that an attack opens.
- No proof that the Tamer lands at the BOTTOM of the digivolution stack: the host had no
  pre-existing digivolution cards, so `stack[0]` was trivially the Tamer.
- No proof that the digivolution requirement is still checked.
- No proof that the "may" can be declined.
- No negative for the Tamer being inert in the trash or the hand.
- The memory negative used a hand-set gauge with no turn boundary, so it never showed the
  clause is evaluated at the real start of a turn.

The rewritten file uses `s.engine.startTurnLoop()` plus `advance(...).waitForMainPhase` /
`endMainPhaseIfOpen` for every timing, `playCard` for the Tamer, and `attack` for the
security check. It contains no `advance(...).fire*` call.

#### Module changes

None. The compiled IR is faithful: the placement cost already carries
`destination: "digivolutionStack"`, `position: "bottom"` and `host: "target"`, the digivolve
binds the same host through `fromSelectionRef: "belphemonHost"`, and both name filters are
substring. Only the test file changed.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-066.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1), Tests 9 passed (9)   [logs/EX10-066-vitest.log]
pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck   -> exit 0, no EX10-066 diagnostics
                                        [logs/EX10-066-typecheck.log]
pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-066.ts apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-066.test.ts -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same files> -> "All matched files use the correct format."
git diff --check -> clean
```

#### Catalog discrepancy (reported, not edited)

`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` stores the EX10-066 effect text with:

- a non-breaking space (U+00A0) after each `[Belphemon]` token (offsets 219 and 299);
- the phrase "in its name" duplicated: "...a Digimon card with [Belphemon] in its name in
  its name in the trash...". The official English text prints it once.

Neither affects behaviour — the compiler already produced the right filters — but the
duplicated phrase is a transcription defect in the catalog. The catalog is the coordinator's
file, so the test asserts the stored string verbatim (including the U+00A0 escapes) and the
defect is reported here.

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained red tests; nothing is skipped.
- The `[End of Your Turn]` clause has no once-per-turn wording and no second-firing
  question, so no reset test is needed.
- The clause's interaction with a second Belphemon host (host choice among several) is not
  asserted; `autoSelectCards` would pick arbitrarily and the text places no constraint on
  which host is chosen, so there is nothing to prove beyond the filter, which is proved.

### EX10-067 — Ryoma Mogami

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-067.test.ts`: Start Turn/Security, Save filter, under-Tamer source, evolution-cost reduction, suspend/Alliance grant and duration/refusal.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Purple Tamer, play cost 4, DP 0, type [Hunter], rarity U, max 4 per deck.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| C1 | `[Start of Your Turn] If you have 2 or less memory, set it to 3.` |
| C2 | `[Your Turn] When any of your Digimon digivolve into a Digimon with ＜Save＞ in its text, by suspending this Tamer and placing 1 Digimon card with ＜Save＞ in its text from under your Tamers as that Digimon's bottom digivolution card, that Digimon gains ＜Alliance＞ for the turn.` |
| C3 | `[Security] Play this card without paying the cost.` |

#### Rulings

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card EX10-067` returns **no knowledge-base entries**, which matches
`KB-INDEX.md` (EX10-067, 0 ids). No Q&A id to cover.

Comprehensive rules consulted:

- §16-20 ＜Save＞ — places a card under 1 of your Tamers; the processing is optional.
- §4-3 / §16-21-6 — a card placed under a Tamer that already holds cards goes to the **bottom**.
- §4-22 — "a card with ＜XX＞ in its text" means the icon is printed on the card. §4-22-5 adds
  that parenthetical notes are not text (see "Remaining gaps").
- §16-24 ＜Alliance＞ — the granted keyword itself.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 fires, memory 2 -> 3 | `StartOfYourTurn` / `SetMemory value 3`, `condition memoryAtMost 2` | "sets memory to 3 from 2 or less through the real turn loop" (`startTurnLoop` + `waitForMainPhase(0)`) |
| C1 boundary and zone | same | "leaves 3 or more memory alone, and is inert from the trash" |
| C2 trigger | `YourTurn` / `SubTrigger whenOneOfYoursDigivolves`, `sourceFilter { controllerDefault: mine, kind: [Digimon], keywords: [Save] }` | positive test drives a real `digivolve` intent (BT2-067 -> BT10-076) |
| C2 cost: suspend this Tamer | `cost.costs[0] kind: suspend, isSelfRef` | positive test asserts `ryoma.isSuspended === true`; negatives assert it stays `false` |
| C2 cost: place 1 ＜Save＞ Digimon card from under your Tamers, at the subject's bottom | `cost.costs[1] kind: place`, `filter { controller: mine, kind: [Digimon], zone: underTamers, keywords: [Save] }`, `from: [underTamers]`, `destination: digivolutionStack`, `position: "bottom"`, `host: "triggerSource"` | positive test asserts the exact stack order `[savedA, base]` by instance id, and that exactly one of two banked cards moved |
| C2 recipient and duration | `GainKeyword Alliance`, `target.sourceRef: triggerSubject`, `duration: forTheTurn` | positive test asserts `observe().hasKeyword(subject, "Alliance")` is true on the turn and **false** after the turn loop reaches the opponent's Main |
| C2 optionality ("by <cost>") | prompted optional | "is optional: declining leaves the Tamer unsuspended…" (`autoDeclineOptional`) |
| C2 ＜Save＞ gate on the subject | `sourceFilter.keywords: [Save]` | "ignores a digivolution into a Digimon without ＜Save＞ in its text" (BT4-080 Bakemon) |
| C2 cost zone/filter gates | `zone: underTamers`, `keywords: [Save]` | "pays nothing when the bank is empty, holds a non-＜Save＞ card, or sits under a Digimon" (3 fixtures) |
| C2 controller and `[Your Turn]` | `controllerDefault: "mine"` | "the opponent's own digivolution on their turn does nothing" |
| C2/C1 zone inertness | n/a — a Tamer only acts in play | "is inert from the hand and the trash" |
| C3 | `Security` / `PlayWithoutCost payCost: false`, `isSecurity: true` | "[Security] plays itself for free when a real attack checks it" — seat 1 attacks the player, the security check plays the Tamer |
| Peer | EX10-023's `[Digivolve] While you have [Ryoma Mogami], [Astamon]: Cost 7` | "peer: EX10-023 Quartzmon's cost-7 route needs this exact Tamer IN PLAY, not in the trash" (positive + refused negative through the public `digivolve` intent) |

#### What the previous audit missed

The committed test proved every clause through **injected timing** only:
`advance().fireForPermanent(OnStartTurn, …)`, `advance().fireSubTrigger("whenOneOfYoursDigivolves", …)`
and `advance().fireForInstance(SecuritySkill, …)`. None of it drove a turn, a digivolution
or a security check. Under the re-audit rubric that scores 0 for Behavior. It also used
EX10-065 Yukio Oikawa as the bank host (a Tamer that carries its own `[Start of Your Turn]`
memory clause) and asserted `stack[0]` without a second stack card, so `position: "bottom"`
was never actually distinguished from "directly beneath the top card".

The rewritten test replaces all of it with public intents and `startTurnLoop()`, and adds:
the optional-decline branch, the `[Your Turn]` / "your Digimon" negative, the three
cost-unpayable fixtures, the hand/trash inertness negative, the `forTheTurn` expiry through
the loop, and the EX10-023 cross-card gate.

#### Module changes

None. `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-067.ts` is unchanged, so the compiled IR does not drift
and `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` needs no sync for this card.

#### Fails-when-reverted proof

Mutating the module (`position: "bottom"` removed and `host: "triggerSource"` -> `"self"`)
made the behavioural test fail on the stack order — the banked card no longer landed under
the digivolved Digimon — as well as the IR contract test. Log:
`docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-067-mutation.log`. The module was restored immediately
(`git status` shows only the test file modified in this lane).

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-067.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 11 passed (11)   [logs/EX10-067-vitest.log]

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        -> clean            [logs/EX10-067-typecheck.log]
pnpm exec oxlint <module> <test>          -> no findings
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <module> <test>   -> formatted
git diff --check                          -> clean
```

#### Catalog discrepancies

`effectText` contains a **non-breaking space (U+00A0)** after each `＜Save＞` token instead of
a normal space. The text is otherwise identical to the official wording. The test asserts the
catalog string verbatim, so the quirk is now pinned rather than silently tolerated. No edit
was made to the catalog.

#### Remaining gaps

- **＜Alliance＞ is proved as an active grant, not exercised in a battle.** The test reads the
  production continuous ledger through `observe().hasKeyword` and proves the grant appears and
  expires with the turn, but does not attack with the recipient to show the DP transfer and
  ＜Security A. +1＞ of §16-24. That is ＜Alliance＞'s own machinery, not this card's clause.
- **`[Start of Your Turn]` is not proved silent on the opponent's turn.** `state.memory` is
  signed from the turn player's view and the pass bonus already puts the incoming player at 3,
  so no public line reaches an opponent turn that starts at 2 or less memory with this Tamer
  in play. The trigger keyword is asserted in the IR contract test instead.
- **Engine observation, not a defect of this card:** `textHasKeyword`
  (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts`) matches a keyword icon
  anywhere in `effectText`/`inheritedEffectText`, including inside parenthetical reminder
  notes, which CR §4-22-5 excludes from "text". No fixture in this lane hits it (reminder notes
  spell the keyword out rather than repeating the icon), but it is a cross-cutting risk for any
  "with ＜XX＞ in its text" filter. Coordinator's call.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog/rules | 2 | Exact catalog asserted verbatim (including the U+00A0 quirk); KB confirmed empty; CR §16-20, §4-3, §16-21-6, §4-22, §16-24 read and applied. |
| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a named IR node; the cost host and bottom position are asserted and mutation-verified. |
| Behavior | 2 | All three clauses driven through public intents and the real turn loop, with positives, negatives, an optional decline and duration expiry, and exact endpoints (stack order by instance id, memory, hand, deck, security, trash, no pending decision). Reservation: ＜Alliance＞'s in-battle effect is not exercised. |
| Peer/stack | 2 | Real digivolution stack (Lv.3 -> Lv.4) with asserted source-card order; EX10-023's `[Ryoma Mogami]` gate proved positive and refused through the public digivolve intent; a bank under a Digimon proves the `underTamers` zone boundary. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

### EX10-068 — Digimon Emperor

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-068.test.ts`: Start Main/play/Security, distinct colors, play-cost deletion, same-color return/play, refusal, opponent-controller scope and Q5181/Q5182.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Tamer, White, play cost 5, DP 0, no evolution requirement, no inherited text.

#### Printed clauses (catalog `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`)

1. `[Start of Your Main Phase]` For every 2 colors your opponent's Digimon and Tamers have,
   gain 1 memory.
2. `[On Play]` Delete 1 of your opponent's play cost 5 or lower Digimon.
3. `[On Play]` Then, by returning 1 Digimon card from your opponent's trash to the bottom of
   the deck, from your hand or trash and without paying the cost, you may play 1 level 4 or
   lower Digimon card with the same color as the card this effect returned.
4. `[Security]` Play this card without paying the cost.

Catalog text matches the official card. One cosmetic note only: `effectText` has a double
space between the two bracketed clauses. No edit made (catalog is coordinator-owned).

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Question | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5181 | Returning a Blue/Green two-colour card — which colours may I then play? | `Q5181/Q5182 returns a Blue/Green card to the deck bottom and plays a level-4-or-lower %s Digimon free` (parameterised Blue `BT1-027` and Green `BT1-065`) |
| Q5182 | What does "the same color" mean? | Same test (either colour is enough) plus the negative `Q5182 negative: a Red payload shares no colour ...` |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 scaling `floor(colors / 2)` | `gains 1 memory for 3 opposing colours and 2 for 5, counting Tamers as well as Digimon` | `GainMemory` `amount: 1`, `scaling: { per: 2, unit: "colors" }` -> `interpreter/scaling.ts countColors` (distinct effective colours over battle-area permanents) |
| 1 floor at 1 colour | `gains no memory when the opponent's board shows a single colour` | same |
| 1 controller scope | `counts only the OPPONENT's board: my own multi-coloured Digimon add nothing` | `filter.controller: "opponent"` |
| 1 counts Tamers | `gains 1 memory for 3 opposing colours ...` (the opposing White Tamer is the 3rd colour) | `filter.kind: ["Digimon", "Tamer"]` |
| 1 inert off-board | `is inert in the trash and in the hand ...` | trigger is registered per battle-area permanent only |
| 2 delete, cost ceiling | `[On Play] deletes exactly 1 opposing play-cost-5-or-lower Digimon and pays the printed 5 memory` | `Delete` `filter.playCostLte: 5`, `count: 1`, `controller: "opponent"` |
| 2 no legal target | `[On Play] deletes nothing when every opposing Digimon costs more than 5` | same |
| 3 return cost + destination | both Q5181/Q5182 rows, asserted as the opponent's deck **bottom** | `cost: { kind: "return", to: "deckBottom", target.filter: { zone: "trash", controller: "opponent" } }` |
| 3 colour gate | Q5181/Q5182 positives and the Red negative | `filter.sameColorAsReturned: true` -> `targeting/loose.ts:405` runtime match against `ctx.lastReturnedColors`, plus the transactional preflight in `actions/runAction.ts:639-660` |
| 3 level ceiling | `level boundary: a Blue LEVEL 5 payload is not playable even though the colour matches` | `levelComparison: { op: "lte", value: 4 }` |
| 3 source zones | `plays the same-colour payload out of my TRASH as well as my hand` | `from: ["hand", "trash"]` |
| 3 "you may" | `optional "you may": declining leaves the return unpaid but keeps the mandatory deletion` | `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true` |
| 4 security | `[Security] plays itself into the battle area free during a real security check` | `Security` effect, `PlayWithoutCost` `target: { isSelf: true, filter: { isSelfRef: true } }`, `payCost: false` |
| all | `records the exact catalog and all three printed clauses` | structural |

#### How the proof is driven

Every behavioural case uses public intents only:

- `startTurnLoop()` + `waitForMainPhase(0)` for clause 1, ending each run with a real
  `surrender` intent so the loop resolves. Expected memory is asserted as a **delta against a
  control board** built by the same helper, so no turn-start bookkeeping is baked into the
  number.
- `playCard` for clauses 2 and 3.
- `attack` -> real security check for clause 4.

No `advance.fire*` / `fireSubTrigger` / `fireTiming` call appears in the file. The previous
test file drove **all five** of its behavioural cases through injected timing
(`fireForPermanent(OnPlay/OnStartMainPhase)`, `fireForInstance(SecuritySkill)`); that is the
main defect this re-audit fixes.

Fixtures are inert cards with no printed or inherited text (BT1-009, BT1-013, BT1-014,
BT1-027, BT1-038, BT1-065, BT10-022, BT10-055, BT8-041). `BT16-021` carries text but is only
ever placed in a trash or a deck, where it is inert. No Digi-Egg and no numeric `security: n`
form is used.

#### Mutation check (the assertions bite)

With `sameColorAsReturned: true` removed and `playCostLte: 5` widened to `9` in the module,
4 of 14 tests fail: the structural contract, the cost-ceiling negative, the Q5182 colour
negative, and the security check (the attacker becomes a legal deletion target). Module
restored immediately; `git diff` for `EX10-068.ts` is empty.

Log: `logs/EX10-068-mutation.log`.

#### Defects found and fixed

- **Test file rewritten** (`EX10-068.test.ts`). The previous file had no public-intent proof
  at all, no negative for the level ceiling, no optional-refusal case, no proof of the
  `trash` source zone, no controller-scope negative for the memory trigger, no
  inert-in-trash/hand negative, and asserted the security clause through injected timing.
- **No module change was needed.** The compiled IR already matched every printed clause, and
  the engine already carries the `sameColorAsReturned` capability with a transactional
  preflight. `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` therefore does not drift.

#### Remaining gaps

- No peer module in the repository uses `sameColorAsReturned`; EX10-068 is the sole consumer,
  so there is no second card to cross-check the capability against.
- A Tamer has no digivolution stack and no evolution route, so the rubric's evolution-stack
  requirement is inapplicable. The applicable peer case is covered instead: a copy of
  EX10-068 sits under **opponent** control on the board at the same time as mine (proving the
  trigger and the colour count are both controller-scoped), and the security clause resolves
  against a live opposing attacker.
- Clause 1 is asserted as a delta rather than an absolute memory value, because the turn-loop
  entry sets the dial before the trigger runs. The delta is exact (0, +1, +2) and the control
  board differs only in the opponent's battle area.

| IR trace | **2/2** | Every clause mapped to a compiled action and followed into `countColors`, `loose.ts` colour matching and the `runAction.ts` preflight |
| Behaviour | **2/2** | 13 behavioural cases, all through public intents and the real turn loop, with positives, boundaries, negatives and an optional refusal; mutation-verified |
| Peer / stack | **2/2** | Mirror opposing copy of the same Tamer, opposing multi-colour boards, and a real security check against a live attacker; no stack exists for a Tamer |
| Delivery gates | **0** | Worker lane |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |
