### EX10-069 — Unique Emblem: Gravel Hearts

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-069.test.ts`: Main/Security, Close/Sunarizamon play, Delay accept/refuse, Mineral+LIBERATOR conjunction, evolution cost and illegal-source/peer stack.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Black Option, play cost 3, [LIBERATOR], rarity U, max 4 per deck.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|--------|
| C1 | `[Main] You may play 1 [Sunarizamon] or [Close] from your hand or trash without paying the cost.` |
| C2 | `Then, place this card in the battle area.` |
| C3 | `[Your Turn] When any of your [Close]s suspend, ＜Delay＞` |
| C4 | `・1 of your [Mineral] or [Rock] trait Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with the [Mineral] trait and [LIBERATOR] trait in the hand with the digivolution cost reduced by 3.` |
| C5 | `[Security] Activate this card's [Main] effects.` |

Catalog agrees with the official text. No discrepancy found.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Covered by |
|----|--------|-----------|
| Q5183 | The ＜Delay＞ digivolve target must have BOTH [Mineral] and [LIBERATOR], not either | one positive and two negatives, all through public intents |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
|--------|------|----|
| C1 (hand) | `plays a hand [Sunarizamon] for free and then places itself in the battle area` | `PlayWithoutCost from:["hand","trash"] payCost:false optional:true`, `nameOrTrait ["Sunarizamon","Close"]` |
| C1 (trash, second token) | `also reaches the TRASH, and [Close] is a second legal free play` | same action |
| C1 ("You may") | `"You may": declining the free play still places the Option — the "Then" is mandatory` | `optional: true` |
| C2 | every C1 test asserts the Option permanent and its `placedByEffect` marker | `PlaceInBattleAreaSelf` |
| C2 (colour gate) | `refuses the play with no Black card in play` | `playCard` colour gate, not the IR |
| C3 + C4 | `Q5183 digivolves a [Mineral] host into a [Mineral]+[LIBERATOR] hand card for 3 less, and trashes itself` | `SubTrigger whenSuspended` with `sourceFilter [Close]`, `Digivolve from:["hand"] reduceCost:3` |
| C3 (＜Delay＞ gate) | `§16-17-3: the ＜Delay＞ cannot activate on the turn the Option entered play` | `withIntrinsicDelayGate` -> `delayArmedIntrinsic` in `interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts` |
| C3 (source filter) | `ignores a suspension that is not a [Close]` | `sourceFilter` |
| C3 (＜Delay＞ decline) | `hand-answered: accepting the [Close] suspension but declining the ＜Delay＞ leaves the Option in play` | `optional: true` on the SubTrigger's `Digivolve`, answered through `respondDecision` |
| C4 ([Rock] base) | `the [Rock] half of the base filter is live: a [Rock] Lv.3 host digivolves for 3 less` | `target.filter.nameOrTrait [{tokens:["Mineral","Rock"],match:"trait"}]` |
| C4 (illegal base) | `refuses an illegal base: a Digimon with neither [Mineral] nor [Rock] cannot take the ＜Delay＞ digivolve` | same filter, negative |
| C4 (Q5183 conjunction) | two `Q5183 refuses a hand Digimon with ...` cases | `into: { nameOrTrait:[{tokens:["Mineral"],match:"trait"}], traits:["LIBERATOR"] }` |
| C5 | `[Security] activates the [Main] effects when a real attack checks it` | `ActivateMain`, `isSecurity: true` |

#### What changed

Only `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-069.test.ts`. The module is unchanged, so
`packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` does not drift.

The committed test proved every behavioural clause through injected timing
(`advance.fireForInstance`, `advance.fireSubTrigger`) and reached past the seam by writing
`s.perm("emblem").placedByEffect = true` to keep a Board-Spec-seeded Option alive against the
§17-1-3-2-2 rule sweep. Under the brief's evidence standard that scores 0 in the Behaviour
column. The rewrite drives every clause publicly:

- The Option is now **played** with `playCard`, so the engine's own colour gate, its play cost
  and `PlaceInBattleAreaSelf`'s `placedByEffect` marker are all exercised rather than asserted
  into place.
- The ＜Delay＞ window is reached by a **real [Close] suspension**: playing EX10-028 Landramon
  pays its `[On Play]` cost by trashing an inert [Mineral] digivolution card, which fires
  EX10-063 Close's own `[All Turns]` "by suspending this Tamer, gain 1 memory". That suspension
  goes through `primitives.suspend` -> `fireSuspensionTriggers` -> `whenSuspended`.
- §16-17-3 is proved by the **real turn loop**: the same chain run on the turn the Option
  entered play leaves the ＜Delay＞ unspent; run on the next own turn it fires.
- `[Security]` is proved by a **real attack** that checks security, with no ＜Blocker＞ on the
  defending side so the block window closes on its own.

Fixtures follow the brief: no Digi-Egg and no numeric `security: <n>`; BT10-062 Golemon is the
inert [Mineral] card (no main and no inherited text) and doubles as the Black colour anchor;
BT1-009/013/014 fill decks and security.

#### Round 2: what was added

Still only `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-069.test.ts`. The module is unchanged, so
`packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` does not drift.

1. **The ＜Delay＞'s own decline is now proved.** The round-1 gap was that the window nests two
   optionals (Close's "by suspending this Tamer" cost and the Delay's "trash this card to
   activate" ask) while `autoAcceptOptional` / `autoDeclineOptional` are global. The new test
   drops both flags and answers every `optional` request by hand through the public
   `respondDecision` intent, routed by `DecisionRequest.sourceCardId` read from `s.decisions`
   (helper `answerOptionals`). It accepts the Close suspension and declines EX10-069's own ask,
   then asserts that both prompts really were raised, that the Option stays in the battle area
   and out of the trash (no §16-17-1 cost paid), that the host keeps its top card, that the
   hand card stayed put with no digivolution draw, and that memory is 5.
2. **The `[Rock]` half of the C4 base filter is proved.** BT4-065 Gotsumon is Black Lv.3,
   carries `[Rock]` and NOT `[Mineral]`, and has no main and no inherited text — the only fully
   inert `[Rock]` Lv.3 in the pool. EX8-048 digivolves from any black Lv.3 for 2, so the route
   is legal without help. `delayBoard` now takes the host card id; every existing scenario keeps
   the `[Mineral]` Sunarizamon host.
3. **An illegal-base negative was added.** Board: EX10-063 Close, BT10-062 Golemon (Lv.4,
   `[Mineral]`, inert) carrying an inert `[Rock]` card, and BT3-060 Psychemon (Black Lv.3,
   `[Reptile]`, inert). Landramon's cost trashes the `[Rock]` card from a `[Mineral]` Digimon's
   digivolution cards, so Close suspends and the window really opens — but no legal base exists:
   Golemon and the played Landramon are Lv.4 (EX8-048 needs a black Lv.3), and Psychemon, the
   one black Lv.3, carries neither trait. `preferInstanceIds` biases the selection toward
   Psychemon, so the assertion is that the base filter — not candidate ordering — is what
   refuses it.

#### Commands and results

```
timeout 300 pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-069.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 14 passed (14)
     logs/EX10-069-vitest-round2.log (round 1: 11 passed, logs/EX10-069-vitest-final.log)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        -> clean (logs/EX10-069-typecheck-round2.log)
pnpm exec oxlint <module> <test>          -> 0 findings
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <module> <test>   -> all matched files use the correct format
git diff --check                          -> clean
```

Mutation checks. `logs/EX10-069-mutation-rock.log`: narrowing the base filter's tokens to
`["Mineral"]` fails the IR contract test **and** the new `[Rock]` test — the `[Rock]` token is
load-bearing behaviour, not decoration. `logs/EX10-069-mutation-illegal-base.log`: deleting the
base filter's `nameOrTrait` gate entirely fails the IR contract test **and** the new
illegal-base negative, with Psychemon actually digivolving (`expected 'inst-159' to be
'inst-171'`). The module was restored after each.

Round-1 mutation check (`logs/EX10-069-mutation.log`): collapsing the module's conjunction back to the
single OR-matched `traits: ["Mineral", "LIBERATOR"]` fails 3 tests — the IR contract test and
**both** Q5183 behavioural negatives, the latter with the host actually digivolving
(`expected 'inst-83' to be 'inst-92'`). The conjunction is therefore load-bearing behaviour,
not only a shape assertion. The module was restored immediately.

#### Defects

None found in the module. The Q5183 conjunction, the ＜Delay＞ trash cost, the §16-17-3
entered-play gate and the "impossible payload does not consume the Delay" guard all behave as
printed. No test is retained red; no engine seam is missing for this card.

#### Remaining gaps

1. **The host is Board-Spec seeded, not bred.** The digivolution stack is real after the fact
   (the fuel card is trashed by an effect and the Lv.3 base ends up under the Lv.4), but the
   host itself never came through hatch -> breeding -> battle area. This is a fixture shortcut,
   not an untested clause: the ＜Delay＞ reads the host's traits and level, both of which the
   seeded permanent carries from its real card definition.
2. **The illegal-base negative cannot isolate a single reason on one permanent.** The window
   only opens when a `[Mineral]`/`[Rock]` Digimon loses a digivolution card (that is Close's own
   requirement), so a board where the ONLY Digimon lacks both traits cannot raise the window at
   all. The test therefore keeps a trait-legal but level-illegal Lv.4 as the trigger and puts
   the trait-illegal card at the one legal level; `preferInstanceIds` and the mutation run show
   the base filter is what refuses it.

#### Score

| Column | Score | Why |
|--------|-------|-----|
| Catalog / rules | 2 | Every printed field checked against the catalog; Q5183 read from the KB; CR §16-17-1/§16-17-3 and §17-1-3-2-2 located in the engine and asserted. |
| IR trace | 2 | Every clause mapped to a compiled action; `withIntrinsicDelayGate`, the `delayArmedIntrinsic` branch in `interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts`, the `playCard` colour gate and the Option rule sweep were read to their real semantics. |
| Behaviour | 2 | All five clauses proved through public intents and the real turn loop, with exact endpoints (zones, memory, hand, stack, trash, no pending decision); mutation-verified. |
| Peer / stack | 2 | The chain runs across four peer cards and a real digivolution stack. ### EX10-070 — God Grade Unleashed

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-070.test.ts`: Color waiver, Main draw/place, real link trash, Delay, optional link/Appmon pool, Security and Q5184 rule-trim negative.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Black Option, play cost 2, traits [Appmon]/[Leviathan], rarity C, max 4 per deck.

#### Printed clauses

| # | Clause |
|---|--------|
| 1 | While you have a Digimon or Tamer with the [Appmon] trait on the field, you can ignore this card's color requirements. |
| 2 | [Main] ＜Draw 1＞ Then, place this card in the battle area. |
| 3 | [All Turns] When effects trash any of your Digimon's link cards, ＜Delay＞ ・You may link 1 Digimon card with the [Appmon] trait from your trash to 1 of those Digimon without paying the cost. |
| 4 | [Security] Place this card in the battle area. |

The catalog record matches the official text field for field. No discrepancy found.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Ruling | Covered by |
|----|--------|-----------|
| Q5184 | The [All Turns] effect does NOT trigger when a card gets linked by an effect to an already linked Digimon whose link card is trashed and replaced. | Two tests: the player `linkCard` route and the printed effect route (BT25-070 Logamon's [Main] link). |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
|--------|----|------|
| 1 colour waiver | `Static` / `WaiveColorRequirement` with `youHave` filter `kind: ["Digimon","Tamer"]`, `nameOrTrait [{tokens:["Appmon"], match:"trait"}]` | "is refused as color-requirement-unmet …", "plays off an [Appmon] DIGIMON of another colour …", "plays off an [Appmon] TAMER of another colour …" |
| 2 [Main] draw + place | `Main` / `Draw amount 1` then `PlaceInBattleAreaSelf` | "[Main] draws exactly the top card of deck, pays 2 memory and places itself in the battle area" |
| 3 ＜Delay＞ arming | `AllTurns` / `SubTrigger event whenLinkTrashed`, `sourceFilter {controller:"mine", kind:["Digimon"]}`, `keywords [Delay]` | "§16-17-3 …", "on a later turn, activates the ＜Delay＞ …", "declining the ＜Delay＞ …", "does not arm for an OPPONENT's Digimon's link card …", both Q5184 tests |
| 3 free link | `Link` `from:["trash"]`, `payCost:false`, `optional:true`, target `zone:"trash"`, `kind:["Digimon"]`, `nameOrTrait [Appmon trait]`, `recipient {sourceRef:"triggerSubject", count:1}` | "on a later turn, activates the ＜Delay＞ …" (positive), "does not offer the ＜Delay＞ when the only [Appmon] link material is in HAND …", "does not offer the ＜Delay＞ when the trash holds no linkable [Appmon] Digimon card" |
| 4 [Security] | `Security` / `PlaceInBattleAreaSelf`, `isSecurity: true` | "[Security] places itself in the battle area on a real security check" |

#### What changed

The module `EX10-070.ts` is unchanged — the compiled IR is faithful. The whole change is the
test file, which was rebuilt.

The previous suite proved clause 3 and clause 4 with injected timing and hand-set state:

- `advance(s.engine).verb.trash([...])` for the link-card trash — a direct verb call, not a
  game event, so it earned no behavioural credit under the brief;
- `advance(s.engine).fireForInstance(EffectTiming.SecuritySkill, …)` for [Security] — never a
  real security check;
- `s.perm("godGrade").placedByEffect = true` on a hand-laid Board Spec — the Option was never
  played, so neither the [Main] clause nor the §16-17-3 "not the turn it entered play" gate
  was exercised;
- the colour-requirement waiver (clause 1) had no test at all — only an IR shape assertion.

The rebuilt suite drives every clause through public intents and the production turn loop:

- `playCard` for the Option, with the colour requirement accepted or refused as
  `color-requirement-unmet`;
- BT25-073 Dragomon played from hand as the public link-card trasher — its [On Play] cost is
  "By trashing 1 of your Digimon's link cards", a genuine effect trash (the same vehicle the
  accepted EX10-062 lane uses);
- `s.engine.startTurnLoop()` plus `waitForMainPhase` / `endMainPhaseIfOpen` to carry the played
  Option from its arrival turn to its controller's next turn, so §16-17-3 and the later
  activation are both proved on a card that reached the field by its own [Main] clause;
- a real `attack` on the player for [Security];
- `activateEffect` on BT25-070 Logamon for the effect-driven half of Q5184.

No `advance.fire*`, no `verb.*`, no `placedByEffect` hand-set, and no engine internals remain
in the file.

##### Fixture notes

`traits = forms ∪ attributes ∪ types` (`engine/cards/cardData.ts` `staticTraitsOf`). On EX10
Appmon cards [Appmon] lives in `forms`, not `types` — a `types`-only reading of the catalog
wrongly suggests no Digimon in the game carries the trait.

- EX10-029 Warpmon — Black Lv.4, [Appmon]; the link host.
- BT24-053 Protecmon — Black Lv.3, [Appmon], "[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 1", link effect
  ＜Blocker＞ only; the link card and the link material.
- EX11-027 Maquinamon — carries ＜Link＞ but no [Appmon] trait; used as the host/link pair in
  the negatives. This matters: with an [Appmon] link card, the card Dragomon trashes lands in
  the trash and becomes legal material on its own, so the negative would prove nothing.
- BT21-041 Calendamon — [Tool] trait with a printed ＜Link＞; the material for BT25-070's
  effect link in the Q5184 case.

```

Baseline (the suite as committed, before this lane): 7 passed — `logs/EX10-070-baseline.log`.

Mutation check (`logs/EX10-070-mutation.log`): deleting the `nameOrTrait` [Appmon] reference
from the Link target in `EX10-070.ts` fails three tests, two of them behavioural — both
"does not offer the ＜Delay＞ …" negatives — not only the IR shape test. The module was restored
immediately; it is unmodified in the final tree.

#### Behaviour confirmed

- The colour waiver is real, not cosmetic: with a Red [Appmon] Digimon (BT23-007) or a Red
  [Appmon] Tamer (BT21-084) on the field the Black Option plays; with a Red non-[Appmon]
  Digimon the same intent is refused `color-requirement-unmet` with hand, deck and memory
  untouched.
- [Main] draws exactly the top card of deck (BT1-013, deck left holding BT1-014), pays 2
  memory (5 -> 3) and leaves the Option as a battle-area permanent, not in the trash.
- §16-17-3 holds: a genuine link-card trash on the turn the Option arrived raises no ＜Delay＞
  activation, the Option stays on the field and the [Appmon] material stays in the trash.
- On a later turn the same trigger activates: the Option is trashed as the cost, the [Appmon]
  card leaves the trash and becomes the triggering Digimon's only link card, the trashed link
  card stays trashed, and BT24-053's printed [Link] cost 1 is not charged (memory moves only
  by Dragomon's own play cost 7).
- Declining leaves the Option on the field and links nothing.
- `sourceFilter.controller: "mine"` holds: the opponent trashing their own Digimon's link card
  does not arm the watcher or spend the ＜Delay＞.
- The Link is `from: ["trash"]` and [Appmon]-gated: an [Appmon] copy in hand, or a trash holding
  only a non-[Appmon] ＜Link＞ card and a card with no ＜Link＞ at all, leaves the ＜Delay＞ unspent.
- Q5184 both ways: a `linkCard` intent onto a base-limit-1 host, and BT25-070's [Main] effect
  link onto an already-linked host, each replace the link card through the CR §17-1-3-2-5 rule
  sweep and raise no ＜Delay＞ prompt — even though the replaced [Appmon] card is then sitting in
  the trash as legal material.
- [Security] on a real security check places the Option in the battle area (it survives the
  §17-1-3-2-2 Option sweep because the effect placed it), takes no memory, and runs no [Main]
  draw — the defender's deck and hand are untouched.

#### Observations for the coordinator (no change made)

1. **Two optional prompts per activation.** Accepting the ＜Delay＞ raises a second optional
   prompt from the Link action's own `optional: true`. Both are printed ("＜Delay＞" is an
   activation choice, the bullet says "You may link"), so this is arguably faithful, but it is
   worth a consistent house rule across the ＜Delay＞ family: the accepted EX10-062 lane's
   scripted-answer helper assumes one prompt per clause. The test documents the order.
2. **Recipient wording.** The printed text is "1 of those Digimon" (plural source), and the IR
   binds `sourceRef: "triggerSubject"` — one recipient per firing. `whenLinkTrashed` fires once
   per trashed link instance (`primitives.ts` `trash`), so one firing always has exactly one
   subject and the singular binding is correct. No engine gap.

### EX10-071 — Paradise Lost

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-071.test.ts`: Public trash End Turn, Main Lucemon grants/expiry, real Security play/refusal and Q5185–Q5187; injected probes supplemental and catalog corrected.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Option, Purple/Yellow, play cost 2, [Seven Great Demon Lords], U, 4 per deck.

#### Printed clauses (catalog)

1. **[Static]** "While you have a Digimon with [Lucemon] in its name on the field, you can
   ignore this card's color requirements."
2. **[Trash] [End of Your Turn]** "If you have a Digimon with [Lucemon] in its name, by
   returning this card to the bottom of the deck, trash your top security card and 1 of your
   Digimon attacks without suspending."
3. **[Main]** "Until your opponent's turn ends, 1 of your Digimon with [Lucemon] in its name
   gains ＜Raid＞, ＜Piercing＞, ＜Blocker＞ and +3000 DP."
4. **[Security]** "You may play 1 Digimon with [Lucemon] in its name from your trash without
   paying the cost."

There is no ＜Delay＞ on this card; the coordinator note's ＜Delay＞ item does not apply.

#### Catalog discrepancies (reported, not edited)

- `effectText` doubles the phrase: "a Digimon with [Lucemon] in its name **in its name**" in
  both clause 1 and clause 2. Cosmetic mangling; the meaning is unambiguous.
- `securityEffectText` reads "You may play 1 **[Lucemon] in its name** from your trash" — the
  words "Digimon with" were dropped by the same mangling. Two things follow:
  - the name test is a SUBSTRING test ("in its name"), so `match: "name"` is right, not
    `nameExact`;
  - the clause is limited to Digimon. The compiled `PlayWithoutCost` filter had no `kind`
    gate. Fixed locally (see below). No behavioural difference exists today: every card in
    the catalog whose name contains "Lucemon" is a Digimon (12 prints, checked).

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Question | Where it is proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5185 | Used this card, it went to the trash — can its [Trash] [End of Your Turn] effect then be activated? Yes. | `Q5185/Q5187 through the real turn loop` — `playCard` for the Option, its [Main] effect resolves, the card lands in the trash, and the SAME turn's production End phase activates its trash clause. |
| Q5186 | 0 security cards — can it be activated? Yes; the Digimon still attacks. | `Q5186 through the real turn loop` — seat 0 has an empty security stack; the return cost is still paid and the attack still happens. |
| Q5187 | Can the attacking Digimon attack even if it is suspended? Yes. | Same test as Q5185: the Lucemon declares a NORMAL attack in Main (which suspends it), and the end-of-turn clause then makes it attack a second time. `isSuspended` is read inside the engine's own call stack at the second `attackDeclared`. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 [Static] colour waiver | `waives the Purple/Yellow requirement only while a Lucemon is on the field` — two real `playCard` intents: `color-requirement-unmet` with a Red board, `{ok:true}` with WHITE BT18-086 on board (which supplies neither printed colour, so only the waiver can carry the play). | `Static` / `WaiveColorRequirement` with a `youHave` Digimon-name condition |
| 2 gate ("if you have a Lucemon") | `does not return itself or attack without a Lucemon` | effect-level `condition` (hard gate) plus the same condition on the leading action |
| 2 cost (return to deck bottom) | Q5185 loop test asserts the card leaves the trash and is the LAST card of the deck; `CR 15-7-4: declining the bottom-deck condition…` proves the declined branch leaves it in the trash | `cost: { kind: "return", to: "deckBottom" }`, `optional`, `abortOnDecline` |
| 2 "trash your top security card" | Q5185 loop test: seat 0's security goes 2 -> 1 and the named top card appears in the trash; Q5186 loop test: 0 security, clause still resolves | `trashSecurityTop`, `controller: "mine"`, `count: 1` |
| 2 "1 of your Digimon attacks without suspending" | Q5185 loop test: second `attackDeclared`, declared while suspended, followed by a real `securityChecked` | `Attack`, `withoutSuspending: true` |
| 3 [Main] four grants on ONE Digimon | `Main offers every Lucemon-named Digimon and no other, then buffs exactly one of them`; `Main grants Raid, Piercing, Blocker, and +3000 DP to the same Lucemon` | `bindAs: "lucemonBuffTarget"` + three `fromSelectionRef` grants and `ModifyDP`, all `untilOpponentTurnEnd` |
| 3 duration "until your opponent's turn ends" | `untilOpponentTurnEnd: the four grants live through the opponent's whole turn and lapse at my next Main` — the Option is PLAYED in seat 0's Main under the real turn loop; the grants survive seat 0's turn end, are still live through seat 1's whole turn (public proof: seat 1 attacks the player, a block window opens only because the granted ＜Blocker＞ is live, and seat 0's `declareBlock` with the Lucemon is accepted and resolves — the 3000 DP attacker dies on the 15000 DP blocker and no security is checked), and are all gone at seat 0's next Main | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` on all four actions |
| 3 name filter | same test: the non-Lucemon decoy is not among the offered candidates and is unbuffed | `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Lucemon"], match: "name" }]` (substring) |
| 4 [Security] | `[Security] fires from a REAL security check…` — seat 1 has no ＜Blocker＞, so seat 0's attack on the player resolves on its own, the check reveals EX10-071, and EX10-060 is played from seat 1's trash for free; `Security offers only Lucemon cards…` and `Security refusal plays nothing…` cover the mixed pool, the empty-trash case and the "may" refusal | `PlayWithoutCost`, `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: false`, `optional: true` |

#### Defect fixed locally

- `EX10-071.ts`, `Security` effect: added `kind: ["Digimon"]` to the `PlayWithoutCost` target
  filter. The printed clause plays a **Digimon**; the compiled filter matched any card kind.
  Unobservable today (no non-Digimon Lucemon print exists), so it is a fidelity fix, not a
  behaviour change. The compiled IR changed, so `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json`
  now drifts for this card — the coordinator's sync is required.

#### Weaknesses in the previous audit (all fixed)

Every clause of the committed test proved its behaviour through injected timing
(`advance().fireForInstance(EffectTiming.OnEndTurn, …)` and
`advance().fireForInstance(EffectTiming.SecuritySkill, …)`), which the brief scores 0 in the
Behavior column. Three production proofs were added:

- the trash clause now runs inside the real turn loop's End phase, reached by `endPhase`;
- Q5185 is now the actual question asked — the card is PLAYED this turn and activates from the
  trash it landed in, instead of being seeded there;
- the Security clause now fires from a real security check driven by an `attack` intent.

#### Peer comparison (round 2)

The duration test also plays **EX10-014 Weatherdramon** in the same Main. Its `[On Play]` grants
＜Security A. -1＞ "until their turn ends" — the same printed duration, compiled to the same
`untilOpponentTurnEnd` ref. In one fixture both grants are asserted live during seat 1's turn and
both are gone at seat 0's next Main, so the two cards are proved to share the duration semantics
rather than each card asserting the string in isolation.

#### Fixture notes for other lanes

- A board seeded `suspended: true` cannot prove Q5187 under the turn loop: seat 0's own Active
  phase unsuspends it before Main opens. Suspend it with a real attack instead.
- The two injected-timing trash tests seeded security with BT1-001, a Digi-Egg, which can never
  legally sit in a security stack. Replaced with BT1-013 Muchomon (inert main-deck Digimon);
  the `security` alias and every assertion are unchanged.
- The duration fixture runs with `autoDeclineOptional: true`. With `autoAcceptOptional` the
  Option's own [Trash] [End of Your Turn] clause pays its return cost at seat 0's turn end and
  fires an extra attack that clears seat 1's board, which destroys the block fixture on the next
  turn. Declining aborts that clause cleanly (the CR 15-7-4 test covers the declined branch).
- EX10-060 Lucemon: Satan Mode is the wrong Lucemon for any attack fixture — it prints
  "[When Attacking] … this Digimon unsuspends". The turn-loop tests use BT7-111 Lucemon: Chaos
  Mode, whose only clauses are play/digivolve-time.

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-072.test.ts`: Main, opponent-turn Delay, face-up Dark Masters filter, real Security hand/trash play, delayed deletion/order and Apocalymon peer.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:

delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Option, White, play cost 3,
types `["Dark Masters"]`, rarity C, max 4 per deck. No level, no DP, no evolution costs.
Catalog text matches the official list; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

| # | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| C1 | While you don't have [Spiral Mountain] in the battle area, you can ignore this card's colour requirements. |
| C2 | [Main] ＜Draw 2＞ Then, place this card in the battle area. |
| C3 | [End of Opponent's Turn] ＜Delay＞ You may play 1 face-up Digimon card with the [Dark Masters] trait from your security stack without paying the cost. |
| C4 | (same clause, tail) At the end of your turn, delete the Digimon this effect played. |
| C5 | [Security] You may play 1 Digimon card with the [Dark Masters] trait from your hand or trash without paying the cost. |
| C6 | Then, add this card to the hand. |
| C7 | At turn end, delete the Digimon this effect played. |

`[Spiral Mountain]` in C1 is an exact name; `[Dark Masters]` in C3/C5 is an exact trait.

#### Q&A ids covered

| Id | Substance | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5744 | Both the ＜Delay＞-played and the [Security]-played Digimon are deleted at their stated turn end. | ＜Delay＞ turn-loop test (C4) and [Security] real-attack test (C7). |
| Q5745 | The turn-end delete and another effect triggering at that same turn end are simultaneous processing; the turn player picks the order. | "simultaneous turn-end processing (Q5745)" test — both processings complete at the one turn end. Partial: see Remaining gaps. |
| Q6241 | EX10-072 in the battle area is a legal [Dark Masters] trait card to place under BT15-102 Apocalymon for its play-cost reduction. | "peer interaction (Q6241)" test. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR (`EX10-072.ts`) | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | `effects[0]` Static `WaiveColorRequirement` + `youHaveNone` on `nameOrTrait match:"name"`, `zone:"battleArea"` | "plays with no white source…" (positive) and "does not waive the colour requirement while a [Spiral Mountain] is in the battle area" (negative, both through `playCard`) |
| C2 | `effects[1]` Main `Draw{amount:2}` then `PlaceInBattleAreaSelf` | "plays with no white source, draws 2, and places itself" — exact hand, deck, battle area, trash, memory endpoints |
| C3 | `effects[2]` EndOfOpponentsTurn, `keywords:[Delay]`, `PlayWithoutCost{from:["security"], payCost:false, optional:true, filter.faceUp:true}` | "plays a FACE-UP [Dark Masters] Digimon from security…" (real turn loop) and the `autoDeclineOptional` refusal test |
| C4 | `effects[2].actions[1]` `DelayedDeletePlayed` (default `endOfOwnerTurn`) | same test, seat 1's own next turn end |
| C5 | `effects[3]` Security `PlayWithoutCost{from:["hand","trash"], bindResultAs:"playedByThisEffect"}` | three real-security-check tests: hand play, trash play, non-matching-card negative |
| C6 | `effects[3].actions[1]` `AddToHandSelf` | "…returns itself to hand…" and "still returns itself to the hand when there is no [Dark Masters] card to play" |
| C7 | `effects[3].actions[2]` `SubTrigger{event:"endOfTurn", on:{filter:{boundRef:"playedByThisEffect"}}}` + inner `Delete` on `isSelfRef` | "…deletes the played Digimon at THAT turn's end (Q5744)" — with an untouched bystander as the control |

The asymmetry between C4 and C7 is real and is asserted structurally as well: C4's ＜Delay＞
resolves on the OPPONENT's turn, so its delete waits for the controller's own next turn end
(`DelayedDeletePlayed` default `endOfOwnerTurn`); C7's [Security] effect resolves on the
opponent's turn and deletes at the end of THAT turn, so it is an unguarded `endOfTurn`
watcher anchored to the played permanent.

#### What this re-audit changed

The module (`EX10-072.ts`) needed no change: every clause behaves correctly under public
intents. The defect was in the evidence, not the implementation.

`EX10-072.test.ts` was rewritten. The previous file proved C3–C7 only through injected
timing — `advance(...).fireForPermanent(EffectTiming.OnEndTurn, …)`,
`fireForInstance(EffectTiming.SecuritySkill, …)` and `fireArmedSubTriggers("endOfTurn", …)`
— which the brief scores 0 in the Behavior column. It also hand-poked
`s.perm("spiral").placedByEffect = true` to survive the §17-1-3-2-2 rule sweep, a workaround
for a state the public [Main] play sets on its own.

New proofs, all through public intents and the production turn loop:

- ＜Delay＞ over four real turns: seat 1 plays EX10-072 with `playCard` (which sets
  `placedByEffect`, so it survives the rule sweep into the next turn — asserted), the
  ＜Delay＞ fires at the end of seat 0's turn, and the played Digimon is deleted at the end
  of seat 1's own next turn. The delete is confirmed to be a deletion, not a disappearance,
  by EX10-057's own [On Deletion] landing it face up at the bottom of security.
- Face-down control: a second [Dark Masters] card sits face-down in the same security stack
  and is never a candidate.
- ＜Delay＞ declined (`autoDeclineOptional`): nothing is played, the security card stays.
- [Security] through a real attack on the player, not an injected `SecuritySkill` fire:
  three variants (play from hand, play from trash, non-matching card refused) plus the
  no-candidate case, and the turn-end delete asserted with a bystander control.
- Q6241 peer/stack case: EX10-072 established in the battle area is placed under BT15-102
  Apocalymon as one of its play-cost [Dark Masters] cards.
- Q5745 simultaneity: BT15-079 Piedmon's `[End of Opponent's Turn] Delete this Digimon`
  gives the same turn end a second processing alongside the [Security] delete; both resolve.

No defect required a module edit and no test is retained red.

  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism --testTimeout=20000
=> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 11 passed (11)
   (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-072-vitest-final.log)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
=> exit 0, no diagnostics (docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/logs/EX10-072-typecheck.log)

=> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same two files>
=> all matched files use the correct format
git diff --check
=> clean
```

Two intermediate vitest runs (`EX10-072-vitest-1.log`, `-2.log`, `-3.log`) are kept in
`logs/`. Run 1 failed two ＜Delay＞ cases because seat 0's Main phase auto-passed with no
legal action, so a raw `endPhase` returned `wrong-phase`; the fix was
`advance(...).endMainPhaseIfOpen(seat)`, not a fixture change.

#### Remaining gaps

- **Q5745 is covered only in outcome, not in choice.** The ruling's substance is that the
  turn player *may choose* the resolve order of the simultaneous turn-end processings. The
  test proves both processings complete at that single turn end; it does not assert an
  `orderTriggers` decision is offered to the turn player, because in this pairing the two
  processings belong to the non-turn player (seat 0) while the turn player is seat 1, and
  the outcome is order-independent (two deletions, no shared target). Proving the *choice*
  needs a pair of same-controller turn-end processings whose outcomes differ by order; no
  such pair exists among EX10-072's own clauses. Reported, not worked around.
- The ＜Delay＞'s `DelayedDeletePlayed` falls back to `ctx.source.permanent()` when nothing
  was played (`removal.ts` `case "DelayedDeletePlayed"`). Here the Option was already
  trashed as the ＜Delay＞ activation cost, so that lookup is undefined and nothing is armed;
  the declined-branch test confirms no stray delete. Noted as a latent sharp edge in the
  shared action, not a defect of this card.

| IR trace | 2/2 | All four IR effects mapped clause by clause, including the deliberate C4/C7 timing asymmetry and the `boundRef` anchor; structural assertions pin the parts a behavioural test cannot see (the absent `faceUp` gate on [Security], the absent `endOfCurrentTurn` timing on ＜Delay＞). |
| Behavior | 2/2 | Every clause proved through `playCard`, a real attack-driven security check and the production turn loop, with exact endpoints and negative controls; no injected timing anywhere in the file. |
| Peer / stack | 2/2 | Q6241 proved by placing EX10-072 under BT15-102 Apocalymon through the public play; Q5745 simultaneity proved against BT15-079's competing turn-end processing; bystander controls in both delete proofs. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane. |
| **Total** | **8/10** | |

#### Defects found in the committed test (all fixed locally)

1. **Seam violation.** The file defined `primitivesOf(s)` and cast through
   `engine.primitives` three times. The brief scores that 0 for peer/stack. Replaced with
   `advance(s.engine).verb.trash(...)`, which is the sanctioned seam verb over the same
   production primitive.
2. **Injected timing on both link windows.** `[When Digivolving]` and
   `[End of Opponent's Turn]` were proved only by `advance().fireForPermanent(...)` —
   structural, no behavioural credit. Replaced: `[When Digivolving]` now fires from a real
   `appFusion` intent (App Fusion is a digivolution, `digivolve.ts:886` fires
   `WhenDigivolving`), and `[End of Opponent's Turn]` now fires from
   `startTurnLoop()` + `endMainPhaseIfOpen(0)` with Deusmon on seat 1. No
   `advance().fire*` call remains in the file.
3. **`verb.appFuseInto` instead of the public intent.** The brief names this explicitly.
   Both App Fusion tests now use `applyIntent(0, { type: "appFusion", ... })`.
4. **Vacuous Q5188 test.** The old test passed for the wrong reason. Its `setupEngine`
   carried **no** decision-automation flags, so the §17-1-3-2-5 sweep's
   "which excess link card do you give up?" prompt (`GameEngine.chooseExcessLinkCards`,
   Q6370) hung unanswered, the trim never happened, and "the opponent's Digimon survived"
   asserted nothing. Proven by the fix run: with the flags added the board ends at 2 links
   with exactly one old link in the trash; without them it stayed at 3. Added
   `autoAcceptOptional` / `autoSelectCards`, plus assertions that exactly one of the two old
   links reached the trash and that no decision is left pending.
5. **＜Link +1＞ had no behavioural proof at all** — only a `compiled.effects[...].keywords`
   shape assertion. Added a rule-sweep test driven by a real `attack` intent.
6. **Weak App Fusion negative.** "Only one name present" was tested with no link material at
   all, which any refusal reason satisfies. Now the host carries a **wrong** linked material
   (BT21-041 Calendamon), so the refusal is specifically about the `[Cometmon]` name.

#### Peer comparison on one board (EX10-030 Cometmon)

`crossFireBoard()` seats EX10-073 Deusmon and EX10-030 Cometmon side by side on seat 0, each
carrying its own link card, against a cost-3 and a cost-5 opponent Digimon at 20 000 DP. Both
cards print the same `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` -> `SubTrigger whenLinkTrashed` shape with
`isSelfRef` on the `sourceFilter`; the payloads differ (Deusmon deletes the lowest-play-cost
opponent Digimon, Cometmon gives 1 opponent Digimon -8000 DP for the turn). Each test trashes
**exactly one** link card and asserts both payloads' endpoints:

| trashed link | fires | endpoints asserted |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Deusmon's | Deusmon only | seat 1 battle area is exactly `[oppHigh]` (cost-3 deleted); `oppHigh.currentDP === 20_000` (no -8000); Cometmon still holds its own link; seat 0 trash is exactly the Deusmon link |
| Cometmon's | Cometmon only | `oppHigh.currentDP === 12_000`; seat 1 battle area is still exactly `[oppLow, oppHigh]` (no delete) and `oppLow.currentDP === 20_000`; Deusmon still holds its own link; seat 0 trash is exactly the Cometmon link |

Both tests end with no pending decision and no `actionRejected`. `preferInstanceIds` names the
cost-5 Digimon, so a cross-firing -8000 would land on the very permanent the isolation
assertion reads — the negative is decisive rather than lucky. Deusmon's own Delete is narrowed
to `lowestPlayCost`, so the same preference cannot steer it.

The link trash uses `advance().verb.trash` (the sanctioned seam over the production `trash`
primitive, `byEffectSeat` set). BT25-073 Dragomon's `[On Play]` cost
"By trashing 1 of your Digimon's link cards" was considered as a fully public route and
rejected: its cost sits inside an `optional` + `abortOnDecline` `Modal` that must resolve a
free play or a free Option use of a `[TS]` card, and no inert `[TS]` card with cost 5 or less
exists in the catalog, so the route would either abort the cost or drag a second `[On Play]`
into the board. Recorded as a fixture gap, not worked around.

#### Notes for readers of the module

`＜Link +1＞` is *not* a `grantedKeywords` entry. `interpreter/actions/board.ts:422` routes
the `"Link"` (and `"LinkMax"`) keyword to `fx.grantLinkMax`, and `mindLink.linkMax` sums that
delta onto `BASE_LINK_MAX = 1`. The observable is `observe().linkMaxDelta(...)` /
`engine.linkMaxOf(...)`, never `observe().keywordAmount(perm, "Link")` (which reads 0 and is
correct). The test now says so.

| drop `isSelfRef` from the `whenLinkTrashed` `sourceFilter` | 2 tests red (`trashing ANOTHER Digimon's link card does NOT fire`, `peer isolation: trashing COMETMON's link card ...`) |

### EX10-074 — Beelzemon

#### Current revalidation (2026-09-12)

Independent Luna review of current catalog, KB, module and complete colocated
suite `apps/api/src/cards/EX10/EX10-074.test.ts`: Alternate/Blast Digivolve, three timings, mill/play-cost scaling, exact deck-top cost, De-Digivolve, Overflow, order and Baalmon peer.
The 76-file collection baseline passed this suite. Card-level evidence credit:
catalog/rules 2/2, executable IR 2/2, behavioral proof 2/2, peer/stack 2/2;
delivery gates 2/2: **10/10**. Closing gates and committed delivery are shared
across the collection. The older report below is historical.

#### Historical report (2026-09-08)

Lane: card-only worker. Worktree `audit-ex10-reaudit`. Module unchanged; the test file was
rewritten. No git writes.

#### Card summary

Catalog (`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`): Lv.6 Digimon, Purple/Black, play cost 7,
DP 12000, Mega, Virus, [Demon Lord] [Seven Great Demon Lords], UR, `isAce: true`,
`overflowMemory: 4`. Evo costs Purple Lv.5 / Black Lv.5, memory 4. No inherited or security text.
The catalog text matches the official list; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] While you have 20 or more cards in trash, Lv.3 w/[Impmon] in name: Cost 4` —
   an alternate route with a live condition. "w/[Impmon] in name" is a **substring** gate.
2. `[Hand] [Counter] ＜Blast Digivolve＞`.
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] [When Attacking] Trash the top 2 cards of your deck. Then,
   delete 1 of your opponent's play cost 6 or lower Digimon. For every 10 cards in your trash,
   add 3 to the play cost maximum.`
4. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] If you have 10 or more cards in your trash, by returning 2
   non-Digi-Egg cards from your trash to the top of the deck, ＜De-Digivolve 2＞ 1 of your
   opponent's Digimon.`
5. ACE: ＜Overflow -4＞ (rule-side, from `isAce` + `overflowMemory`).

#### Q&A ids covered

| Q&A | Ruling | Proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5189 | The [On Play]/[When Digivolving] effects trigger simultaneously; the player chooses the activation order. | `Q5189: the two [On Play] clauses are simultaneous and the player picks the order` — drives the real `orderTriggers` decision (`autoOrderTriggers: false`) and runs BOTH orders; the two orders end with different decks. |
| Q5190 | A "by" cost cannot be partly paid: exactly 2 cards must be returned. | `Q5190: pays by returning exactly 2 non-Digi-Egg cards to the DECK TOP and De-Digivolves 2` (positive) and `Q5190: one non-Digi-Egg card among Digi-Eggs cannot partially pay the return cost` (negative). |
| Q5191 | With 20+ cards in trash, ＜Blast Digivolve＞ may digivolve a Lv.3 [Impmon] into this card during the opponent's counter timing. | `Q5191: ＜Blast Digivolve＞ from hand at counter timing over the 20-trash Impmon path`, plus the 19-trash negative. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Alternate Digivolve, 20-trash gate | `allows the Impmon alternate digivolution only with 20 or more cards in trash` (19 refused / 20 accepted, cost 4 charged, source card in the stack) | `digivolutionRequirement[0].whileCondition zoneCount trash >= 20`, `cost: 4` |
| "w/[Impmon] in name" is a substring | `the alternate route reads [Impmon] as a name SUBSTRING, and refuses a non-Impmon Lv.3` (BT12-073 "Impmon (X Antibody)" accepted; BT1-009 Monodramon refused) | `digivolutionRequirement[0].names: ["Impmon"]` — `names` is the substring field `digivolutionRequirementsFor` reads (see `reencoded-ir.test.ts:405`) |

Negative at 19 trash: not eligible, stays in hand. | `effects[0] trigger Counter, isFromHand, keywords BlastDigivolve` |
| Mill 2, then delete, ceiling 6 | `[When Attacking] fires on a real attack, mills 2, and holds the printed cost-6 boundary` (public `attack` intent; cost-6 deleted, cost-7 survives, security checked) | `TrashTopDeck 2` + `Delete playCostLte 6` |

#### Defects found in the committed audit

The committed test file passed 10/10 but did not prove what it claimed:

1. **Q5189 was not covered at all.** Every test passed `autoOrderTriggers: true`, which answers
   the order prompt with the engine's own order. Nothing asserted that a prompt was raised or
   that the choice mattered. Now proved with `autoOrderTriggers: false`, both orders driven, and
   an order-dependent endpoint (which two cards end up in the deck).
2. **A silently-timing-out `settle` hid a wrong claim.** The old Q5190 test settled on
   `topCard.cardId === "BT1-010"`, which never becomes true (`popFromStack` takes the card
   directly under the top, so the first peel exposes the LAST `under` entry). `settle` returns
   quietly after its tick budget, so the test spun and then asserted only `stack` length 2 —
   consistent with a single peel. `amount: 2` was therefore never proved. Worse, the fixture
   stacked only Lv.3 sources, and `deDigivolve`'s `levelFloor = 3` stops after one peel there.
   The new test uses Lv.4/Lv.5 sources and asserts both peeled cards by id.
3. **"to the top of the deck" was never asserted.** The old test only counted non-`BT1-001`
   cards in the trash. The new test asserts the paid cards by instance id on the deck top.
4. **Three clauses rested on injected timing.** `[When Attacking]`, the decline path and the
   Q5190 negative all used `advance().fireForPermanent(...)`, which earns no behavioural credit.
   All three now run through public intents (`attack`, `playCard`).
5. **The Q5190 negative was near-vacuous.** It fired OnPlay and asserted the stack was unchanged
   without any `settle`, so it would have passed even if the effect had never started.
6. **The substring name gate was untested.** `EX2-039` is named exactly "Impmon", so the old
   positive could not tell a substring gate from an exact one, and there was no wrong-name
   negative.

No engine or module defect was found. The module's IR is faithful; nothing in
`EX10-074.ts` was changed, so `effects.json` does not drift.

#### Revert sensitivity

A single mutation run over the module (`names: ["Impmon"] -> ["Yaamon"]`, `raise: 3 -> 0`,
`DeDigivolve amount: 2 -> 1`, return `count: 2 -> 1`) fails **9 of the 14** tests. The module was
restored immediately afterwards (`git diff --stat` shows the module unchanged).

```

Fixture correction after the coordinator's review: both Q5191 tests seeded seat 1's security
with `BT1-002`, a Digi-Egg, which the brief forbids. Both now seed `BT1-013` (Muchomon, an inert
main-deck Lv.3 Digimon). Every assertion was kept unchanged and the focused file was re-run under

with oxlint, `oxfmt --check` and `git diff --check` clean afterwards. The earlier typecheck run
stands: the change is inside a test fixture literal and the module is still untouched.

#### Remaining gaps

- **`[When Attacking]` scaled ceiling.** Proved at the printed base of 6 through a real attack;
  the +3-per-10 scaling is proved on the `[On Play]` copy only (same compiled `playCostCeiling`).
- **Turn loop.** No clause on this card is once-per-turn or duration-bound, so nothing here
  needs a next-turn reset; the tests use single-window flows rather than `startTurnLoop`.
- **Overflow ordering (CR §4-18-5-1).** `applyOverflow` documents that a player's own
  simultaneous Overflow instances are processed in array order instead of by free choice. Not a
  defect of this card; noted because this card is an ACE.

## Mechanisms

### Simultaneous DigiXros material expansion (2026-09-12)

Q5177 and Q5179 require one paid expander activation to authorize independent
material quotas for every qualifying card in a simultaneous play batch. A
rule-valid synthetic caller in the existing `src/engine/digiXrosPreparation.test.ts`
uses public `playCard` to invoke a paid `PlayWithoutCost` action with `count: 2`,
`costReduction: 9`, and `allowDigiXros: true`. EX10-064 and both EX10-058 targets
retain their real modules. The accepted reproduction fails at the two-material
stack assertion because the baseline interpreter prepares only a one-card picker.

An earlier unreduced fixture seeded memory 30 and failed before both placements:
the gauge clamps each payment to the legal range, leaving the second printed cost
unpayable. That fixture failure is rejected as Q5177 evidence. The final tests
start at memory 9 and isolate batch preparation and per-card material selection.
The synthetic caller override is confined to an existing justified engine test
seam and restored in `finally`.

The engine prepares all qualifying pending cards in one replacement window, pays
each matching Tamer's optional suspension once, and records its zone grant against
every qualifying pending instance. Each card has its own picker, quotas, material
IDs, cost reduction, stack placement, and On Play DigiXros count. Scheduled play
cards and materials reserved by an earlier picker are excluded from all material
zones. Preparation exceptions and aborted pickers clear all temporary per-play
grants; persistent grants remain intact. The primitive adapter preserves the
legacy singular preparation hook when the batch hook is unavailable.

The added public-intent cases prove two independent under-Tamer/trash pairs from
one Tamer, two Tamers granting quota two independently to each play, and batch
refusal with unchanged source zones and no suspension. Accepted material payments
end at memory 6; refusal ends at memory 2. Focused cases are green. Restricting the expansion loop temporarily to the
primary pending ID fails the exact batch test: the second stack is empty instead
of containing its distinct material pair. Restoring the original bytes makes the
case pass. The closing 355-file regression and shared/API/web typecheck pass.
The engine fix and tests are committed in `cf445a05c`.

### Seams 9 and 21 — simultaneous trigger ordering, and pending activation across windows

Source: `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/SEAM-9-21-MECHANISM.md`.

#### Seam 9 `simultaneous-same-timing-trigger-ordering`

##### Mechanism

Two independent defects hid behind one symptom ("no `orderTriggers` prompt").

**9a — the activation gate kept a mandatory effect out of the ordering tier.**
`drainCurrentTimingWindow` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/stack.ts`) groups collected effects by
*activation tier*: everything that becomes activatable in the same pass shares a tier, and only the
newest tier is offered to `chooseOrder`. `canActivateEffect`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/effect.ts`) refused any effect whose every action is
board-targeted when the board holds no candidate ("Board-targeted actions gate activation", citing
BT3-014). EX10-023 Quartzmon prints two `[When Digivolving]` clauses — suspend everything, then
delete 1 *suspended* opposing Digimon. On pass 1 nothing is suspended yet, so only the suspend
clause was activatable; the delete clause entered a later tier and resolved alone. The controller
never chose an order.

**9b — the `whenAttacking` watcher bus fired outside the attack's timing window.**
`CombatController.resolveAttack` (`apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts`) ran
`fireTiming(OnUseAttack)` first and the `whenAttacking` / `whenOpponentAttacks` SubTrigger bus
afterwards. Every other event already folds its watchers into its window through
`GameEngine.withPendingSubTriggers` (play, digivolve, unsuspend, turn end, opponent draw); attack
declarations did not. So EX10-009 Creepymon's printed `[When Attacking]` and BT24-078's
`{Trash} [Your Turn]` watcher, which react to the same declaration, reached the player as three
independent `optional` prompts in engine order.

The second half of the EX10-009 red (a queued `[When Attacking]` entry whose source stops being the
top card must be retired) needed no new code: `stack.ts`'s presence diff plus
`identityAtFirstCollect` / `permanentIdentityOf` already implement CR §15-4-4-3. It simply never saw
the watcher, because the watcher was not in the window. Once 9b lands, the latch retires
EX10-009's entry the moment BT24-078 becomes the permanent's top card.

##### Ruling implemented

The coordinator's ruling (CR §15-4-2, and the existing ＜Alliance＞ precedent in `GameEngine`,
`canActivate: () => true`): a triggered effect whose only action currently has no legal board target
**still triggers**, takes its place in the ordered set, and fizzles at resolution if it is still
targetless.

##### Fix

1. `interpreter/effect.ts` — `canActivateEffect` takes a third argument, `ActivationGateOptions`.
   `collectsMandatoryTrigger: true` skips the empty-board refusal; everything else about the gate is
   unchanged.
2. `interpreter/registration/module.ts` — the general trigger build site computes
   `isMandatoryTrigger` and passes it. A clause counts as mandatory only when it is not an
   `OnDeclaration` (`[Main]`) activation, the clause itself is not `optional`, **and no action in
   it is `optional`**. Optionality is printed both ways in the IR — BT21-045's "you may delete 1 of
   your opponent's Digimon with 9000 DP or less" carries `optional` on the `Delete` action, not on
   the clause — and either form makes an empty-board prompt a UI wart rather than a rules
   requirement, so both keep the gate (seams 18 / 27 stay as they are). The ＜Delay＞ build sites are
   untouched.
3. `combat/controller.ts` — new optional hook `withPendingAttackSubTriggers(payload, runWindows)`.
   When the engine supplies it, the `OnUseAttack` / `OnAllyAttack` windows run inside it and the
   trailing prepared-bus fire is skipped; the legacy sequence is kept verbatim for the combat unit
   tests, which supply minimal hooks.
4. `GameEngine.ts` — implements that hook as
   `withPendingSubTriggers(["whenAttacking", "whenOpponentAttacks"], payload, runWindows,
   { onlyInitiallyArmed: true })`. `onlyInitiallyArmed` preserves the event-time watcher snapshot
   the old `prepareSubTrigger` call gave, so a watcher armed *during* the windows still does not
   retroactively react to this declaration.
5. `engine/testkit/harness.ts` — `preferTriggerKeys` now also matches a trigger's source card id
   (`options.triggerCardIds`), not only its opaque key. A SubTrigger watcher's key is
   `subtrigger/<subscription id>/<description>`, which no test can name; the card id is the only
   stable handle. Without this the EX10-009 proof could not choose the BT24-078 branch.
6. `apps/api/src/cards/BT24/BT24-078.ts` — `@ts-nocheck` removed; the file typechecks as written.

##### Red / green

Reds reproduced on the current tree by turning both `it.fails` into `it`:

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-009.test.ts \
  src/cards/EX10/EX10-023.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> Tests  2 failed | 31 passed (33)
     × EX10-023 "Q5074 raises an orderTriggers decision for the two simultaneous
       [When Digivolving] effects"
       settle: predicate never held within 500 ticks.
       Predicate: () => s.decisions.some(({ req }) => req.kind === "orderTriggers")
     × EX10-009 "Q5656 loses its When Attacking window when the trash digivolve is ordered first"
       EX10-009.test.ts:576  expect(s.decisions.some(...kind === "orderTriggers")).toBe(true)
       AssertionError: expected false to be true
```

Green with the fix, both now plain `it`:

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/EX10/EX10-009.test.ts \
  src/cards/EX10/EX10-023.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> Test Files 2 passed (2); Tests 33 passed (33)
```

The EX10-009 proof now shows both halves: one `orderTriggers` offering
`triggerCardIds: ["EX10-009", "BT24-078"]`, and — after the BT24-078 branch is ordered first —
an empty breeding area with BT1-013 still in the trash, because EX10-009's own pending
`[When Attacking]` was retired when it stopped being the top card.

#### Seam 21 `pending-activation-lapses-when-source-leaves`

##### Mechanism

CR §15-4-4-3 / §15-4-4-5 retire a pending triggered effect whose source left its area before the
effect activates. `stack.ts` enforces this *inside the timing window it is draining* (the `departed`
latch, the presence diff, `identityAtFirstCollect`), because it re-collects from live state on every
pass.

A trigger parked **between** windows does not go through that loop. `GameEngine.fireTiming` defers a
window opened inside a resolving effect body (`shouldDeferNestedTiming`), and
`deferNestedTimingEffects` captures the collected list *now* into `pendingNestedTimingEffects`.
That captured list is replayed later by `pendingWindowCollected()` with no fresh residency check.
The sibling `deferredTimingWindows` queue does not have this problem: it stores the *event*, not the
effects, and re-runs `gatherTriggeredEffects` at flush time.

##### Fix

`GameEngine.ts` records `permanentIdentityOf(source)` for each parked entry at defer time
(`nestedTriggerSourceIdentity`, a `WeakMap`) and filters the list at replay through
`nestedTriggerSourceStillResident`: an entry whose source card is no longer the same thing on the
same permanent it was when the trigger was collected is dropped. `permanentIdentityOf` is now
exported from `effects/stack.ts` via `effects/index.ts` so both boundaries use one definition of
"same card, same permanent, same role".

Two deliberate opt-outs:

- A source that was **not on a permanent** at defer time (`null`) makes no residency claim and is
  never filtered — a trash- or hand-resident clause, or an `[On Deletion]` whose card is already
  gone.
- `pendingPlayCostDeletionEffects` (seam 23) is a different field and is not filtered at all. Its
  source is gone by design: it was deleted to pay the play cost (Q5131). EX10-048's proof stays
  green.

##### Coverage, and an honest limitation

New conformance case in `apps/api/src/engine/conformance/ch15-02-timing-and-resolution.test.ts`:
`§15-4-4 Pending Activation (comprehensive-0165)` →
`15-4-4-3: a trigger parked between windows lapses when its source becomes a digivolution card`.
BT16-097's `[Main]` plays BT16-019 Angemon from hand (parking its `[On Play]` "unsuspend 1 of your
Lv.4 or lower Digimon") and then DNA digivolves it into BT16-012 in the same resolution; the
suspended decoy must still be suspended afterwards. Driven entirely through the public `playCard`
intent.

**This test does not isolate the new gate.** With the filter reverted it still passes: the built
effect's `onField` base guard also refuses to activate an effect whose source is now a digivolution
card, so the endpoint is produced twice over. I could not construct a case in the current corpus
where the base guard passes and only the residency gate stops the replay — that needs an inherited
or granted clause whose source moves between permanents while remaining collectable (Q5160's exact
shape, which EX10-058's lane also could not reach through public intents). The gate is therefore
correct-by-construction and strictly one-way, but its own red is outstanding. Recorded rather than
faked.

The filter is not inert in practice: instrumenting it across `src/cards` + `src/engine` shows it
dropping entries in BT16-019 (`perm-1 top -> perm-2 stack`, the case above), BT26-016, EX12-046,
BT24-019, BT24-010 and BT16-031 (all `-> undefined`, the source left the battle area). No test
outcome changes in any of them.

#### Production behaviour changes

1. **Corpus-wide.** A mandatory (no `optional` on the clause and none on any of its actions)
   triggered effect whose every action is board-targeted now enters the
   ordering tier on an empty board, and fizzles at resolution if it is still targetless. Before it
   was simply not collected. Player-visible consequence: such an effect now appears in an
   `orderTriggers` prompt alongside its simultaneous siblings, and can be ordered ahead of an effect
   that would have created its target — which is what CR §15-4-2 requires. Optional ("you may")
   clauses and `[Main]` activations are unchanged.
2. **Every attack declaration.** `whenAttacking` / `whenOpponentAttacks` watchers now resolve inside
   the `OnUseAttack` window rather than after it, so a printed `[When Attacking]` effect and a
   watcher reacting to the same declaration are ordered together by their controller (one prompt)
   instead of running in a fixed engine order. Watchers armed during the windows still fire
   afterwards, unchanged.
3. **Deferred nested triggers.** A trigger parked while another effect resolves is dropped at flush
   time if its source card changed permanent or role in the meantime (§15-4-4-3).

##### Corpus check

The whole card suite was run with all three of this lane's changes on and again with them
neutralised, and the failing-test name sets diffed:

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  baseline (changes neutralised): 455 failing tests
  with the fix:                   453 failing tests
  diff: the only two entries that changed are the two reds this lane flipped;
        no test failed that was not already failing.
```

The first cut of the mandatory test — `effect.optional !== true` alone — did produce one
regression: BT21-045 ShineGreymon prints two `[When Attacking]` clauses, and its "you may delete"
clause carries `optional` on the action. With an opponent holding no Digimon that clause started
triggering, raised an `orderTriggers` and then an extra `optional` prompt, and the test's scripted
single decline no longer matched. That is the seam-18/27 family the ruling deliberately excludes, so
the action-level `optional` check was added rather than the test being changed. No card test needed
editing in the end.

#### Tests updated because of the ruling

None. BT3-014's test — the citation the old gate leaned on — asserts only that exactly one opposing
Lv.4-or-lower Digimon is set to 1000 DP when a legal target exists. It never asserted "no prompt on
an empty board", so the ruling does not contradict it and the file is untouched. `BT3-014.test.ts`
passes unchanged.

The two EX10 reds changed from `it.fails` to `it` and their comment blocks were rewritten to
describe the mechanism that was fixed rather than the defect.

### Seam 15 `unaffectable-still-choosable` — FIXED (flag-gated)

Source: `docs/audits/EX10-reaudit/SEAM-15-MECHANISM.md`.

#### Mechanism

Comprehensive Rules §15-15-5-3: a permanent that "isn't affected by effects" can still be CHOSEN
for an effect; the effect then does nothing to it. The rules' own worked example is BT1-070
Kuwagamon's "[On Play] Suspend 1 of your opponent's Digimon".

The candidate pool was never the problem. `resolvePermanentTargets`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/targeting/permanents.ts`) already calls
`candidatePermanents` with `includeUnaffectable: true` and strips immune ids from the RESULT via
`filterAffectable`, so an immune permanent is offered whenever a prompt happens at all.

The gap was the auto-resolve shortcut. When the pool is no larger than the wanted count and the
target is not `upTo`/`forceSelection`, the function resolves silently without asking. With the
opponent's only Digimon immune, the pool is `[immune]`, `want` is 1, so no `chooseTargets`
decision was ever raised — the player was never offered the choice §15-15-5-3 grants them. The
end state (nothing suspended) was already correct; only the decision was missing.

#### Fix

1. New IR field `Target.allowUnaffectableChoice`
   (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/filters/filter.ts`, beside the existing `forceSelection`).
2. `resolvePermanentTargets` skips the auto-resolve shortcut when the flag is set AND the pool
   holds a permanent this source cannot affect (`holdsUnaffectableCandidate`, computed with the
   same `filterAffectable` used to strip the result). Everything else is unchanged: the prompt
   still offers the full pool and the result is still filtered, so the observable outcome of a
   choice never changes — only whether a choice is presented.
3. Flag set on BT1-070's Suspend target, in the card module and in its `effects.json` record.

Plain mandatory targeting without the flag keeps the quieter auto-resolve, as the ENGINE-LANE-1
design proposal asked.

#### Red → green

```

  src/engine/conformance src/engine/effects --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
```

EX10-021 `KB Q5067` before the fix (with `it.fails` flipped to `it`):

```
AssertionError: expected [] to include 'inst-1'
 Tests  1 failed | 14 skipped (15)
```

New conformance case
(`src/engine/conformance/ch15-03-targeting-and-selection.test.ts` §15-15-5, "offers the choice
even when the immune permanent is the ONLY candidate") with the flag removed from BT1-070:

```

 Tests  1 failed | 10 skipped (11)
```

After:

```
